## The Infidel Half Century / 不信神的半个世纪
===Original===



===Chinese===

不信神的半个世纪

### The Dawn of Darwinism / 达尔文主义的黎明

### The Dawn of Darwinism /

### The Dawn of Darwinism /

### The Dawn of Darwinism /

### The Dawn of Darwinism /

### The Dawn of Darwinism /

### The Dawn of Darwinism /

### The Dawn of Darwinism /

### The Dawn of Darwinism / 
===Original===

One day early in the eighteen hundred and sixties, I, being then a small boy, was with my nurse, buying something in the shop of a petty newsagent, bookseller, and stationer in Camden Street, Dublin, when there entered an elderly man, weighty and solemn, who advanced to the counter, and said pompously, “Have you the works of the celebrated Buffoon?”

My own works were at that time unwritten, or it is possible that the shop assistant might have misunderstood me so far as to produce a copy of *Man and Superman*. As it was, she knew quite well what he wanted; for this was before the Education Act of 1870 had produced shop assistants who know how to read and know nothing else. The celebrated Buffoon was not a humorist, but the famous naturalist Buffoon. Every literate child at that time knew Buffoon’s Natural History as well as Aesop’s Fables. And no living child had heard the name that has since obliterated Buffoon’s in the popular consciousness: the name of Darwin.

Ten years elapsed. The celebrated Buffoon was forgotten; I had doubled my years and my length; and I had discarded the religion of my forefathers. One day the richest and consequently most dogmatic of my uncles came into a restaurant where I was dining, and found himself, much against his will, in conversation with the most questionable of his nephews. By way of making myself agreeable, I spoke of modern thought and Darwin. He said, “Oh, that’s the fellow who wants to make out that we all have tails like monkeys.” I tried to explain that what Darwin had insisted on in this connection was that some monkeys have no tails. But my uncle was as impervious to what Darwin really said as any Neo-Darwinian nowadays. He died impenitent, and did not mention me in his will.

===Chinese===

十九世纪六十年代初的一天，我还是个小男孩，跟着保姆在都柏林卡姆登街一家小书报文具店里买东西，进来一位上了年纪的先生，气度沉沉，一板一眼，踱到柜台前，拿腔拿调地问：“有那位鼎鼎大名的布丰（Buffoon）的全集吗？”
那时我自己的著作还没有写出来；不然的话，那位店员小姐说不定会把我的意思误会到拿出一册《人与超人》来的地步。事实上，她完全清楚他要的是什么；因为这是1870年教育法案制造出那种识字而别无所知的店员之前的事。鼎鼎大名的布丰不是什么滑稽家，而是那位著名的博物学家布丰。当时凡识字的孩子都同熟悉《伊索寓言》一样熟悉布丰的《博物学》。而没有一个活着的孩子听见过那个日后在大众意识里把布丰的名字抹掉的名字：达尔文。
十年过去了。鼎鼎大名的布丰被忘掉了；我的年纪翻了一倍，个头也翻了一倍；我把祖宗的宗教也丢掉了。有一天，我那几位叔叔里最有钱、因而也最武断的那位，走进一家我正在吃饭的餐馆，很不情愿地发现自己在同他最不成器的侄子说话。为了讨他喜欢，我谈起现代思想和达尔文。他说：“哦，就是那个家伙，想说我们都长着猴子似的尾巴。”我竭力解释，达尔文在这个问题上坚持的其实是：有些猴子没有尾巴。可我这位叔叔对达尔文真正说了什么，同今天任何一位新达尔文主义者一样充耳不闻。他至死不悟，遗嘱里也没提到我。

===Original===

Twenty years elapsed. If my uncle had been alive, he would have known all about Darwin, and known it all wrong. In spite of the efforts of Grant Allen to set him right, he would have accepted Darwin as the discoverer of Evolution, of Heredity, and of modification of species by Selection. For the pre-Darwinian age had come to be regarded as a Dark Age in which men still believed that the book of Genesis was a standard scientific treatise, and that the only additions to it were Galileo’s demonstration of Leonardo da Vinci’s simple remark that the earth is a moon of the sun, Newton’s theory of gravitation, Sir Humphry Davy’s invention of the safety-lamp, the discovery of electricity, the application of steam to industrial purposes, and the penny post. It was just the same in other subjects. Thus Nietzsche, by the two or three who had come across his writings, was supposed to have been the first man to whom it occurred that mere morality and legality and urbanity lead nowhere, as if Bunyan had never written *Badman*. Schopenhauer was credited with inventing the distinction between the Covenant of Grace and the Covenant of Works which troubled Cromwell on his deathbed. People talked as if there had been no dramatic or descriptive music before Wagner; no impressionist painting before Whistler; whilst as to myself, I was finding that the surest way to produce an effect of daring innovation and originality was to revive the ancient attraction of long rhetorical speeches; to stick closely to the methods of Molière; and to lift characters bodily out of the pages of Charles Dickens.

===Chinese===

又过了二十年。倘若我那位叔叔还活着，他会把达尔文知道个底朝天，而且知道得全不对。尽管有格兰特·艾伦的努力想纠正他，他仍旧会把达尔文当作进化、遗传和物种经由选择而变异这三大发现的发现者。因为达尔文之前的时代，已经被当作一个黑暗时代：人们那时还相信《创世记》是一部标准的科学论著，此后仅有的增补，不过是伽利略对列奥纳多·达·芬奇一句简单话——地球是太阳的一颗月亮——的证明、牛顿的万有引力学说、汉弗莱·戴维爵士发明安全灯、电的发现、蒸汽之应用于工业，还有便士邮政。别的科目也一样。譬如尼采，在那两三个碰巧读过他著作的人看来，竟被当成头一个想到“单靠道德、合法和客气走不到哪儿去”的人，仿佛班扬从来没写过《坏人》似的。叔本华被记在名下发明了恩典之约与行为之约的区分——那可是让克伦威尔临终不得安生的难题。人们谈起来，好像瓦格纳之前没有戏剧性和描绘性的音乐，惠斯勒之前没有印象派绘画；至于我自己，我则发现，要制造大胆创新、独出心裁的效果，最稳当的法子，莫过于复兴冗长的雄辩演说的古老魅力；紧紧遵循莫里哀的手法；再把人物整个儿从查尔斯·狄更斯的书页里搬出来。

### The Advent of the Neo-Darwinians / 新达尔文主义者的登场

### The Advent of the Neo-Darwinians /

### The Advent of the Neo-Darwinians /

### The Advent of the Neo-Darwinians /

### The Advent of the Neo-Darwinians /

### The Advent of the Neo-Darwinians /

### The Advent of the Neo-Darwinians /

### The Advent of the Neo-Darwinians /

### The Advent of the Neo-Darwinians / 
===Original===

This particular sort of ignorance does not always or often matter. But in Darwin’s case it did matter. If Darwin had really led the world at one bound from the book of Genesis to Heredity, to Modification of Species by Selection, and to Evolution, he would have been a philosopher and a prophet as well as an eminent professional naturalist, with geology as a hobby. The delusion that he had actually achieved this feat did no harm at first, because if people’s views are sound, about evolution or anything else, it does not make two straws difference whether they call the revealer of their views Tom or Dick. But later on such apparently negligible errors have awkward consequences. Darwin was given an imposing reputation as not only an Evolutionist, but as *the* Evolutionist, with the immense majority who never read his books. The few who never read any others were led by them to concentrate exclusively on Circumstantial Selection as the explanation of all the transformations and adaptations which were the evidence for Evolution. And they presently found themselves so cut off by this specialization from the majority who knew Darwin only by his spurious reputation, that they were obliged to distinguish themselves, not as Darwinians, but as Neo-Darwinians.

Before ten more years had elapsed, the Neo-Darwinians were practically running current Science. It was 1906; I was fifty; I published my own view of evolution in a play called *Man and Superman*; and I found that most people were unable to understand how I could be an Evolutionist and not a Neo-Darwinian, or why I habitually derided Neo-Darwinism as a ghastly idiocy, and would fall on its professors slaughterously in public discussions. It was in the hope of making me clear the matter up that the Fabian Society, which was then organizing a series of lectures on Prophets of the Nineteenth Century, asked me to deliver a lecture on the prophet Darwin. I did so; and scraps of that lecture, which was never published, variegate these pages.

===Chinese===

这一路的无知并不总有关系，也不常有关系。可在达尔文这件事上，它有关系。倘若达尔文当真一跃而把世界从《创世记》带到遗传、带到物种经由选择而变异、带到进化，那他就不仅是位杰出的职业博物学家、业余玩玩地质学，还是一位哲人兼先知。说他当真成就了这桩伟业的错觉，起初并无害处；因为人们的见解只要站得住，不管是进化还是别的，管这见解的揭示者叫汤姆还是迪克，不差两根稻草的轻重。可后来，这类看似无足轻重的错误就出了别扭的后果。在从来不读他书的广大人群里，达尔文得了一个了不得的名声：不仅是一位进化论者，而且是*那位*进化论者。那几个除了他的书什么也不读的人，则被这些书引得把“环境选择”当作一切变化与适应——也就是进化的全部证据——的唯一解释，一门心思钻了进去。他们不久便发现，这样的专门化把他们同那大多数只知道达尔文的虚假名声的人隔断了，只好自称不是达尔文主义者，而是新达尔文主义者。
不出十年，新达尔文主义者实际上主持了时下的科学。那是1906年；我五十岁；我在一出名叫《人与超人》的戏里发表了我自己对进化的看法；我发现大多数人不明白：我怎么既是进化论者、又不是新达尔文主义者；不明白我为什么惯于讥诮新达尔文主义是一场可怕的愚蠢，在公开讨论中动辄对它的教授们大开杀戒。费边社当时正组织一个“十九世纪的先知们”系列讲座，大约就是想叫我把这件事讲讲清楚，请我主讲先知达尔文。我讲了；那次从未刊行的讲演的零章断句，点缀在这几页文字里。

===Original===

Ten more years elapsed. Neo-Darwinism in politics had produced a European catastrophe of a magnitude so appalling, and a scope so unpredictable, that as I write these lines in 1920, it is still far from certain whether our civilization will survive it. The circumstances of this catastrophe, the boyish cinema-fed romanticism which made it possible to impose it on the people as a crusade, and especially the ignorance and errors of the victors of Western Europe when its violent phase had passed and the time for reconstruction arrived, confirmed a doubt which had grown steadily in my mind during my forty years public work as a Socialist: namely, whether the human animal, as he exists at present, is capable of solving the social problems raised by his own aggregation, or, as he calls it, his civilization.

===Chinese===

又是十年。新达尔文主义在政治上结出的，是一场规模骇人、范围莫测的欧洲大灾难；直到1920年我写下这几行字的时候，我们的文明能否熬过它，仍远无定论。这场灾难的种种情状——那种使它得以作为一场圣战强加给人民的、喂电影长大、孩子气的浪漫主义，尤其是暴力阶段过去、重建的时辰到来之后西欧胜利者的无知与过失——坐实了一个疑心；这疑心在我作为社会主义者从事公众工作的四十年里不断增长着：人类这种动物，照他现在的样子，究竟有没有能力解决他自己的聚集——他所谓的文明——所提出的社会问题。

### Cowardice of the Irreligious / 
===Original===

Another observation I had made was that goodnatured unambitious men are cowards when they have no religion. They are dominated and exploited not only by greedy and often half-witted and half-alive weaklings who will do anything for cigars, champagne, motor cars, and the more childish and selfish uses of money, but by able and sound administrators who can do nothing else with them than dominate and exploit them. Government and exploitation become synonymous under such circumstances; and the world is finally ruled by the childish, the brigands, and the blackguards. Those who refuse to stand in with them are persecuted and occasionally executed when they give any trouble to the exploiters. They fall into poverty when they lack lucrative specific talents. At the present moment one half of Europe, having knocked the other half down, is trying to kick it to death, and may succeed: a procedure which is, logically, sound Neo-Darwinism. And the goodnatured majority are looking on in helpless horror, or allowing themselves to be persuaded by the newspapers of their exploiters that the kicking is not only a sound commercial investment, but an act of divine justice of which they are the ardent instruments.

===Chinese===

我还有一条观察：性情和善、没有野心的人，一旦没了宗教，便成了懦夫。他们不但被那些贪得无厌、往往半呆半死的软骨头支配和盘剥——这班人为了雪茄、香槟、汽车和金钱更孩子气、更自私的用途什么都肯干——而且也被那些干练正当的管理者支配和盘剥，因为除了支配和盘削，后者拿他们也没有别的办法。在这种情势下，政府和剥削成了同义语；世界终于由孩童、土匪和恶棍统治。不肯同他们同流的人，一旦给剥削者添了麻烦，便遭迫害，偶尔还被处决。他们缺少挣钱的专门本事，便陷于贫穷。眼下，半个欧洲把另外半个打翻在地，正要把它踢死，而且可能得手：这套程序，逻辑上，是地道的新达尔文主义。而和善的大多数在一旁束手地看着，满脸恐怖，或者听凭剥削者的报纸说服，相信这番踢打不但是一桩稳妥的商业投资，而且是一桩神圣的正义之举，而自己正是那踊跃的工具。

===Original===

But if Man is really incapable of organizing a big civilization, and cannot organize even a village or a tribe any too well, what is the use of giving him a religion? A religion may make him hunger and thirst for righteousness; but will it endow him with the practical capacity to satisfy that appetite? Good intentions do not carry with them a grain of political science, which is a very complicated one. The most devoted and indefatigable, the most able and disinterested students of this science in England, as far as I know, are my friends Sidney and Beatrice Webb. It has taken them forty years of preliminary work, in the course of which they have published several treatises comparable to Adam Smith’s *Wealth of Nations*, to formulate a political constitution adequate to existing needs. If this is the measure of what can be done in a lifetime by extraordinary ability, keen natural aptitude, exceptional opportunities, and freedom from the preoccupations of bread-winning, what are we to expect from the parliament man to whom political science is as remote and distasteful as the differential calculus, and to whom such an elementary but vital point as the law of economic rent is a *pons asinorum* never to be approached, much less crossed? Or from the common voter who is mostly so hard at work all day earning a living that he cannot keep awake for five minutes over a book?

===Chinese===

可是，倘若人当真组织不起一个大文明，连一个村子一个部落也组织不好，给他宗教又有什么用？宗教可以使他饥渴慕义；可它能赋予他满足这胃口实际的本领吗？好意里不含一粒政治学——那是一门顶复杂的学问。据我所知，在英国，这门科学最献身最不知疲倦、最能干最无私的学生，就是我的朋友西德尼·韦伯和比阿特丽斯·韦伯。他们做了四十年的准备工作，其间出版了几部堪与亚当·斯密的《国富论》相比的论著，才拟出一份适合当前需要的政治宪章。倘若这就是非凡的才具、天生的敏锐、特殊的机会、外加不必为糊口操劳，在一个人一生里所能办到的极限，那么我们又能指望那班议员什么呢——政治学之于他们，同微积分一样遥远而倒胃口，像经济地租定律这样一个初等却性命攸关的要点，对他们是一座永远不会走近、更不用说跨过去的“愚人桥”？又能指望那普通选民什么呢——他们大多成天为生计苦干，捧起书来五分钟都熬不住？

### Is There Any Hope in Education / 教育可有希望

### Is There Any Hope in Education /

### Is There Any Hope in Education /

### Is There Any Hope in Education /

### Is There Any Hope in Education /

### Is There Any Hope in Education /

### Is There Any Hope in Education /

### Is There Any Hope in Education /

### Is There Any Hope in Education / 
===Original===

The usual answer is that we must educate our masters: that is, ourselves. We must teach citizenship and political science at school. But must we? There is no must about it, the hard fact being that we must *not* teach political science or citizenship at school. The schoolmaster who attempted it would soon find himself penniless in the streets without pupils, if not in the dock pleading to a pompously worded indictment for sedition against the exploiters. Our schools teach the morality of feudalism corrupted by commercialism, and hold up the military conqueror, the robber baron, and the profiteer, as models of the illustrious and the successful. In vain do the prophets who see through this imposture preach and teach a better gospel: the individuals whom they convert are doomed to pass away in a few years; and the new generations are dragged back in the schools to the morality of the fifteenth century, and think themselves Liberal when they are defending the ideas of Henry VII, and gentlemanly when they are opposing to them the ideas of Richard III. Thus the educated man is a greater nuisance than the uneducated one: indeed it is the inefficiency and sham of the educational side of our schools (to which, except under compulsion, children would not be sent by their parents at all if they did not act as prisons in which the immature are kept from worrying the mature) that save us from being dashed on the rocks of false doctrine instead of drifting down the midstream of mere ignorance. There is no way out through the schoolmaster.

===Chinese===

惯常的回答是：我们必须教育我们的主人，也就是教育我们自己。必须在学校里教公民课和政治学。可是非教不可吗？没有这个“必须”；硬邦邦的事实是，我们*切不可*在学校里教政治学和公民课。谁敢这么教，那位教师很快就会落到分文不名、流落街头、没了学生的地步——弄不好还要站上被告席，听人拿一份措辞冠冕堂皇的起诉书，控他煽动叛乱、开罪剥削者。我们的学校教的是被商业主义腐蚀了的封建主义道德，把军事征服者、强盗男爵和发战争财的人捧为显赫与成功的楷模。看穿这套把戏的先知们宣讲更好的福音，也是枉然：他们感化的个人，命里注定不出几年便凋零了；新的一代在学校里被拖回十五世纪的道德，自己捍卫着亨利七世的观念，还自以为是自由派；拿理查三世的观念去对抗那些观念，还自以为绅士。这样，受过教育的人反倒比没受过教育的更是个祸害：说实在的，幸亏我们学校教育的一面那样无效、那样虚假（若不是它们兼作监狱、把未成年者关起来免得搅扰成年者，家长要不是被迫，根本不会把孩子送去），我们才免于撞上邪说的礁石，只在单纯无知的中流漂着。学校教师这条路走不通。

### Homeopathic Education / 顺势疗法式的教育

### Homeopathic Education /

### Homeopathic Education /

===Original===

In truth, mankind cannot be saved from without, by schoolmasters or any other sort of masters: it can only be lamed and enslaved by them. It is said that if you wash a cat it will never again wash itself. This may or may not be true: what is certain is that if you teach a man anything he will never learn it; and if you cure him of a disease he will be unable to cure himself the next time it attacks him. Therefore, if you want to see a cat clean, you throw a bucket of mud over it, when it will immediately take extraordinary pains to lick the mud off, and finally be cleaner than it was before. In the same way doctors who are up-to-date (say .00005 percent of all the registered practitioners, and 20 percent of the unregistered ones), when they want to rid you of a disease or a symptom, inoculate you with that disease or give you a drug that produces that symptom, in order to provoke you to resist it as the mud provokes the cat to wash itself.

===Chinese===

其实，人类不可能靠外力——靠教师或任何别的主子——得救：外力只能把人搞残、奴役。据说猫要是让人洗过一回澡，就再也不自己洗了。这话真真假假；千真万确的是，一件事你要是教了人，他就永远学不会；一种病你要是替他治好了，下次它再来，他就没本事自己治了。所以，你若想看一只干净的猫，就朝它泼一桶泥，它会立刻使出浑身解数把泥舔掉，末了比先前还干净。同样，跟上时代的医生们（姑且说占注册行医者的十万分之五，占不注册者的百分之二十）要替你除掉一种病或一个症状，就给你接种这种病，或者给你一味产生这个症状的药，为的是激起你去抵抗它，正像泥激起猫自己洗澡。

===Original===

Now an acute person will ask me why, if this be so, our false education does not provoke our scholars to find out the truth. My answer is that it sometimes does. Voltaire was a pupil of the Jesuits; Samuel Butler was the pupil of a hopelessly conventional and erroneous country parson. But then Voltaire was Voltaire, and Butler was Butler: that is, their minds were so abnormally strong that they could throw off the doses of poison that paralyse ordinary minds. When the doctors inoculate you and the homeopathists dose you, they give you an infinitesimally attenuated dose. If they gave you the virus at full strength it would overcome your resistance and produce its direct effect. The doses of false doctrine given at public schools and universities are so big that they overwhelm the resistance that a tiny dose would provoke. The normal student is corrupted beyond redemption, and will drive the genius who resists out of the country if he can. Byron and Shelley had to fly to Italy, whilst Castlereagh and Eldon ruled the roost at home. Rousseau was hunted from frontier to frontier; Karl Marx starved in exile in a Soho lodging; Ruskin’s articles were refused by the magazines (he was too rich to be otherwise persecuted); whilst mindless forgotten nonentities governed the land; sent men to the prison or the gallows for blasphemy and sedition (meaning the truth about Church and State); and sedulously stored up the social disease and corruption which explode from time to time in gigantic boils that have to be lanced by a million bayonets. This is the result of allopathic education. Homeopathic education has not yet been officially tried, and would obviously be a delicate matter if it were. A body of schoolmasters inciting their pupils to infinitesimal peccadilloes with the object of provoking them to exclaim, “Get thee behind me, Satan,” or telling them white lies about history for the sake of being contradicted, insulted, and refuted, would certainly do less harm than our present educational allopaths do; but then nobody will advocate homeopathic education. Allopathy has produced the poisonous illusion that it enlightens instead of darkening.

===Chinese===

于是机灵人要问我：倘若如此，我们的虚假教育怎么没有激起学子们去找出真理？我的回答是：有时候激起了。伏尔泰是耶稣会士的学生；塞缪尔·巴特勒是一位绝望地守旧又守错的乡下教区牧师的学生。可伏尔泰到底是伏尔泰，巴特勒到底是巴特勒：就是说，他们的头脑强得反常，能甩掉那些麻痹寻常头脑的毒剂。医生给你接种、顺势疗法者给你下药，给的都是稀释到极小的剂量。倘若把全毒给你，它会压倒你的抵抗力，直接发作。公立学校和大学里下的邪说剂量大得很，把小剂量所能激起的抵抗力整个淹没了。寻常的学生腐化得不可救药，还要是有本事，就把那抵抗的天才赶出国门。拜伦和雪莱只好逃往意大利，让卡斯尔雷和埃尔登在国内称王称霸；卢梭被从一道国境追到另一道国境；卡尔·马克思流亡在索霍的寓所里挨饿；罗斯金的文章被杂志退掉（他太有钱，没法再往狠里迫害）；与此同时，一群没头脑的、被遗忘的无名之辈治理着国土，把人送上监狱和绞架，罪名是渎神和煽动（也就是关于教会与国家的真相）；还勤勤恳恳地积攒着社会的疾病与腐败，让它们隔三差五爆成巨大的疖子，得用一百万把刺刀来切开。这就是对抗疗法式教育的成果。顺势疗法式的教育还没被正式试过；真要试，显然也是件精细事。一伙教师引诱学生犯无穷小的过失，好激得他们喊“撒但，退我后边去”，或者为了挨反驳、受抢白、被驳倒而对学生讲些善意的史谎——这当然比我们眼下的教育对抗疗法者闯的祸小；可是没有谁会主张顺势疗法式的教育。对抗疗法制造了一个有毒的错觉：它以为自己在启蒙，其实在蒙昧。

===Original===

The suggestion may, however, explain why, whilst most people’s minds succumb to inculcation and environment, a few react vigorously: honest and decent people coming from thievish slums, and sceptics and realists from country parsonages.

===Chinese===

不过，这个说法或许可以解释：为什么大多数人的头脑向灌输和环境屈服，少数人却起而猛反——诚正的人出自窃贼横行的贫民窟，怀疑论者和现实主义者出自乡下牧师住宅。

### The Diabolical Efficiency of Technical Education / 技术教育那魔鬼般的效率

### The Diabolical Efficiency of Technical Education /

### The Diabolical Efficiency of Technical Education /

### The Diabolical Efficiency of Technical Education /

### The Diabolical Efficiency of Technical Education /

### The Diabolical Efficiency of Technical Education /

### The Diabolical Efficiency of Technical Education /

### The Diabolical Efficiency of Technical Education / 
===Original===

But meanwhile﻿—and here comes the horror of it﻿—our technical instruction is honest and efficient. The public schoolboy who is carefully blinded, duped, and corrupted as to the nature of a society based on profiteering, and is taught to honor parasitic idleness and luxury, learns to shoot and ride and keep fit with all the assistance and guidance that can be procured for him by the most anxiously sincere desire that he may do these things well, and if possible superlatively well. In the army he learns to fly; to drop bombs; to use machine-guns to the utmost of his capacity. The discovery of high explosives is rewarded and dignified: instruction in the manufacture of the weapons, battleships, submarines, and land batteries by which they are applied destructively, is quite genuine: the instructors know their business, and really mean the learners to succeed. The result is that powers of destruction that could hardly without uneasiness be entrusted to infinite wisdom and infinite benevolence are placed in the hands of romantic schoolboy patriots who, however generous by nature, are by education ignoramuses, dupes, snobs, and sportsmen to whom fighting is a religion and killing an accomplishment; whilst political power, useless under such circumstances except to militarist imperialists in chronic terror of invasion and subjugation, pompous tufthunting fools, commercial adventurers to whom the organization by the nation of its own industrial services would mean checkmate, financial parasites on the money market, and stupid people who cling to the status quo merely because they are used to it, is obtained by heredity, by simple purchase, by keeping newspapers and pretending that they are organs of public opinion, by the wiles of seductive women, and by prostituting ambitious talent to the service of the profiteers, who call the tune because, having secured all the spare plunder, they alone can afford to pay the piper. Neither the rulers nor the ruled understand high politics. They do not even know that there is such a branch of knowledge as political science; but between them they can coerce and enslave with the deadliest efficiency, even to the wiping out of civilization, because their education as slayers has been honestly and thoroughly carried out.

===Chinese===

可是与此同时——可怕之处就在这里——我们的技术教育却是诚实而高效的。公立学校的学生，关于这个建立在发战争财之上的社会的性质，被细心地蒙蔽、哄骗、腐蚀，被教导去敬仰寄生的闲散和奢侈，可他学射击、学骑马、学健身，却有人怀着最诚挚的盼他学好的心愿，为他置办得来的一切辅助和指导，而且但愿他不但好、还要顶好地学会。进了军队，他学开飞机，学投弹，学把机枪使出他全部的本事。烈性炸药的发现受到奖励和尊崇：教人制造应用这些炸药的武器——战列舰、潜艇、陆上炮台——的功课，是货真价实的：教官懂行，真心要学员学成。结果，那种连无限的智慧和无限的仁慈掌管起来都要捏一把汗的毁灭力量，落到了一批浪漫的、学生腔的爱国者手里——他们天性再慷慨，受了这样的教育，也是无知者、受骗者、势利鬼，把打架当宗教、把杀人是本事；与此同时，政治权力——在这样的情势下，只对下列人等有用：慢性地害怕入侵和征服的军国帝国主义分子，妄自尊大的攀附权贵的蠢货，视国家自办工业为死棋的商业冒险家，金融市场上的寄生者，还有仅仅因为习惯了而死抱现状不放的蠢人——则通过世袭、通过赤裸裸的收买、通过豢养报纸而冒充舆论喉舌、通过妖娆女人的手腕、通过把野心勃勃的人才卖身给发战争财者（他们点曲子，因为独吞了全部浮财，只有他们出得起乐师的钱）而获得。统治者和被统治者都不懂高等政治。他们甚至不知道世上还有政治学这样一门学问；可是他们合起伙来，能够以致命的效率强制和奴役，直至抹掉整个文明，因为他们作为杀手的功课，教得诚实，学得彻底。

===Original===

Essentially the rulers are all defectives; and there is nothing worse than government by defectives who wield irresistible powers of physical coercion. The commonplace sound people submit, and compel the rest to submit, because they have been taught to do so as an article of religion and a point of honor. Those in whom natural enlightenment has reacted against artificial education submit because they are compelled; but they would resist, and finally resist effectively, if they were not cowards. And they are cowards because they have neither an officially accredited and established religion nor a generally recognized point of honor, and are all at sixes and sevens with their various private speculations, sending their children perforce to the schools where they will be corrupted for want of any other schools. The rulers are equally intimidated by the immense extension and cheapening of the means of slaughter and destruction. The British Government is more afraid of Ireland now that submarines, bombs, and poison gas are cheap and easily made than it was of the German Empire before the war; consequently the old British custom which maintained a balance of power through command of the sea is intensified into a terror that sees security in nothing short of absolute military mastery of the entire globe: that is, in an impossibility that will yet seem possible in detail to soldiers and to parochial and insular patriotic civilians.

===Chinese===

本质上，统治者全是些有缺陷的人；再没有比一群挥着无可抗拒的物理强制力量的有缺陷者实行统治更糟的了。平庸而健全的人们屈服了，还强迫其余的人屈服，因为他们受的教育把屈服当作信条和名誉攸关。那些天然的开明反抗了人为教育的人屈服，是因为被迫；可他们倘若不是懦夫，是会抵抗的，而且终将有效地抵抗。他们之所以是懦夫，是因为他们既没有一种官方认可、业已确立的宗教，又没有一个公认的名誉准则，各自揣着私人的玄想，乱作一团，只好仍旧把孩子送进那些学校——因为没有别的学校，孩子在那里受腐蚀。统治者同样被杀戮和毁灭手段的极大扩展与廉价吓破了胆。如今潜艇、炸弹和毒气又便宜又好造，英国政府比战前面对德意志帝国时更怕爱尔兰；于是，那条靠制海权维持均势的老英国惯例，恶性膨胀成一种恐怖，认定除非取得全球范围的绝对军事主宰便无安全可言：也就是说，认定一桩不可能的事——而这桩不可能的事，在军人和眼界狭隘、岛民心态的爱国平民看来，却又分明一步步地可能。

### Flimsiness of Civilization / 文明之薄脆

### Flimsiness of Civilization /

### Flimsiness of Civilization /

### Flimsiness of Civilization /

### Flimsiness of Civilization /

### Flimsiness of Civilization /

### Flimsiness of Civilization /

### Flimsiness of Civilization / 
===Original===

This situation has occurred so often before, always with the same result of a collapse of civilization (Professor Flinders Petrie has let out the secret of previous collapses), that the rich are instinctively crying “Let us eat and drink; for tomorrow we die,” and the poor, “How long, O Lord, how long?” But the pitiless reply still is that God helps those who help themselves. This does not mean that if Man cannot find the remedy no remedy will be found. The power that produced Man when the monkey was not up to the mark, can produce a higher creature than Man if Man does not come up to the mark. What it means is that if Man is to be saved, Man must save himself. There seems no compelling reason why he should be saved. He is by no means an ideal creature. At his present best many of his ways are so unpleasant that they are unmentionable in polite society, and so painful that he is compelled to pretend that pain is often a good. Nature holds no brief for the human experiment: it must stand or fall by its results. If Man will not serve, Nature will try another experiment.

What hope is there then of human improvement? According to the Neo-Darwinists, to the Mechanists, no hope whatever, because improvement can come only through some senseless accident which must, on the statistical average of accidents, be presently wiped out by some other equally senseless accident.

===Chinese===

这样的局面从前也出现过，回回都是文明崩溃收场（弗林德斯·皮特里教授早把前几次崩溃的底细漏了出来）；于是富人们本能地喊：“我们吃喝快乐吧，因为明天要死了”；穷人们喊：“还要多久，主啊，还要多久？”可那无情的回答依旧是：自助者天助。这并不是说，人若找不着药方，药方就永远找不着了。猴子不成器的时候造出人的那股力量，人若不成器，也照样能造出比人更高的造物。这句话的意思是：人若要得救，必须自救。似乎并没有什么非救他不可的道理。他远不是什么理想的造物。就在他眼下的最佳状态，他的许多行径已如此不雅，体面社会提都提不得；又如此痛苦，他只好硬说痛苦往往是好事。大自然并不替人类这场实验辩护：它成也罢败也罢，全看结果。人若不肯效力，大自然自会另试一场实验。
那么人类进步还有什么指望？照新达尔文主义者、照机械论者说来，毫无指望，因为进步只能来自某种无意义的偶然，而按偶然的统计平均，它迟早要被另一次同样无意义的偶然一笔勾销。

### Creative Evolution / 创造进化

### Creative Evolution /

### Creative Evolution /

### Creative Evolution /

### Creative Evolution /

### Creative Evolution /

### Creative Evolution /

### Creative Evolution / 
===Original===

But this dismal creed does not discourage those who believe that the impulse that produces evolution is creative. They have observed the simple fact that the will to do anything can and does, at a certain pitch of intensity set up by conviction of its necessity, create and organize new tissue to do it with. To them therefore mankind is by no means played out yet. If the weight lifter, under the trivial stimulus of an athletic competition, can “put up a muscle,” it seems reasonable to believe that an equally earnest and convinced philosopher could “put up a brain.” Both are directions of vitality to a certain end. Evolution shows us this direction of vitality doing all sorts of things: providing the centipede with a hundred legs, and ridding the fish of any legs at all; building lungs and arms for the land and gills and fins for the sea; enabling the mammal to gestate its young inside its body, and the fowl to incubate hers outside it; offering us, we may say, our choice of any sort of bodily contrivance to maintain our activity and increase our resources.

===Chinese===

可是这门阴郁的教义，吓不倒那些相信推动进化的冲动是创造性的人。他们观察到一件简单的事实：做一件事的意志，在确信其必要而激发的一定强度上，能够、而且确实会创造出新的组织来做这件事。因此在他们看来，人类绝没有到山穷水尽的地步。倘若举重运动员凭着运动比赛这一点微不足道的刺激便能“练起一块肌肉”，那么一位同样认真、同样确信的哲学家能“练起一副头脑”，似乎也就合乎情理。两者都是把生命力导向某个目标。进化给我们看的，正是这股生命力的导向干出来的种种事：给蜈蚣置办一百条腿，又把鱼的腿一条不留地收走；为陆地造肺和臂，为海洋造鳃和鳍；叫哺乳动物把幼崽怀在体内，叫飞禽把蛋孵在体外；可以说，任凭我们在种种身体装置里挑选，以维持我们的活动、扩充我们的资源。

### Voluntary Longevity / 自愿的长寿

### Voluntary Longevity /

### Voluntary Longevity /

### Voluntary Longevity /

### Voluntary Longevity /

### Voluntary Longevity /

### Voluntary Longevity /

### Voluntary Longevity / 
===Original===

Among other matters apparently changeable at will is the duration of individual life. Weismann, a very clever and suggestive biologist who was unhappily reduced to idiocy by Neo-Darwinism, pointed out that death is not an eternal condition of life, but an expedient introduced to provide for continual renewal without overcrowding. Now Circumstantial Selection does not account for natural death: it accounts only for the survival of species in which the individuals have sense enough to decay and die on purpose. But the individuals do not seem to have calculated very reasonably: nobody can explain why a parrot should live ten times as long as a dog, and a turtle be almost immortal. In the case of man, the operation has overshot its mark: men do not live long enough: they are, for all the purposes of high civilization, mere children when they die; and our Prime Ministers, though rated as mature, divide their time between the golf course and the Treasury Bench in parliament. Presumably, however, the same power that made this mistake can remedy it. If on opportunist grounds Man now fixes the term of his life at three score and ten years, he can equally fix it at three hundred, or three thousand, or even at the genuine Circumstantial Selection limit, which would be until a sooner-or-later-inevitable fatal accident makes an end of the individual. All that is necessary to make him extend his present span is that tremendous catastrophes such as the late war shall convince him of the necessity of at least outliving his taste for golf and cigars if the race is to be saved. This is not fantastic speculation: it is deductive biology, if there is such a science as biology. Here, then, is a stone that we have left unturned, and that may be worth turning. To make the suggestion more entertaining than it would be to most people in the form of a biological treatise, I have written *Back to Methuselah* as a contribution to the modern Bible.

===Chinese===

在显然可以随意更改的事项中，有一项是个人寿命的长短。魏斯曼——一位非常聪明、富于启发的生物学家，不幸被新达尔文主义弄成了痴人——指出，死并不是生命的永恒条件，而是为不至拥挤、又能不断更新而引入的一种权宜。环境选择解释不了自然死亡：它只解释得了一种物种的存活，条件是其中的个体懂事，懂得故意衰败、故意死掉。可这些个体的算盘似乎打得并不高明：谁也解释不了，为什么鹦鹉该活狗的十倍长，乌龟几乎不死。至于人，这一手做得过了头：人活得不够长：死的时候，就高等文明的一切目的而言，还不过是孩子；我们的首相们，虽被当作成年人，光阴却平分在高尔夫球场和议会的前座上。不过想来，把这件事办错了的那股力量，也能把它纠正过来。人如今出于权宜，把寿数定在七十岁；他同样可以定在三百岁，三千岁，甚至定到环境选择真正的极限——直到一场迟早难免的致命事故给个体收了场。要他延长眼下的年限，需要的只是：几场如上次大战那样的大灾难，叫他确信，倘若人种要得救，至少得活得比他对高尔夫和雪茄的爱好更久。这可不是异想天开：这是演绎的生物学——倘若真有生物学这门科学的话。那么，这就是一块我们还没翻过的石头，也许值得翻一翻。为了让这个建议比写成一部生物学论著更叫大多数人爱读，我把《回到梅土撒拉》写成了献给现代圣经的一篇文字。

===Original===

Many people, however, can read treatises and cannot read Bibles. Darwin could not read Shakespeare. Some who can read both, like to learn the history of their ideas. Some are so entangled in the current confusion of Creative Evolution with Circumstantial Selection by their historical ignorance that they are puzzled by any distinction between the two. For all their sakes I must give here a little history of the conflict between the view of Evolution taken by the Darwinians (though not altogether by Darwin himself) and called Natural Selection, and that which is emerging, under the title of Creative Evolution, as the genuinely scientific religion for which all wise men are now anxiously looking.

===Chinese===

然而，许多人读得下论著，读不下圣经。达尔文就读不懂莎士比亚。有些两者都读得下的人，喜欢了解自己思想的历史。还有些人，因为对历史的无知，深深纠缠在时下把创造进化同环境选择混作一团的混乱里，分不清两者的区别就犯迷糊。为了他们大家的缘故，我必须在这里讲一讲两派进化观冲突的简史：一派是达尔文主义者（虽不全是达尔文本人的）的进化观，称为自然选择；另一派正在以“创造进化”的名义兴起，成为一切明智者正焦灼期待的、真正科学的宗教。

### The Early Evolutionists / 早期的进化论者

### The Early Evolutionists /

### The Early Evolutionists /

### The Early Evolutionists /

### The Early Evolutionists /

### The Early Evolutionists /

### The Early Evolutionists /

### The Early Evolutionists / 
===Original===

The idea of Evolution, or Transformation as it is now sometimes called, was not first conceived by Charles Darwin, nor by Alfred Russel Wallace, who observed the operation of Circumstantial Selection simultaneously with Charles. The celebrated Buffoon was a better Evolutionist than either of them; and two thousand years before Buffoon was born, the Greek philosopher Empedocles opined that all forms of life are transformations of four elements, Fire, Air, Earth, and Water, effected by the two innate forces of attraction and repulsion, or love and hate. As lately as 1860 I myself was taught as a child that everything was made out of these four elements. Both the Empedocleans and the Evolutionists were opposed to those who believed in the separate creation of all forms of life as described in the book of Genesis. This “conflict between religion and science,” as the phrase went then, did not perplex my infant mind in the least: I knew perfectly well, without knowing that I knew it, that the validity of a story is not the same as the occurrence of a fact. But as I grew up I found that I had to choose between Evolution and Genesis. If you believed that dogs and cats and snakes and birds and beetles and oysters and whales and men and women were all separately designed and made and named in Eden garden at the beginning of things, and have since survived simply by reproducing their kind, then you were not an Evolutionist. If you believed, on the contrary, that all the different species are modifications, variations, and elaborations of one primal stock, or even of a few primal stocks, then you were an Evolutionist. But you were not necessarily a Darwinian; for you might have been a modern Evolutionist twenty years before Charles Darwin was born, and a whole lifetime before he published his *Origin of Species*. For that matter, when Aristotle grouped animals with backbones as blood relations, he began the sort of classification which, when extended by Darwin to monkeys and men, so shocked my uncle.

===Chinese===

进化——如今有时叫作转化——这个观念，不是查尔斯·达尔文头一个想出来的，也不是阿尔弗雷德·拉塞尔·华莱士——他与查尔斯同时观察到环境选择的运作——想出来的。鼎鼎大名的布丰，就是比他们两位更好的进化论者；而在布丰出世前两千年，希腊哲人恩培多克勒便发表意见说，一切生命形态都是四种元素——火、气、土、水——由吸引与排斥这也就是爱与恨这两种内力促成的转化。迟至1860年，我小时候受的教养还说，万事万物都由这四种元素做成。恩培多克勒派和进化论者，都反对那些相信《创世记》所描述的一切生命形态分别受造的人。这场当时所谓的“宗教与科学之争”，一点也没难住我那颗幼小的心灵：我清清楚楚地知道——虽然不知道自己知道——一个故事的有效，同一件事实的发生，不是一回事。可等我长大，我发觉我必须在进化和《创世记》之间择一而从。倘若你相信狗、猫、蛇、鸟、甲虫、牡蛎、鲸、男人和女人，都在太初时于伊甸园里分别设计、制造、命名，此后仅靠繁殖各自的种类存活，那你就不是进化论者。反过来，倘若你相信所有不同的物种，都是同一始祖血统、或哪怕少数几支始祖血统的变异、变体和加工，那你就是进化论者。可你未必是达尔文主义者；因为你在查尔斯·达尔文出世前二十年，就能做一名现代意义上的进化论者，比他发表《物种起源》早整整一辈子。说起来，亚里士多德把有脊骨的动物归作血亲时，他已开了这种分类法的头——待到达尔文把它推及猴子和人，就吓坏了我那位叔叔。

===Original===

Genesis had held the field until the time (1707﻿–﻿1778) of Linnaeus the famous botanist. In the meantime the microscope had been invented. It revealed a new world of hitherto invisible creatures called Infusorians, as common water was found to be an infusion of them. In the eighteenth century naturalists were very keen on the Infusorian Amoebas, and were much struck by the way in which the members of this old family behaved and developed. But it was still possible for Linnaeus to begin a treatise by saying “There are just so many species as there were forms created in the beginning,” though there were hundreds of commonplace Scotch gardeners, pigeon fanciers, and stock breeders then living who knew better. Linnaeus himself knew better before he died. In the last edition of his *System of Nature*, he began to wonder whether the transmutation of species by variation might not be possible. Then came the great poet who jumped over the facts to the conclusion. Goethe said that all the shapes of creation were cousins; that there must be some common stock from which all the species had sprung; that it was the environment of air that had produced the eagle, of water the seal, and of earth the mole. He could not say how this happened; but he divined that it did happen. Erasmus Darwin, the grandfather of Charles, carried the environment theory much further, pointing out instance after instance of modifications made in species apparently to adapt it to circumstances and environment: for instance, that the brilliant colors of the leopard, which make it so conspicuous in Regent’s Park, conceal it in a tropical jungle. Finally he wrote, as his declaration of faith, “The world has been evolved, not created: it has arisen little by little from a small beginning, and has increased through the activity of the elemental forces embodied in itself, and so has rather grown than come into being at an almighty word. What a sublime idea of the infinite might of the great Architect, the Cause of all causes, the Father of all fathers, the *Ens Entium*! For if we would compare the Infinite, it would surely require a greater Infinite to cause the causes of effects than to produce the effects themselves.” In this, published in the year 1794, you have nineteenth-century Evolution precisely defined. And Erasmus Darwin was by no means its only apostle. It was in the air then.

===Chinese===

《创世记》称雄于世，直到著名植物学家林奈（1707–1778）的时代。其间显微镜发明了，揭示出一个前所未见的生灵的新世界，名为纤毛虫——寻常的水被发现原是一剂它们的浸液。十八世纪的博物学家热心于纤毛虫纲的变形虫，深为这一古老家族成员的行为和发育所动。可林奈仍能在论著开篇写下“物种之数，恰如太初所造之形之数”，尽管当时活着的成百上千个寻常的苏格兰园丁、养鸽迷和家畜育种者都明白不是这么回事。林奈本人临死前也明白了。在他《自然系统》的最后一版里，他开始怀疑，物种经由变异而发生转化，说不定是可能的。随后来了那位一跃跨过事实、直扑结论的大诗人。歌德说，万物的形态都是表亲；必有一支共同的血统，一切物种由之而生；是空气的环境造出了鹰，水的环境造出了海豹，泥土的环境造出了鼹鼠。他说不出这是怎样发生的；可他凭直觉悟到它确乎发生。查尔斯的祖父伊拉斯谟斯·达尔文把环境学说推进得更远，一例又一例地指出物种为适应处境和环境而生的变易：譬如豹子那身华丽的斑纹，在摄政公园里何等显眼，在热带丛林里却正好藏身。末了，他写下他的信经：“世界是演化而来，不是受造而来：它从一个小小的开端一点一点长起，凭自身内蕴的种种基本力量而增长，与其说是凭一句全能的话语凭空而有，不如说是长成的。对那伟大的建筑师、万因之因、万父之父、‘存在之存在’（Ens Entium）的无限权能，这是何等崇高的观念！因为若要比较无限，那么，使诸因得以成立的那个无限，必然比直接造出诸果的那个无限更大。”这段发表于1794年的话，把十九世纪的进化论说得毫厘不爽。伊拉斯谟斯·达尔文绝非它唯一的使徒。它当时就在空气中。

===Original===

A German biologist named Treviranus, whose book was published in 1802, wrote, “In every living being there exists a capacity for endless diversity of form. Each possesses the power of adapting its organization to the variations of the external world; and it is this power, called into activity by cosmic changes, which has enabled the simple zoophytes of the primitive world to climb to higher and higher stages of organization, and has brought endless variety into nature.” There you have your evolution of Man from the amoeba all complete whilst Nelson was still alive on the seas. And in 1809, before the battle of Waterloo, a French soldier named Lamarck, who had beaten his musket into a microscope and turned zoologist, declared that species were an illusion produced by the shortness of our individual lives, and that they were constantly changing and melting into one another and into new forms as surely as the hand of a clock is continually moving, though it moves so slowly that it looks stationary to us. We have since come to think that its industry is less continuous: that the clock stops for a long time, and then is suddenly “put on” by a mysterious finger. But never mind that just at present.

===Chinese===

一位名叫特雷维拉努斯的德国生物学家，他的书出版于1802年，他写道：“每一个活物身上，都存在形态无限多样的能力。每一个体都拥有使自己的构造适应外部世界变化的力量；正是这股被宇宙变化唤起的力量，使太初世界里的简单动植物爬向一级又一级更高的构造，给自然带来了无限的多样。”瞧，人自变形虫而来的进化，早在纳尔逊还在海上活着的时候，就已全套备齐了。而1809年，滑铁卢之役以前，一个名叫拉马克的法国士兵——他把火枪打成了显微镜，转行做了动物学家——宣告：物种是一种错觉，是我们个体生命短暂造成的；物种在不断变化、不断互相融和、融进新的形态，正如钟表的针在不停走动，只是走得慢，看着像静止。我们后来倒认为它的勤劳不那么连续：钟停了很久很久，然后被一根神秘的手指猛然“拨快”。不过眼下先不去管它。

### The Advent of the Neo-Lamarckians / 新拉马克主义者的登场

### The Advent of the Neo-Lamarckians /

### The Advent of the Neo-Lamarckians /

### The Advent of the Neo-Lamarckians /

### The Advent of the Neo-Lamarckians /

### The Advent of the Neo-Lamarckians /

### The Advent of the Neo-Lamarckians /

### The Advent of the Neo-Lamarckians / 
===Original===

I call your special attention to Lamarck, because later on there were Neo-Lamarckians as well as Neo-Darwinians. I was a Neo-Lamarckian. Lamarck passed on from the conception of Evolution as a general law to Charles Darwin’s department of it, which was the method of Evolution. Lamarck, whilst making many ingenious suggestions as to the reaction of external causes on life and habit, such as changes of climate, food supply, geological upheavals and so forth, really held as his fundamental proposition that living organisms changed because they wanted to. As he stated it, the great factor in Evolution is use and disuse. If you have no eyes, and want to see, and keep trying to see, you will finally get eyes. If, like a mole or a subterranean fish, you have eyes and don’t want to see, you will lose your eyes. If you like eating the tender tops of trees enough to make you concentrate all your energies on the stretching of your neck, you will finally get a long neck, like the giraffe. This seems absurd to inconsiderate people at the first blush; but it is within the personal experience of all of us that it is just by this process that a child tumbling about the floor becomes a boy walking erect; and that a man sprawling on the road with a bruised chin, or supine on the ice with a bashed occiput, becomes a bicyclist and a skater. The process is not continuous, as it would be if mere practice had anything to do with it; for though you may improve at each bicycling lesson *during* the lesson, when you begin your next lesson you do not begin at the point at which you left off: you relapse apparently to the beginning. Finally, you succeed quite suddenly, and do not relapse again. More miraculous still, you at once exercise the new power unconsciously. Although you are adapting your front wheel to your balance so elaborately and actively that the accidental locking of your handle bars for a second will throw you off; though five minutes before you could not do it at all, yet now you do it as unconsciously as you grow your finger nails. You have a new faculty, and must have created some new bodily tissue as its organ. And you have done it solely by willing. For here there can be no question of Circumstantial Selection, or the survival of the fittest.

===Chinese===

我请你特别注意拉马克，因为后来不仅有新达尔文主义者，也有新拉马克主义者。我就是个新拉马克主义者。拉马克从进化这一通律，进而研究查尔斯·达尔文分管的那部分——进化的方法。拉马克在就外部原因对生命和习性的反应提出许多巧妙猜想（如气候变化、食物供给、地壳剧变等等）的同时，真正奉为根本命题的，是活的有机体之所以变化，是因为它们想要变。照他的表述，进化的大因子是用与不用。你若没有眼睛，又想看见，一个劲儿想看见，你终会长出眼睛。你若像鼹鼠或地下鱼那样有眼睛而不想看，你会失去眼睛。你若爱吃苦口树顶的嫩叶爱到肯把全副精力集中在拉长脖子上，你终会得到长颈鹿那样的长脖子。这在不动脑筋的人乍听自然荒唐；可我们人人亲历过：一个满地打滚的娃娃变成直立行走的男孩，一个下巴磕破、趴在路上的、或后脑勺磕扁、仰在冰上的人，变成自行车手和溜冰者，靠的正是这个过程。这过程并不连续——倘若单凭练习就能成事，它本该连续：因为学车那一课里你也许*课内*确有长进，可下一课开始时，你并不从上回停下的地方接着来：你显然又跌回了起点。末了，你忽然一下子成了，而且再不跌回去。更奇的是，你立刻就在不知不觉中运用这新本事。尽管你精细而主动地把前轮调节到你的平衡上，车把偶然锁死一秒钟就会把你甩出去；尽管五分钟前你还全然不会，如今你做它，就像长指甲一样全不自觉。你有了一项新的官能，就必定造出了某种新的身体组织作它的器官。而你办成这件事，全凭意愿。因为这里谈不上环境选择，谈不上适者生存。

===Original===

The man who is learning how to ride a bicycle has no advantage over the non-cyclist in the struggle for existence: quite the contrary. He has acquired a new habit, an automatic unconscious habit, solely because he wanted to, and kept trying until it was added unto him.

===Chinese===

学骑自行车的人在生存斗争中对不会骑车的人没有优势：恰恰相反。他获得了一个新习惯，一个自动的、无意识的习惯，仅仅因为他想要它，并且不断尝试，直到它被加给了他。

### How Acquirements Are Inherited / 习得如何遗传

### How Acquirements Are Inherited /

### How Acquirements Are Inherited /

### How Acquirements Are Inherited /

### How Acquirements Are Inherited /

### How Acquirements Are Inherited /

### How Acquirements Are Inherited / 
===Original===

But when your son tries to skate or bicycle in his turn, he does not pick up the accomplishment where you left it, any more than he is born six feet high with a beard and a tall hat. The setback that occurred between your lessons occurs again. The race learns exactly as the individual learns. Your son relapses, not to the very beginning, but to a point which no mortal method of measurement can distinguish from the beginning. Now this is odd; for certain other habits of yours, equally acquired (to the Evolutionist, of course, all habits are acquired), equally unconscious, equally automatic, are transmitted without any perceptible relapse. For instance, the very first act of your son when he enters the world as a separate individual is to yell with indignation: that yell which Shakespeare thought the most tragic and piteous of all sounds. In the act of yelling he begins to breathe: another habit, and not even a necessary one, as the object of breathing can be achieved in other ways, as by deep sea fishes. He circulates his blood by pumping it with his heart. He demands a meal, and proceeds at once to perform the most elaborate chemical operations on the food he swallows. He manufactures teeth; discards them; and replaces them with fresh ones. Compared to these habitual feats, walking, standing upright, and bicycling are the merest trifles; yet it is only by going through the wanting, trying process that he can stand, walk, or cycle, whereas in the other and far more difficult and complex habits he not only does not consciously want nor consciously try, but actually consciously objects very strongly. Take that early habit of cutting the teeth: would he do that if he could help it? Take that later habit of decaying and eliminating himself by death﻿—equally an acquired habit, remember﻿—how he abhors it! Yet the habit has become so rooted, so automatic, that he must do it in spite of himself, even to his own destruction.

===Chinese===

可是轮到你的儿子学滑冰、学骑车，他并不会从你停下的地方接着学，正如他生下来不是六英尺高、留着胡子、戴着高帽。你的课与课之间发生过的倒退，又发生了。种族的学习同个体的学习一模一样。你的儿子跌回去，倒不是跌回最初的起点，而是跌回一个点，任何凡人的测量方法都无法把它同起点区分开来。这可就怪了；因为你另一些同样是习得的（对进化论者来说，一切习惯当然都是习得的）、同样无意识、同样自动的习惯，传下去却不见任何可察觉的倒退。譬如，你儿子作为独立个体进入世界的第一个行动，就是愤然放声大哭：莎士比亚认为一切声音中那最悲惨、最可怜的一声就是它。哭的同时他开始呼吸：又是一个习惯，而且并非必需的习惯，因为呼吸的目的可以用别的法子达成，深海鱼类便是。他用心脏泵血，循环血液。他要饭吃，随即对吞下的食物进行最复杂的化学加工。他造出一副牙；把它们丢掉；再换一副新的。同这些惯技相比，走路、直立、骑车不过是鸡毛蒜皮；可站立、走路、骑车，他偏偏要经历那套想要、尝试的过程才办得到，而在那些远更繁难复杂的习惯上，他不但不自觉地想要、不自觉地尝试，反而当真自觉地强烈抗议。拿长牙那个早期习惯说：他要是拦得住，肯干吗？再拿衰老和经由死亡自行退场这个较晚的习惯说——同样记着，是习得的——他何等厌恶它！可这习惯扎得那样深、那样自动，他不由自主非做不可，哪怕搭上自己的毁灭。

===Original===

We have here a routine which, given time enough for it to operate, will finally produce the most elaborate forms of organized life on Lamarckian lines without the intervention of Circumstantial Selection at all. If you can turn a pedestrian into a cyclist, and a cyclist into a pianist or violinist, without the intervention of Circumstantial Selection, you can turn an amoeba into a man, or a man into a superman, without it. All of which is rank heresy to the Neo-Darwinian, who imagines that if you stop Circumstantial Selection, you not only stop development but inaugurate a rapid and disastrous degeneration.

===Chinese===

我们手里有了一套程序，给它足够的时间去运作，就能完全不借环境选择的干预，照拉马克的路子，最终产出最复杂的有机生命形态。倘若你能不劳环境选择把一个行人变成自行车手，把自行车手变成钢琴家或小提琴家，你也能不劳它把一个变形虫变成人，或把人变成超人。而这一切，在新达尔文主义者听来都是不折不扣的异端：他们以为，环境选择一旦停摆，你不但停下了发展，还开启了急速而灾难性的退化。

===Original===

Let us fix the Lamarckian evolutionary process well in our minds. You are alive; and you want to be more alive. You want an extension of consciousness and of power. You want, consequently, additional organs, or additional uses of your existing organs: that is, additional habits. You get them because you want them badly enough to keep trying for them until they come. Nobody knows how: nobody knows why: all we know is that the thing actually takes place. We relapse miserably from effort to effort until the old organ is modified or the new one created, when suddenly the impossible becomes possible and the habit is formed. The moment we form it we want to get rid of the consciousness of it so as to economize our consciousness for fresh conquests of life; as all consciousness means preoccupation and obstruction. If we had to think about breathing or digesting or circulating our blood we should have no attention to spare for anything else, as we find to our cost when anything goes wrong with these operations. We want to be unconscious of them just as we wanted to acquire them; and we finally win what we want. But we win unconsciousness of our habits at the cost of losing our control of them; and we also build one habit and its corresponding functional modification of our organs on another, and so become dependent on our old habits. Consequently we have to persist in them even when they hurt us. We cannot stop breathing to avoid an attack of asthma, or to escape drowning. We can lose a habit and discard an organ when we no longer need them, just as we acquired them; but this process is slow and broken by relapses; and relics of the organ and the habit long survive its utility. And if other and still indispensable habits and modifications have been built on the ones we wish to discard, we must provide a new foundation for them before we demolish the old one. This is also a slow process and a very curious one.

===Chinese===

让我们把拉马克式的进化过程牢牢记在心里。你活着；你想更活着。你想要意识和力量的扩展。于是你想要额外的器官，或现有器官的额外用途：也就是额外的习惯。你得到它们，因为你热切地想要，一直试到它们到来。谁也不知道怎么来的：谁也不知道为什么：我们只知道这事情确乎发生。从一次努力到一次努力，我们悲惨地跌回去，直到旧器官被改造或新器官被造出，那一刻，不可能忽然成为可能，习惯成了。习惯一成，我们便想摆脱对它的意识，好把意识省下来，去作生活的新征服；因为一切意识都意味着牵挂和阻碍。倘若我们得惦记呼吸、消化、血液循环，就再没有注意力匀给别的了——这些运转一旦出了岔子，我们便吃这个苦头。我们想对它们无知无觉，正如我们当初想获得它们；我们终归如愿。可是我们赢得习惯的无意识，付出的代价是失去了对它们的控制；我们又在一个习惯和它相应的器官机能改造之上，再筑一个习惯，于是变得依赖我们的旧习惯。结果，哪怕它们伤害我们，我们也只得坚持下去。我们不能靠停止呼吸来躲开哮喘发作，或逃脱溺死。我们能够丢掉一个习惯、废弃一个器官，当我们不再需要它们，正如当初获得它们一样；可这过程缓慢，且不断被倒退打断；器官和习惯的残余，在其用途消失之后还长久存活。而倘若另有不可缺少的、仍然必需的习惯和改造，恰恰筑在我们想废弃的那些之上，我们就必须先给它们另造地基，再拆旧的。这也是一个缓慢而十分奇特的过程。

### The Miracle of Condensed Recapitulation / 压缩重演的奇迹

### The Miracle of Condensed Recapitulation /

### The Miracle of Condensed Recapitulation /

### The Miracle of Condensed Recapitulation /

### The Miracle of Condensed Recapitulation /

### The Miracle of Condensed Recapitulation /

### The Miracle of Condensed Recapitulation / 
===Original===

The relapses between the efforts to acquire a habit are important because, as we have seen, they recur not only from effort to effort in the case of the individual, but from generation to generation in the case of the race. This relapsing from generation to generation is an invariable characteristic of the evolutionary process. For instance, Raphael, though descended from eight uninterrupted generations of painters, had to learn to paint apparently as if no Sanzio had ever handled a brush before. But he had also to learn to breathe, and digest, and circulate his blood. Although his father and mother were fully grown adults when he was conceived, he was not conceived or even born fully grown: he had to go back and begin as a speck of protoplasm, and to struggle through an embryonic lifetime, during part of which he was indistinguishable from an embryonic dog, and had neither a skull nor a backbone. When he at last acquired these articles, he was for some time doubtful whether he was a bird or a fish. He had to compress untold centuries of development into nine months before he was human enough to break loose as an independent being. And even then he was still so incomplete that his parents might well have exclaimed “Good Heavens! have you learnt nothing from our experience that you come into the world in this ridiculously elementary state? Why can’t you talk and walk and paint and behave decently?” To that question Baby Raphael had no answer. All he could have said was that this is how evolution or transformation happens. The time may come when the same force that compressed the development of millions of years into nine months may pack many more millions into even a shorter space; so that Raphaels may be born painters as they are now born breathers and blood circulators. But they will still begin as specks of protoplasm, and acquire the faculty of painting in their mother’s womb at quite a late stage of their embryonic life. They must recapitulate the history of mankind in their own persons, however briefly they may condense it.

===Chinese===

获得一个习惯的努力之间的倒退很重要，因为如我们所见，它不仅在个体身上从一次努力到一次努力地重现，在种族身上还从一代到一代地重现。这代代之间的倒退，是进化过程一个一成不变的特征。譬如拉斐尔，虽是八代不间断的画家之后，学起画来，显然还得像桑齐奥家从没人拿过画笔那样从头学起。可他还得学呼吸，学消化，学血液循环。虽然他受孕时父母都是完全长成的成年人，他受孕时、连出生时，都不是完全长成的：他得退回去，从一粒原生质做起，挣扎着度过一段胚胎的生涯——其间有一段，他同一只胚胎的狗难分彼此，既没有头盖骨，也没有脊柱。等他终于添齐了这些家什，有段时间他还拿不准自己是鸟是鱼。他不得不把数不清的世纪的发展压缩进九个月，才够得上人，破茧而出，成为独立的生灵。而即便到那时，他仍旧那样不完整，做父母的很可以惊呼：“天哪！我们的经验你一点没学到，怎么还这么可笑地一身原始地来到世上？你怎么就不会说话、走路、画画、规规矩矩做人？”对这个问题，拉斐尔宝宝无从回答。他至多只能说：进化、或者说转化，就是这么发生的。时候一到，那股把千百万年的发展压缩进九个月的力量，也可能把更多个千百万年压进更短的期限；于是拉斐尔们可以生来就是画家，正如他们如今生来就是呼吸者、血液循环者。但他们仍旧要从一粒原生质开始，在胚胎生涯相当晚的阶段，于母亲的子宫里获得绘画的官能。不管压缩得多么简短，他们必须在自己的身体里，把人类的历史重演一遍。

===Original===

Nothing was so astonishing and significant in the discoveries of the embryologists, nor anything so absurdly little appreciated, as this recapitulation, as it is now called: this power of hurrying up into months a process which was once so long and tedious that the mere contemplation of it is unendurable by men whose span of life is threescore-and-ten. It widened human possibilities to the extent of enabling us to hope that the most prolonged and difficult operation of our minds may yet become instantaneous, or, as we call it, instinctive. It also directed our attention to examples of this packing up of centuries into seconds which were staring us in the face in all directions. As I write these lines the newspapers are occupied by the exploits of a child of eight, who has just defeated twenty adult chess players in twenty games played simultaneously, and has been able afterwards to reconstruct all the twenty games without any apparent effort of memory. Most people, including myself, play chess (when they play it at all) from hand to mouth, and can hardly recall the last move but one, or foresee the next but two. Also, when I have to make an arithmetical calculation, I have to do it step by step with pencil and paper, slowly, reluctantly, and with so little confidence in the result that I dare not act on it without “proving” the sum by a further calculation involving more ciphering. But there are men who can neither read, write, nor cipher, to whom the answer to such sums as I can do is instantly obvious without any conscious calculation at all; and the result is infallible. Yet some of these natural arithmeticians have but a small vocabulary; are at a loss when they have to find words for any but the simplest everyday occasions; and cannot for the life of them describe mechanical operations which they perform daily in the course of their trade; whereas to me the whole vocabulary of English literature, from Shakespeare to the latest edition of the *Encyclopaedia Britannica*, is so completely and instantaneously at my call that I have never had to consult even a thesaurus except once or twice when for some reason I wanted a third or fourth synonym. Again, though I have tried and failed to draw recognizable portraits of persons I have seen every day for years, Mr.

===Chinese===

在胚胎学家们的发现里，再没有比这个如今叫作“重演”的现象更惊人、更有分量的了，也没有比它更可笑地不受赏识的：这股把一个曾经漫长乏味到、以人那区区七十年寿数根本无法忍受的过程，催促成几个月的力。它把人类的可能性拓宽到这样的地步：我们尽可以希望，我们头脑中最漫长最艰难的运作，有朝一日变成瞬时的，或者照我们的说法，变成本能的。它还把我们引向那些“把世纪打包成几秒”的例子——它们本来四面八方地瞪着我们。我写下这几行的时候，报上正热闹着一位八岁神童的事迹：他刚刚同时下了二十盘棋，战胜了二十位成年棋手，事后还能不费什么明显的记忆气力，把二十盘棋一概复盘。大多数人，连我在内，下棋（要下的话）都是走一步看一步，上一步都未必记得，更看不见下两步。同样，逢到要做算术，我只得拿铅笔和纸一步一步来，又慢又勉强，对结果那样没把握，不“验算”一遍——再算一回、添上更多笔算——就不敢照它行事。可有些人，读、写、算都不会，我做得出的那类题，答案却毫不经意识地计算、当场显现，而且绝无差错。然而这班天生的算术家里，有的词汇量小得很；稍离开最简单的日常场合就找不着词；每天做惯的行当里的机械操作，打死也描述不出来；而对我来说，从莎士比亚到最新版《不列颠百科全书》的整个英国文学词汇，随叫随到、分秒不误，除了偶尔想要第三第四个同义词，我连辞书都没查过一两回。再者，我画我天天见面的人的像，试了多年也画不像，而

===Original===

Bernard Partridge, having seen a man once, will, without more strain than is involved in eating a sandwich, draw him to the life. The keyboard of a piano is a device I have never been able to master; yet Mr. Cyril Scott uses it exactly as I use my own fingers; and to Sir Edward Elgar an orchestral score is as instantaneously intelligible at sight as a page of Shakespeare is to me. One man cannot, after trying for years, finger the flute fluently. Another will take up a flute with a newly invented arrangement of keys on it, and play it at once with hardly a mistake. We find people to whom writing is so difficult that they prefer to sign their name with a mark, and beside them men who master systems of shorthand and improvise new systems of their own as easily as they learnt the alphabet. These contrasts are to be seen on all hands, and have nothing to do with variations in general intelligence, nor even in the specialized intelligence proper to the faculty in question: for example, no composer or dramatic poet has ever pretended to be able to perform all the parts he writes for the singers, actors, and players who are his executants. One might as well expect Napoleon to be a fencer, or the Astronomer Royal to know how many beans make five any better than his bookkeeper. Even exceptional command of language does not imply the possession of ideas to express; Mezzofanti, the master of fifty-eight languages, had less to say in them than Shakespeare with his little Latin and less Greek; and public life is the paradise of voluble windbags.

All these examples, which might be multiplied by millions, are cases in which a long, laborious, conscious, detailed process of acquirement has been condensed into an instinctive and unconscious inborn one. Factors which formerly had to be considered one by one in succession are integrated into what seems a single simple factor. Chains of hardly soluble problems have coalesced in one problem which solves itself the moment it is raised. What is more, they have been pushed back (or forward, if you like) from postnatal to prenatal ones. The child in the womb may take some time over them; but it is a miraculously shortened time.

===Chinese===

伯纳德·帕特里奇先生见过一个人一面，吃一块三明治的工夫也不费，就把他画得惟妙惟肖。钢琴的键盘是我始终没降服的家什；西里尔·斯科特先生用它，却正如我用我自己的手指；在爱德华·埃尔加爵士眼里，一部乐队总谱见谱即懂，如同莎士比亚的一页之于我。一个人学了多少年，也没法把长笛吹流利。另一个拿起一支键位新排的长笛，一上来就吹，几乎不错一音。我们见过这样的人：写字对他那样难，宁可画押；旁边一位，速记体系一学就会，还随手自创新体系，同认字母一样容易。这类对照比比皆是，而且同一般智力的差异不相干，甚至同该项官能的专门智力也不相干：譬如，从没有哪位作曲家或戏剧诗人敢说自己能把他写给歌手、演员、乐手的各个声部样样亲自演出来。那还不如指望拿破仑同时是位剑客，或者皇家天文官数五颗豆子比他的账房数得更好。就连非凡的语言驾驭力，也不等于有可表达的思想：通晓五十八种语言的梅佐凡蒂，用它们说出的东西，还不如莎士比亚凭他那点拉丁文和更少的希腊文说出的多；公共生活则是空谈家的乐园。
所有这些例子——还可以举上百万个——都是同一种情形：一段漫长的、费力的、自觉的、细目的习得过程，被压缩成了一个本能的、无意识的、先天的过程。从前需要逐项依次考虑的因子，被积分成一个看似单一简单的因子。几乎解不开的一串串难题，熔合为一道题，一提出便自行解开。更有甚者，它们被从产后推回（或者说前移）到了产前。子宫里的孩子做这些也许要花些时间；可那是奇迹般缩短了的时间。

===Original===

The time phenomena involved are curious, and suggest that we are either wrong about our history or else that we enormously exaggerate the periods required for the prenatal acquirement of habits. In the nineteenth century we talked very glibly about geological periods, and flung millions of eons about in the most lordly manner in our reaction against Archbishop Ussher’s chronology. We had a craze for big figures, and positively liked to believe that the progress made by the child in the womb in a month was represented in prehistoric time by ages and ages. We insisted that Evolution advanced more slowly than any snail ever crawled, and that Nature does not proceed by leaps and bounds. This was all very well as long as we were dealing with such acquired habits as breathing or digestion. It was possible to believe that dozens of epochs had gone to the slow building up of these habits. But when we have to consider the case of a man born not only as an accomplished metabolist, but with such an aptitude for shorthand and keyboard manipulation that he is a stenographer or pianist at least five sixths ready-made as soon as he can control his hands intelligently, we are forced to suspect either that keyboards and shorthand are older inventions than we suppose, or else that acquirements can be assimilated and stored as congenital qualifications in a shorter time than we think; so that, as between Lyell and Archbishop Ussher, the laugh may not be with Lyell quite so uproariously as it seemed fifty years ago.

===Chinese===

这里头的时间现象很古怪，它提示我们：要么我们把历史搞错了，要么我们大大高估了产前习得习惯所需的时间。十九世纪里，我们张口闭口地质纪，拿几百万个纪元大把大把地往外撒，用来反叛阿舍尔大主的编年。我们有一种大数字的狂热，甚至乐于相信孩子在子宫里一个月的进步，在史前时代对应着千秋万代。我们一口咬定进化比任何蜗牛爬得都慢，咬定自然决不跳跃。这在谈呼吸、消化这类习得习惯时，倒也罢了。相信几十个世代慢慢筑成这些习惯，是可能的。可当我们面对这样一个人：生下来不仅是位炉火纯青的新陈代谢者，还带着速记和键盘操作的天资，以致他能理智地管住双手的时候，至少六分之五已经现成——我们就不得不疑心：要么键盘和速记是比我们以为的更古老的发明，要么习得的东西，能在比我们想的更短的时间里被同化、储存为先天的资能；如此说来，在赖尔与阿舍尔大主教之间，笑得未必还像五十年前那样，那样毫无保留地归赖尔了。

### Heredity an Old Story / 遗传是桩旧事

### Heredity an Old Story /

### Heredity an Old Story /

### Heredity an Old Story /

### Heredity an Old Story /

### Heredity an Old Story /

### Heredity an Old Story / 
===Original===

It is evident that the evolutionary process is a hereditary one, or, to put it less drily, that human life is continuous and immortal. The Evolutionists took heredity for granted. So did everybody. The human mind has been soaked in heredity as long back as we can trace its thought. Hereditary peers, hereditary monarchs, hereditary castes and trades and classes were the best known of social institutions, and in some cases of public nuisances. Pedigree men counted pedigree dogs and pedigree horses among their most cherished possessions. Far from being unconscious of heredity, or sceptical, men were insanely credulous about it: they not only believed in the transmission of qualities and habits from generation to generation, but expected the son to begin mentally where the father left off.

===Chinese===

显然，进化过程是一个遗传的过程；或者不那么干巴巴地说：人的生命是连续的、不朽的。进化论者把遗传当作理所当然。人人如此。追溯到人类思想能及的地方，人心早就泡在遗传里了。世袭贵族、世袭君主、世袭的种姓、行当和阶级，是尽人皆知的社会制度，有些还是尽人皆知的公害。讲究血统的人，把讲血统的狗和马算在他们最珍爱的财产里。人们非但没有对遗传毫无知觉、或心存怀疑，反而对它轻信到了发疯的地步：他们不但相信品性和习惯代代相传，还指望儿子从父亲停下的地方接着想。

===Original===

This belief in heredity led naturally to the practice of Intentional Selection. Good blood and breeding were eagerly sought after in human marriage. In dealing with plants and animals, selection with a view to the production of new varieties and the improvement and modification of species had been practised ever since men began to cultivate them. My pre-Darwinian uncle knew as well as Darwin that the racehorse and the drayhorse are not separate creations from the Garden of Eden, but adaptations by deliberate human selection of the medieval warhorse to modern racing and industrial haulage. He knew that there are nearly two hundred different sorts of dogs, all capable of breeding with one another and of producing cross varieties unknown to Adam. He knew that the same thing is true of pigeons. He knew that gardeners had spent their lives trying to breed black tulips and green carnations and unheard-of orchids, and had actually produced flowers just as strange to Eve. His quarrel with the Evolutionists was not a quarrel with the evidence for Evolution: he had accepted enough of it to prove Evolution ten times over before he ever heard of it. What he repudiated was cousinship with the ape, and the implied suspicion of a rudimentary tail, because it was offensive to his sense of his own dignity, and because he thought that apes were ridiculous, and tails diabolical when associated with the erect posture. Also he believed that Evolution was a heresy which involved the destruction of Christianity, of which, as a member of the Irish Church (the pseudo-Protestant one), he conceived himself a pillar. But this was only his ignorance; for man may deny his descent from an ape and be eligible as a churchwarden without being any the less a convinced Evolutionist.

===Chinese===

这份对遗传的信仰，自然引出了有意选择的做法。人类的婚配热切地追求好血统、好门第。在花草和牲畜上，着眼于培育新品种、改良和修改物种的选择，自人开始栽培它们起就一直在做。我那位达尔文之前的叔叔同达尔文一样清楚：赛马和拉车马不是伊甸园里分别受造的两造，而是中世纪的战马，经人有意的选择，改造来适应现代的赛跑和工业运输。他知道狗有将近两百个品种，彼此都能交配，能生出亚当不曾见过的杂交变种。他知道鸽子也是这样。他知道园丁们一生一世地试着育黑郁金香、绿康乃馨和闻所未闻的兰花，而且当真培育出了夏娃见了也要称奇的花。他同进化论者争的，不是进化证据这一仗：那些证据他消化的尽够把进化证明上十遍，远在他听说进化之前。他拒不接受的，是同猿猴的表亲关系，是那条隐含的、退化尾巴的嫌疑：因为这冒犯他对自己尊严的自许，又因为他认为猿猴可笑，尾巴一旦同直立姿势沾边更是魔鬼的东西。他还相信进化是桩异端，势必毁了基督教——作为爱尔兰教会（那冒牌新教的教会）的一员，他自认是教会的一根柱子。可这不过是他无知；因为人尽可以否认自己源出猿猴、照旧够格当教堂执事，同时又是十足的进化论者。

### Discovery Anticipated by Divination / 先于发现的直觉

### Discovery Anticipated by Divination /

### Discovery Anticipated by Divination / 
===Original===

What is more, the religious folk can claim to be among the pioneers of Evolutionism. Weismann, Neo-Darwinist though he was, devoted a long passage in his *History of Evolution* to the Nature Philosophy of Lorenz Oken, published in 1809. Oken defined natural science as “the science of the everlasting transmutations of the Holy Ghost in the world.” His religion had started him on the right track, and not only led him to think out a whole scheme of Evolution in abstract terms, but guided his aim in a significantly good scientific shot which brought him within the scope of Weismann. He not only defined the original substance from which all forms of life have developed as protoplasm, or, as he called it, primitive slime (*Urschleim*), but actually declared that this slime took the form of vesicles out of which the universe was built. Here was the modern cell morphology guessed by a religious thinker long before the microscope and the scalpel forced it on the vision of mere laboratory workers who could not think and had no religion. They worked hard to discover the vital secrets of the glands by opening up dogs and cutting out the glands, or tying up their ducts, or severing their nerves, thereby learning, negatively, that the governors of our vital forces do not hold their incessant conversations through the nerves, and, positively, how miserably a horribly injured dog can die, leaving us to infer that we shall probably perish likewise if we grudge our guineas to Harley Street. Lorenz Oken *thought* very hard to find out what was happening to the Holy Ghost, and thereby made a contribution of extraordinary importance to our understanding of uninjured creatures. The man who was scientific enough to see that the Holy Ghost is a scientific fact got easily in front of the blockheads who could only sin against it. Hence my uncle was turning his back on very respectable company when he derided Evolution, and would probably have recanted and apologized at once had anybody pointed out to him what a solecism he was committing.

===Chinese===

更有甚者，信教的人尽可以自称进化论的先驱。魏斯曼虽是新达尔文主义者，也在他的《进化史》里用长长的篇幅论述洛伦兹·奥肯1809年出版的自然哲学。奥肯把自然科学定义为“圣灵在世上永恒变化的科学”。他的宗教引他上了正路，不但领他用抽象的词句想出了一整套进化方案，还指引他放出一记科学上漂亮的好箭，把他射进了魏斯曼的射程。他不但把一切生命形态由之发展的原始物质定义为原生质——他称之为原生黏液（*Urschleim*）——而且当真宣告这黏液取小泡的形态，宇宙便是由这些小泡筑成的。这就是现代的细胞形态学：一位宗教思想家早在显微镜和解剖刀把它强加给一班不会思想、没有宗教、只会实验室的操作工之前，就把它猜中了。那些操作工勤勤恳恳，要发现腺体的生命秘密，就剖开狗，割掉腺体，或扎住腺管，或切断神经，从而从反面学到：我们生命力的主管们并不经由神经进行它们不停的对话；又从正面学到：一条惨遭摧残的狗能死得多惨——留给我们去推想：倘若我们舍不得把几尼奉送给哈利街，我们多半也会照样完蛋。洛伦兹·奥肯为弄清圣灵的动静而*拼命思想*，从而对我们理解未受摧残的生灵，作出了极端重要的贡献。那个科学到看出圣灵是桩科学事实的人，轻而易举地跑到了那班只会对它犯罪的死脑筋前头。可见我叔叔讥笑进化，是把背朝着一群非常可敬的人；倘若有人向他指出他犯下的这个语病，他多半会当场悔过道歉。

===Original===

The metaphysical side of Evolution was thus no novelty when Darwin arrived. Had Oken never lived, there would still have been millions of persons trained from their childhood to believe that we are continually urged upwards by a force called the Will of God. In 1819 Schopenhauer published his treatise on *The World as Will*, which is the metaphysical complement to Lamarck’s natural history, as it demonstrates that the driving force behind Evolution is a will-to-live, and to live, as Christ said long before, more abundantly. And the earlier philosophers, from Plato to Leibniz, had kept the human mind open for the thought of the universe as one idea behind all its physically apprehensible transformations.

===Chinese===

可见，进化在形而上学方面，到达尔文来时早已不是新闻。就算没有奥肯，也还有千百万从小受教养相信我们被一股叫作“上帝的意志”的力量不断向上推的人。1819年，叔本华发表了他的《作为意志的世界》，那是拉马克自然史在形而上学上的对应物：它证明进化背后的推动力是一种求生的意志——而且，如基督早已说过的，求更丰盛地活。更早的哲学家们，从柏拉图到莱布尼茨，则一直使人心胸敞开，容得下“宇宙是同一个观念，隐在它一切可感知的形变背后”这个思想。

### Corrected Dates for the Discovery of Evolution /

All this, remember, is the state of things in the pre-Darwin period, which so many of us still think of as a pre-evolutionary period. Evolutionism was the rage before Queen Victoria came to the throne. To fix this chronology, let me repeat the story told by Weismann of the July revolution in Paris in 1830, when the French got rid of Charles the Tenth. Goethe was then still living; and a French friend of his called on him and found him wildly excited. “What do you think of the great event?” said Goethe. “The volcano is in eruption; and all is in flames. There can no longer be discussion with closed doors.” The Frenchman replied that no doubt it was a terrible business; but what could they expect with such a ministry and such a king? “Stuff!” said Goethe: “I am not thinking of these people at all, but of the open rupture in the French Academy between Cuvier and St. Hilaire. It is of the utmost importance to science,” The rupture Goethe meant was about Evolution, Cuvier contending that there were four species, and St. Hilaire that there was only one.

===Chinese===

要知道，这一切都还是达尔文之前那个时期的行情——我们当中有许多人至今还把那个时期当成进化论之前的时期。早在维多利亚女王登基以前，进化论就风行过一阵。为了把这张年表钉牢，让我把魏斯曼讲的一个故事再说一遍：1830年巴黎的七月革命，法国人在那一年赶走了查理十世。歌德当时还在世。他的一位法国朋友去看他，发现他激动得几乎发狂。“您怎么看这件大事？”歌德说。“火山喷发了；四处一片火海。再不能关起门来讨论了。”法国人回答说，这无疑是一桩可怕的事；可是有这样一届内阁、这样一位国王，还能指望什么呢？“胡扯！”歌德说，“我压根儿不是在想这些人，我想的是法兰西科学院里居维叶同圣伊莱尔的公开决裂。这事对科学至关重要。”歌德说的那场决裂，正是为了进化问题：居维叶坚称有四个物种，圣伊莱尔坚称只有一个。

===Original===

From 1830, when Darwin was an apparently unpromising lad of twenty-one, until 1859, when he turned the world upside down by his *Origin of Species*, there was a slump in Evolutionism. The first generation of its enthusiasts was ageing and dying out; and their successors were being taught from the Book of Genesis, just as Edward VI was (and Edward VII too, for that matter). Nobody who knew the theory was adding anything to it. This slump not only heightened the impression of entire novelty when Darwin brought the subject to the front again: it probably prevented him from realizing how much had been done before, even by his own grandfather, to whom he was accused of being unjust. Besides, he was not really carrying on the family business. He was an entirely original worker; and he was on a new tack, as we shall see presently. And he would not in any case have thought much, as a practical naturalist, of the more or less mystical intellectual speculations of the Deists of 1790−1830. Scientific workers were very tired of Deism just then. They had given up the riddle of the Great First Cause as insoluble, and were calling themselves, accordingly, Agnostics. They had turned from the inscrutable question of Why things existed, to the spade work of discovering What was really occurring in the world and How it really occurred.

With all his attention bent in this new direction, Darwin soon noticed that a good deal was occurring in an entirely unmystical and even unmeaning way of which the older speculative Deist-Evolutionists had taken little or no account. Nowadays, when we are turning in weary disgust and disillusion from Neo-Darwinism and Mechanism to Vitalism and Creative Evolution, it is difficult to imagine how this new departure of Darwin’s could possibly have appealed to his contemporaries as exciting, agreeable, above all as hopeful. Let me therefore try to bring back something of the atmosphere of that time by describing a scene, very characteristic of its superstitions, in which I took what was then considered an unspeakably shocking part.

===Chinese===

从1830年——那一年达尔文还是个看上去毫不起眼的二十一岁小伙子——直到1859年他凭《物种起源》把世界翻转过来，进化论闹了一场萧条。第一代热心人上了年纪，相继凋零；他们的后继者所受的教导，和爱德华六世当年所受的一样出自《创世记》（说起来，爱德华七世又何尝不是）。知道这套理论的人当中，没有谁再往里添过一点东西。这场萧条不但加深了达尔文重新把这门学问推到前台时那种全然崭新的印象，多半还使他看不出前人——连他自己的祖父——已经做下了多少，以致被人指责对祖父不公。再说，他也并不真是在继承家业。他是个全然独创的研究者；而且他走的是一条新路子，这一点我们随后就会看到。何况，身为一个务实的博物学家，他对1790至1830年间自然神论者那类多少带点神秘气的智识玄想，本来也不大会放在眼里。那时的科学工作者早对自然神论厌倦透顶。他们已把“伟大第一因”这个谜当作无解而撇下，并且据此自称不可知论者。他们已从“事物为何存在”这个无从索解的问题上转过身去，改做实证的苦工：查明世界上真正在发生着什么，以及它究竟是怎样发生的。

把全部注意力转向这个新方向之后，达尔文很快就注意到：有不少事情正在以全然不带神秘、甚至毫无意义的方式发生着，而上一辈好作思辨的自然神论进化论者对这些几乎全无理会。如今，当我们怀着厌倦的憎恶和幻灭，从新达尔文主义和机械论转向活力论和创造进化时，实在难以想象，达尔文的这一新转向当年怎么会叫他的同代人觉得刺激、觉得惬意，尤其是觉得有望。因此，让我来描述一个场面，多少唤回一点那时的空气——这场面极能代表当年种种迷信，而我在其中扮演了一个当时被认为说不出口地骇人的角色。

### Defying the Lightning: A Frustrated Experiment / 向闪电叫板：一次受挫的实验

### Defying the Lightning: A Frustrated Experiment /

### Defying the Lightning: A Frustrated Experiment /

### Defying the Lightning: A Frustrated Experiment /

### Defying the Lightning: A Frustrated Experiment / 
===Original===

One evening in 1878 or thereabouts, I, being then in my earliest twenties, was at a bachelor party of young men of the professional class in the house of a doctor in the Kensingtonian quarter of London. They fell to talking about religious revivals; and an anecdote was related of a man who, having incautiously scoffed at the mission of Messrs. Moody and Sankey, a then famous firm of American evangelists, was subsequently carried home on a shutter, slain by divine vengeance as a blasphemer. A timid minority, without quite venturing to question the truth of the incident﻿—for they naturally did not care to run the risk of going home on shutters themselves﻿—nevertheless showed a certain disposition to cavil at those who exulted in it; and something approaching to an argument began. At last it was alleged by the most evangelical of the disputants that Charles Bradlaugh, the most formidable atheist on the Secularist platform, had taken out his watch publicly and challenged the Almighty to strike him dead in five minutes if he really existed and disapproved of atheism. The leader of the cavillers, with great heat, repudiated this as a gross calumny, declaring that Bradlaugh had repeatedly and indignantly contradicted it, and implying that the atheist champion was far too pious a man to commit such a blasphemy. This exquisite confusion of ideas roused my sense of comedy. It was clear to me that the challenge attributed to Charles Bradlaugh was a scientific experiment of a quite simple, straightforward, and proper kind to ascertain whether the expression of atheistic opinions really did involve any personal risk. It was certainly the method taught in the Bible, Elijah having confuted the prophets of Baal in precisely that way, with every circumstance of bitter mockery of their god when he failed to send down fire from heaven. Accordingly I said that if the question at issue were whether the penalty of questioning the theology of Messrs. Moody and Sankey was to be struck dead on the spot by an incensed deity, nothing could effect a more convincing settlement of it than the very obvious experiment attributed to Mr. Bradlaugh, and that consequently if he had not tried it, he ought to have tried it. The omission, I added, was one which could easily be remedied there and then, as I happened to share Mr.

===Chinese===

1878年前后一个晚上，我那年才二十出头，在伦敦肯辛顿一带一位医生家里参加职业阶层青年的一场单身汉聚会。他们谈起宗教复兴运动；有人讲了一桩轶闻，说有个人不慎嘲笑了穆迪与桑基两位先生的布道事业——那是当年一家大名鼎鼎的美国福音派商号——随后便被人用百叶窗抬回家，作为渎神者被天雷殛毙了。一小撮胆子小的，虽不敢当真质疑这件事的真假——他们自然不想冒自己也被人用百叶窗抬回家的风险——却对那些乐得拍手的人露出几分想挑刺的意思；于是一场近乎争论的东西开场了。到后来，争论者当中最福音派的一位断言：查尔斯·布拉德洛（Charles Bradlaugh）——世俗主义讲坛上最厉害的无神论者——曾当众掏出怀表，向全能者叫板：倘若他真存在、真不赞成无神论，就请在五分钟内把他打死。挑刺一伙的首领大为激愤，斥之为无耻谰言，说布拉德洛已一再愤然辟谣，并暗示这位无神论主将是个极其虔诚的人，断不会犯这等渎神之罪。这团精妙的观念乱麻唤起了我的喜剧感。在我看来，安到查尔斯·布拉德洛头上的那个挑战，是一次十分简单、直截了当而且正当的科学实验，为的是查明发表无神论见解是否当真伴有人身危险。这无疑也是《圣经》里教的方法：以利亚正是这样驳倒巴力众先知的，而且当他们的神未能从天上降火时，还把他们的神狠狠挖苦了个够。于是我说，倘若争的是“质疑穆迪与桑基两位先生的神学，会不会当场被一位盛怒的神明打死”这个问题，那么天下再没有比那个安在布拉德洛先生头上的、一望而知的实验更能叫人心服的了；所以，他若没有试过，他就该试。这个缺漏，我又说，当场就可以补上，因为碰巧在这件事上，我同布拉德洛先生的看法完全一致。

===Original===

Bradlaugh’s views as to the absurdity of the belief in these violent interferences with the order of nature by a short-tempered and thin-skinned supernatural deity. Therefore﻿—and at that point I took out my watch.

The effect was electrical. Neither sceptics nor devotees were prepared to abide the result of the experiment. In vain did I urge the pious to trust in the accuracy of their deity’s aim with a thunderbolt, and the justice of his discrimination between the innocent and the guilty. In vain did I appeal to the sceptics to accept the logical outcome of their scepticism: it soon appeared that when thunderbolts were in question there were no sceptics. Our host, seeing that his guests would vanish precipitately if the impious challenge were uttered, leaving him alone with a solitary infidel under sentence of extermination in five minutes, interposed and forbade the experiment, pleading at the same time for a change of subject. I of course complied, but could not refrain from remarking that though the dreadful words had not been uttered, yet, as the thought had been formulated in my mind, it was very doubtful whether the consequences could be averted by sealing my lips. However, the rest appeared to feel that the game would be played according to the rules, and that it mattered very little what I thought so long as I said nothing. Only the leader of the evangelical party, I thought, was a little preoccupied until five minutes had elapsed and the weather was still calm.

===Chinese===

——即相信一位坏脾气、脸皮薄的超自然神明竟会这样粗暴地干涉自然秩序，实在荒唐。于是——说到这里，我掏出了怀表。

效果如同电击。怀疑派也好，虔信派也好，谁都没打算听任实验做出结果来。我劝虔信的人放心信赖他们那位神明掷雷的准头，信赖他明辨无辜与有罪的公道：白费唇舌。我呼吁怀疑的人接受怀疑主义的逻辑结论：也白费唇舌——很快就见分晓了：一牵扯到霹雳，世上就没有怀疑派了。主人眼见这句大不敬的挑战一出口，客人们就要夺门而散，撇下他独自陪着一个已判死刑、五分钟后就要伏诛的孤零零的异教徒，急忙出面禁止这项实验，同时恳请大家换个话题。我自然从命，却忍不住议论了一句：可怕的话虽不曾出口，可这个念头既已在我心里成形，封住嘴巴是不是就能免掉后果，实在大可怀疑。不过其余诸位似乎都觉得，这局牌到底还是会照规矩打，只要我什么都不说，我心里想些什么无关紧要。只有那位福音派的首领，我看，有点神不守舍，直到五分钟过去，天色依旧风平浪静。

### In Quest of the First Cause / 追寻第一因

### In Quest of the First Cause /

### In Quest of the First Cause /

### In Quest of the First Cause /

### In Quest of the First Cause / 
===Original===

Another reminiscence. In those days we thought in terms of time and space, of cause and effect, as we still do; but we do not now demand from a religion that it shall explain the universe completely in terms of cause and effect, and present the world to us as a manufactured article and as the private property of its Manufacturer. We did then. We were invited to pity the delusion of certain heathens who held that the world is supported by an elephant who is supported by a tortoise. Muhammad decided that the mountains are great weights to keep the world from being blown away into space. But we refuted these orientals by asking triumphantly what the tortoise stands on? Freethinkers asked which came first: the owl or the egg. Nobody thought of saying that the ultimate problem of existence, being clearly insoluble and even unthinkable on causation lines, could not be a causation problem. To pious people this would have been flat atheism, because they assumed that God must be a Cause, and sometimes called him The Great First Cause, or, in still choicer language, The Primal Cause. To the Rationalists it would have been a renunciation of reason. Here and there a man would confess that he stood as with a dim lantern in a dense fog, and could see but a little way in any direction into infinity. But he did not really believe that infinity was infinite or that the eternal was also sempiternal: he assumed that all things, known and unknown, were caused.

===Chinese===

再讲一段回忆。那些年月，我们用时间与空间、原因与结果来思考，今天也还是如此；只是今天我们不再要求一种宗教用因果把宇宙解释个通透，不再要它把世界当作一件制成品、当作其制造者的私产呈交给我们。当年我们却要。人家还请我们去怜悯某些异教徒的妄想，他们说世界由一头大象驮着，象又由一只龟驮着。穆罕默德断定群山是压分量的大秤砣，免得世界被吹进太空里去。可我们用一声得胜的反问驳倒了这些东方人：那龟站在什么上头？自由思想家们问：先有猫头鹰还是先有蛋？谁也不曾想到这样说：存在的终极问题，既然明明白白循因果的思路既无解又无从想起，那它压根儿就不是一个因果问题。在虔敬的人听来，这话等于赤裸裸的无神论，因为他们认定上帝必定是一个原因，有时还称他为“伟大第一因”，或者用更考究的字眼，“元初因”。在理性主义者听来，这话则是背弃理性。偶尔有一个人肯承认：自己好比提着一盏昏暗的灯笼站在浓雾里，朝哪个方向都只能望进无穷里的一小段。可他并不真信无穷是无穷的，也不真信永恒同时是无尽绵延的：他认定万事万物，无论已知的未知的，都各有其因。

===Original===

Hence it was that I found myself one day towards the end of the eighteen-seventies in a cell in the old Brompton Oratory arguing with Father Addis, who had been called by one of his flock to attempt my conversion to Roman Catholicism. The universe exists, said the father: somebody must have made it. If that somebody exists, said I, somebody must have made him. I grant that for the sake of argument, said the Oratorian. I grant you a maker of God. I grant you a maker of the maker of God.
