I grant you as long a line of makers as you please; but an infinity of makers is unthinkable and extravagant: it is no harder to believe in number one than in number fifty thousand or fifty million; so why not accept number one and stop there, since no attempt to get behind him will remove your logical difficulty? By your leave, said I, it is as easy for me to believe that the universe made itself as that a maker of the universe made himself: in fact much easier; for the universe visibly exists and makes itself as it goes along, whereas a maker for it is a hypothesis. Of course we could get no further on these lines. He rose and said that we were like two men working a saw, he pushing it forward and I pushing it back, and cutting nothing; but when we had dropped the subject and were walking through the refectory, he returned to it for a moment to say that he should go mad if he lost his belief. I, glorying in the robust callousness of youth and the comedic spirit, felt quite comfortable and said so; though I was touched, too, by his evident sincerity.

===Chinese===

因此，七十年代末的一天，我发现自己坐在老布朗普顿礼拜堂的一间小室里，同阿迪斯神父辩论；他是被羊群里的某一位请来设法劝我皈依罗马天主教的。宇宙存在着，神父说：总得有人把它造出来。倘若那“有人”存在着，我说，也总得有人把他造出来。为了辩论起见，这我承认，那位奥拉托利会士说。我给你承认一个造上帝的。我给你承认一个造“造上帝者”的。你要多长一串制造者，我都给你承认；可是无穷的制造者既不可思议又嫌浪费：信第一号并不比信第五万号或五千万号容易；既然你想绕到他背后去也除不掉你的逻辑困难，何不干脆接受第一号，就此打住？恕我直言，我说，我相信宇宙自己造出了自己，同相信宇宙的制造者自己造出了自己一样容易；说实在的还容易得多；因为宇宙明明白白存在着，而且一边走下去还一边不断造着自己，而它的制造者不过是一个假设。当然，顺着这些思路我们再也走不下去了。他站起身来，说我们像两个拉大锯的人，他把锯推过去，我把锯拉回来，什么也没锯着；可是等话题丢开、我们正穿过食堂的时候，他又回头补了一句：他要是丢了信仰，就会发疯。我呢，正仗着青年人那种结实的没心没肺和一副喜剧心肠，只觉得浑身舒坦，也照实说了；虽然他那显而易见的诚恳，也叫我为之一动。

===Original===

These two anecdotes are superficially trivial and even comic; but there is an abyss of horror beneath them. They reveal a condition so utterly irreligious that religion means nothing but belief in a nursery bogey, and its inadequacy is demonstrated by a toy logical dilemma, neither the bogey nor the dilemma having anything to do with religion, or being serious enough to impose on or confuse any properly educated child over the age of six. One hardly knows which is the more appalling: the abjectness of the credulity or the flippancy of the scepticism. The result was inevitable. All who were strong-minded enough not to be terrified by the bogey were left stranded in empty contemptuous negation, and argued, when they argued at all, as I argued with Father Addis. But their position was not intellectually comfortable. A member of parliament expressed their discomfort when, objecting to the admission of Charles Bradlaugh into parliament, he said “Hang it all, a man should believe in something or somebody.” It was easy to throw the bogey into the dustbin; but nonetheless the world, our corner of the universe, did not look like a pure accident: it presented evidences of design in every direction. There was mind and purpose behind it. As the anti-Bradlaugh member would have put it, there must be somebody behind the something: no atheist could get over that.

===Chinese===

这两段轶事，表面上是琐屑的，甚至是滑稽的；底下却是一口恐怖的深渊。它们揭示出一种境况，其非宗教达到了这步田地：宗教只剩下一个育儿室妖怪的信仰，而其不济又由一件逻辑上的小玩具来演示——妖怪也罢，两难推理也罢，都同宗教毫不相干，也都不够分量，蒙不倒也搅不乱任何一个受过像样教育的六岁以上的孩子。你简直不知道哪一样更骇人：是轻信之卑贱，还是怀疑之轻浮。结局是必然的。凡是头脑够硬、吓不倒的人，都被撇在空洞而鄙夷的否定里搁了浅，真要同人争起来，便同我同阿迪斯神父那样争。可他们的处境在理智上并不舒服。有位议员反对查尔斯·布拉德洛进入议会时，道出了这种不安：“见鬼，一个人总得信点什么，或者信什么人。”把妖怪扔进垃圾箱是容易的；可世界——我们这一角宇宙——看上去仍不像一场纯粹的偶然：它四面八方都摆着设计的证据。它背后有心思，有目的。用那位反布拉德洛议员的话来说：那个“什么”背后总得有个“谁”：哪个无神论者也迈不过这道坎。

### Paley’s Watch / 佩利的钟表

### Paley’s Watch /

### Paley’s Watch /

### Paley’s Watch /

### Paley’s Watch / 
===Original===

Paley had put the argument in an apparently unanswerable form. If you found a watch, full of mechanism exquisitely adapted to produce a series of operations all leading to the fulfilment of one central purpose of measuring for mankind the march of the day and night, could you believe that it was not the work of a cunning artificer who had designed and contrived it all to that end? And here was a far more wonderful thing than a watch, a man with all his organs ingeniously contrived, cords and levers, girders and kingposts, circulating systems of pipes and valves, dialysing membranes, chemical retorts, carburettors, ventilators, inlets and outlets, telephone transmitters in his ears, light recorders and lenses in his eye: was it conceivable that this was the work of chance? that no artificer had wrought here? that there was no purpose in this, no design, no guiding intelligence? The thing was incredible. In vain did Helmholtz declare that “the eye has every possible defect that can be found in an optical instrument, and even some peculiar to itself,” and that “if an optician tried to sell me an instrument which had all these defects I should think myself quite justified in blaming his carelessness in the strongest terms, and sending him back his instrument.” To discredit the optician’s skill was not to get rid of the optician. The eye might not be so cleverly made as Paley thought, but it was made somehow, by somebody.

And then my argument with Father Addis began all over again. It was easy enough to say that every man makes his own eyes: indeed the embryologists had actually caught him doing it. But what about the very evident purpose that prompted him to do it? Why did he want to see, if not to extend his consciousness and his knowledge and his power? That purpose was at work everywhere, and must be something bigger than the individual eye-making man. Only the stupidest muckrakers could fail to see this, and even to know it as part of their own consciousness. Yet to admit it seemed to involve letting the bogey come back, so inextricably had we managed to mix up belief in the bogey’s existence with belief in the existence of design in the universe.

===Chinese===

佩利曾把这套论证摆成一个看似无法驳倒的形式。假如你捡到一块表，里面满是机关，件件精巧地配合起来，做出一连串动作，全部引向一个中心目的——替人类量度昼夜的行程——你能相信它不是一位巧匠为着这个目的设计筹划出来的作品吗？而这儿有一件比钟表奇妙得多的东西：一个人，一身器官安排得处处见巧思——索缆与杠杆、桁架与主柱、循环不息的管道阀门系统、渗析的薄膜、化学蒸馏釜、汽化器、通风机、进气口与出气口、耳朵里的电话送话器、眼睛里的感光器和镜片：说这是偶然的作品，可想得到吗？说这里没有匠人动过手？说这里面没有目的、没有设计、没有指引的智力？这无法令人相信。亥姆霍兹宣称“眼睛具备光学仪器可能有的全部缺陷，甚至还有几种为它所独有的缺陷”，还说“倘若一位光学仪器商想卖给我一台毛病一应俱全的仪器，我会认为自己完全有理由用最严厉的字句责备他的粗心大意，并把仪器退还给他”——全都白说。抹黑光学师的手艺，除不掉光学师。眼睛也许不像佩利想的造得那么巧，可它总归是造出来的，总有某个谁来造。

于是，我同阿迪斯神父的那场辩论又从头开了场。说人人自己造自己的眼睛，这话足够容易：胚胎学家们实际上还当场捉过他现行。可是，怂恿他去造的那个明明白白的目的，又怎么说？他若不是为了扩展自己的意识、知识和力量，为什么要看？那个目的到处都在起作用，必定比那个单枪匹马造眼睛的人大。只有最蠢的揭黑幕者们才会看不见这一点，甚至不能把它当作自己意识的一部分加以体认。然而一承认它，就好像要把妖怪请回来似的：我们竟把“妖怪存在”的信仰，同“宇宙中存在设计”的信仰，缠到了如此难分难解的地步。

### The Irresistible Cry of Order, Order! / “秩序，秩序！”的不可抗拒呼声

### The Irresistible Cry of Order, Order! /

### The Irresistible Cry of Order, Order! /

### The Irresistible Cry of Order, Order! /

### The Irresistible Cry of Order, Order! / 
===Original===

Our scornful young scientific and philosophic lions of today must not blame the Church of England for this confusion of thought. In 1562 the Church, in convocation in London “for the avoiding of diversities of opinions and for the establishment of consent touching true religion,” proclaimed in their first utterance, and as an Article of Religion, that God is “without body, parts, or passions,” or, as we say, an *Elan Vital* or Life Force. Unfortunately neither parents, parsons, nor pedagogues could be induced to adopt that article. St. John might say that “God is spirit” as pointedly as he pleased; our Sovereign Lady Elizabeth might ratify the Article again and again; serious divines might feel as deeply as they could that a God with body, parts, and passions could be nothing but an anthropomorphic idol: no matter: people at large could not conceive a God who was not anthropomorphic: they stood by the Old Testament legends of a God whose parts had been seen by one of the patriarchs, and finally set up as against the Church a God who, far from being without body, parts, or passions, was composed of nothing else, and of very evil passions too. They imposed this idol in practice on the Church itself, in spite of the First Article, and thereby homeopathically produced the atheist, whose denial of God was simply a denial of the idol and a demonstration against an unbearable and most unchristian idolatry. The idol was, as Shelley had been expelled from Oxford for pointing out, an almighty fiend, with a petty character and unlimited power, spiteful, cruel, jealous, vindictive, and physically violent. The most villainous schoolmasters, the most tyrannical parents, fell far short in their attempts to imitate it. But it was not its social vices that brought it low. What made it scientifically intolerable was that it was ready at a moment’s notice to upset the whole order of the universe on the most trumpery provocation, whether by stopping the sun in the valley of Ajalon or sending an atheist home dead on a shutter (the shutter was indispensable because it marked the utter unpreparedness of the atheist, who, unable to save himself by a deathbed repentance, was subsequently roasted through all eternity in blazing brimstone). It was this disorderliness, this refusal to obey its own laws of nature, that created a scientific need for its destruction.

===Chinese===

今天我们这些傲视一切的青年科学界与哲学界雄狮，可别把这团思想混乱记在英国国教会账上。1562年，教会在伦敦的教士会议上，“为避免意见纷歧，并为确立关乎真宗教的一致同意”，在头一篇文告里、以一条宗教信条的身份宣布：上帝“无形体、无部分、无激情”——或者照我们的说法，是一种生命冲力（Elan Vital），即生命力。不幸的是，做父母的、做牧师的、做教书先生的，一个也不肯采纳那一条。圣约翰尽可以随他喜欢，要多着力就多着力地说“上帝是灵”；我们至尊的伊丽莎白女主尽可以一再批准那条信纲；严肃的神学家们尽可以深深地觉得，一位有身体、有部分、有激情的上帝只能是拟人的偶像：全然无用：大众设想不出一位不拟人的上帝：他们死守着旧约的传说——有位族长亲眼见过上帝的部分——终于竖起了一位与教会作对的上帝：他非但谈不上无形体、无部分、无激情，简直除了形体、部分、激情之外一无所有，而且激情还坏得很。他们实际上把这尊偶像强加给了教会本身，全然不顾第一条信纲，从而以顺势疗法的方式制造出无神论者——无神论者否认上帝，无非是否认这尊偶像，是对一种叫人无法忍受、极不基督徒的偶像崇拜的抗议。这尊偶像，正如雪莱因指出这一点而被牛津开除的那样，是一位全能的恶魔：器量褊狭而权力无限，怀恨、残忍、嫉妒、眦睚必报，还动辄动粗。最恶毒的校长、最暴虐的父母，模仿起他来都差得远。可是把它拉下马的，并不是它在世风上的这些恶德。使它在科学上无法容忍的是：单为一点最不值钱的口实，它就随时准备把整个宇宙的秩序掀翻，不论是叫太阳停在亚雅仑谷，还是叫一个无神论者死在百叶窗上抬回家（百叶窗必不可少，因为它标明那位无神论者毫无准备，来不及用临终忏悔自救，随后便在熊熊燃烧的硫磺火里永世被烤）。正是这种无秩序、这种拒不遵守自己立下的自然法则，造成了科学上必须除掉它的需要。

===Original===

Science could stand a cruel and unjust god; for nature was full of suffering and injustice. But a disorderly god was impossible. In the Middle Ages a compromise had been made by which two different orders of truth, religious and scientific, had been recognized, in order that a schoolman might say that two and two make four without being burnt for heresy. But the nineteenth century, steeped in a meddling, presumptuous, reading-and-writing, socially and politically powerful ignorance inconceivable by Thomas Aquinas or even Roger Bacon, was incapable of so convenient an arrangement; and science was strangled by bigoted ignoramuses claiming infallibility for their interpretation of the Bible, which was regarded, not as a literature nor even as a book, but partly as an oracle which answered and settled all questions, and partly as a talisman to be carried by soldiers in their breast pockets or placed under the pillows of persons who were afraid of ghosts. The tract shops exhibited in their windows bullet-dinted testaments, mothers’ gifts to their soldier sons whose lives had been saved by it; for the muzzle-loaders of those days could not drive a projectile through so many pages.

===Chinese===

科学受得了一位残忍而不公的上帝；因为自然界本来就满是苦难与不公。可是一位无秩序的上帝就受不下了。中世纪曾达成一项妥协，承认宗教与科学两种并行的真理秩序，好让一位经院学者说“二加二等于四”而不至于当异端烧死。可是十九世纪浸泡在一种好管闲事、僭越自大、人人能读能写、在社会与政治上强大得可怕的无知里——那种无知，托马斯·阿奎那想象不到，连罗吉尔·培根也想象不到——根本做不出这么便宜的一项安排；于是科学被一帮顽固的蠢货扼住了喉咙，他们宣称自己对《圣经》的解释绝无谬误，而《圣经》那时不被当作一部文学、甚至不被当作一部书，倒是一半被当作一尊有问必答、一答了结的神谕，一半被当作一件护身符——士兵揣在胸前口袋里，怕鬼的人压在枕头底下。小册子铺子的橱窗里陈列着给弹头打出凹痕的《新约》，那是母亲们送给当兵的儿子、靠它保住性命的礼物；因为当年的前膛枪没法把一颗弹丸射穿那么厚一沓纸页。

### The Moment and the Man / 时势与英雄

### The Moment and the Man /

### The Moment and the Man /

### The Moment and the Man /

### The Moment and the Man / 
===Original===

This superstition of a continual capricious disorder in nature, of a lawgiver who was also a lawbreaker, made atheists in all directions among clever and lightminded people. But atheism did not account for Paley’s watch. Atheism accounted for nothing; and it was the business of science to account for everything that was plainly accountable. Science had no use for mere negation: what was desired by it above all things just then was a demonstration that the evidences of design could be explained without resort to the hypothesis of a personal designer. If only some genius, whilst admitting Paley’s facts, could knock the brains out of Paley by the discovery of a method whereby watches could happen without watchmakers, that genius was assured of such a welcome from the thought of his day as no natural philosopher had ever enjoyed before.

The time being thus ripe, the genius appeared; and his name was Charles Darwin. And now, what did Darwin really discover?

===Chinese===

这一种迷信——迷信自然里有一种接连不断、任性使气的无秩序，迷信一位自己便是违法者的立法者——在聪明人和轻浮人当中四处制造出无神论者。可是无神论解释不了佩利的表。无神论什么也解释不了；而把一切显然可以解释的东西都解释出来，正是科学的本分。科学用不着纯粹的否定：它当时最渴望的，是一份证明：证明设计的种种证据，无须求助“存在一位人格化设计者”这一假设，也能解释得通。只要有一位天才，一面承认佩利的事实，一面靠发现一种不用钟表匠也能生出钟表的办法，把佩利的脑袋敲掉，那么这位天才必能享受当世思想界空前未有的欢迎。

时机既已成熟，天才应运而出；他的名字叫查尔斯·达尔文。那么，达尔文到底发现了什么呢？

===Original===

Here, I am afraid, I shall require once more the assistance of the giraffe, or, as he was called in the days of the celebrated Buffoon, the camelopard (by children, cammyleopard). I do not remember how this animal imposed himself illustratively on the Evolution controversy; but there was no getting away from him then; and I am old-fashioned enough to be unable to get away from him now. How did he come by his long neck? Lamarck would have said, by wanting to get at the tender leaves high up on the tree, and trying until he succeeded in wishing the necessary length of neck into existence. Another answer was also possible: namely, that some prehistoric stockbreeder, wishing to produce a natural curiosity, selected the longest-necked animals he could find, and bred from them until at last an animal with an abnormally long neck was evolved by intentional selection, just as the racehorse or the fantail pigeon has been evolved. Both these explanations, you will observe, involve consciousness, will, design, purpose, either on the part of the animal itself or on the part of a superior intelligence controlling its destiny. Darwin pointed out﻿—and this and no more was Darwin’s famous discovery﻿—that a third explanation, involving neither will nor purpose nor design either in the animal or anyone else, was on the cards. If your neck is too short to reach your food, you die. That may be the simple explanation of the fact that all the surviving animals that feed on foliage have necks or trunks long enough to reach it. So bang goes your belief that the necks must have been designed to reach the food. But Lamarck did not believe that the necks were so designed in the beginning: he believed that the long necks were evolved by wanting and trying. Not necessarily, said Darwin. Consider the effect on the giraffes of the natural multiplication of their numbers, as insisted on by Malthus. Suppose the average height of the foliage-eating animals is four feet, and that they increase in numbers until a time comes when all the trees are eaten away to within four feet of the ground. Then the animals who happen to be an inch or two short of the average will die of starvation. All the animals who happen to be an inch or so above the average will be better fed and stronger than the others.

===Chinese===

说到这里，恐怕我又得请长颈鹿帮忙了；在鼎鼎大名的布丰的时代，它叫作“驼豹”（孩子们叫它“骆驼豹”）。我不记得这头牲畜是怎样在进化论战里自封了例证的；反正当年谁也躲不开它；而我又老派得可以，如今照样躲不开它。它那长脖子是怎么来的？拉马克会说：靠一心够树上高处的嫩叶，试着试着，直到把需要的那段脖子“想”了出来。另一个答案也有可能：某位史前畜牧人想造出一件天然奇物，专挑他寻得到的脖子最长的牲口配种，直到最后靠有意选择育出一种脖子长得反常的动物，正如赛马或扇尾鸽就是这般育出来的。这两种解释，你会注意到，都离不开意识、意志、设计、目的——要么在动物自己身上，要么在支配它命运的一个更高智慧身上。达尔文指出——达尔文那桩著名的发现，有此而已，别无其他——还有第三种解释可以成立：它既不必动物有意志，也不必任何人有目的或设计。你的脖子若短得够不着吃的，你就死。一切以树叶为生的幸存动物，脖子或躯干都长到够得着食物——这个事实的解释，可能就这么简单。于是你那个“脖子必是被设计来够食物”的信仰，砰的一声砸了锅。可是拉马克原也不信脖子起初便是这般设计出来的：他信长脖子是靠想要和尝试进化出来的。未必然，达尔文说。试想动物数目的自然增殖——马尔萨斯强调的那回事——加在长颈鹿身上的作用。假定食叶动物的平均高度是四英尺，并且不断增殖，直到有一天，所有的树离地四英尺以下都啃光了。那么，碰巧比平均高度矮上一两寸的动物就会饿死。碰巧比平均高度高出一两寸的动物，就会吃得比别人更足，长得比别人更壮。

===Original===

They will secure the strongest and tallest mates; and their progeny will survive whilst the average ones and the sub-average ones will die out. This process, by which the species gains, say, an inch in reach, will repeat itself until the giraffe’s neck is so long that he can always find food enough within his reach, at which point, of course, the selective process stops and the length of the giraffe’s neck stops with it. Otherwise, he would grow until he could browse off the trees in the moon. And this, mark you, without the intervention of any stockbreeder, human or divine, and without will, purpose, design, or even consciousness beyond the blind will to satisfy hunger. It is true that this blind will, being in effect a will to live, gives away the whole case; but still, as compared to the open-eyed intelligent wanting and trying of Lamarck, the Darwinian process may be described as a chapter of accidents. As such, it seems simple, because you do not at first realize all that it involves. But when its whole significance dawns on you, your heart sinks into a heap of sand within you. There is a hideous fatalism about it, a ghastly and damnable reduction of beauty and intelligence, of strength and purpose, of honor and aspiration, to such casually picturesque changes as an avalanche may make in a mountain landscape, or a railway accident in a human figure. To call this Natural Selection is a blasphemy, possible to many for whom Nature is nothing but a casual aggregation of inert and dead matter, but eternally impossible to the spirits and souls of the righteous. If it be no blasphemy, but a truth of science, then the stars of heaven, the showers and dew, the winter and summer, the fire and heat, the mountains and hills, may no longer be called to exalt the Lord with us by praise; their work is to modify all things by blindly starving and murdering everything that is not lucky enough to survive in the universal struggle for hogwash.

===Chinese===

它们会赢得最强壮、最高大的配偶；它们的后代将存活，而中等的、中等以下的将死绝。这个让物种够得着的范围增加——比方说——一寸的过程，会一而再、再而三地重演，直到长颈鹿的脖子长到随处在够得着的高度里找得到足够的食料；到那一点，选择过程自然停摆，长颈鹿脖子的长度也就随之停住。不然，它会长到能啃着月亮里的树梢。而这一切，请注意，没有任何畜牧人插手，无论尘世的还是神明的；也没有意志、目的、设计，甚至没有意识——除了那条盲目充饥的欲念。不错，这条盲目的意志，既然实质上是一条求生的意志，其实早已把全部底细泄露了；不过，比起拉马克那种睁着眼、有心智的想要和尝试来，达尔文式的程序仍旧不妨说成一章偶然。这样看它似乎简单，因为你起初意识不到它牵连的全部。可当它的全部意义豁然照临你时，你的心便沉作腹中一堆散沙。这里面有一种骇人的宿命论，一种把美丽与智慧、力量与目的、荣誉与抱负，都可憎而该咒地化作了雪崩之于山色、车祸之于人身的那类偶然点缀的变化。管这个叫自然选择，是一句渎神的话：对那些把自然只看作一堆惰性死物的偶然聚合的人，这话还说得出口；对义人们的性灵与魂魄，则万世说不出口。倘若这不是渎神，而是科学的真理，那么天上的星辰、霖雨甘露、寒冬炎夏、火光暑热、群山冈陵，便再不得被请来同我们一起称颂主、尊主为大；它们的差事，是在那场争夺猪食的普遍斗争里，盲目地饿杀一切运气不够好、没能活下来的东西，以此修改万物。

### The Brink of the Bottomless Pit / 无底坑的边缘

### The Brink of the Bottomless Pit /

### The Brink of the Bottomless Pit /

### The Brink of the Bottomless Pit /

### The Brink of the Bottomless Pit / 
===Original===

Thus did the neck of the giraffe reach out across the whole heavens and make men believe that what they saw there was a gloaming of the gods. For if this sort of selection could turn an antelope into a giraffe, it could conceivably turn a pond full of amoebas into the French Academy. Though Lamarck’s way, the way of life, will, aspiration, and achievement, remained still possible, this newly shown way of hunger, death, stupidity, delusion, chance, and bare survival was also possible: was indeed most certainly the way in which many apparently intelligently designed transformations had actually come to pass. Had I not preluded with the apparently idle story of my revival of the controversial methods of Elijah, I should be asked how it was that the explorer who opened up this gulf of despair, far from being stoned or crucified as the destroyer of the honor of the race and the purpose of the world, was hailed as Deliverer, Savior, Prophet, Redeemer, Enlightener, Rescuer, Hope Giver, and Epoch Maker; whilst poor Lamarck was swept aside as a crude and exploded guesser hardly worthy to be named as his erroneous forerunner. In the light of my anecdote, the explanation is obvious. The first thing the gulf did was to swallow up Paley, and the Disorderly Designer, and Shelley’s Almighty Fiend, and all the rest of the pseudo-religious rubbish that had blocked every upward and onward path since the hopes of men had turned to Science as their true Savior. It seemed such a convenient grave that nobody at first noticed that it was nothing less than the bottomless pit, now become a very real terror. For though Darwin left a path round it for his soul, his followers presently dug it right across the whole width of the way. Yet for the moment, there was nothing but wild rejoicing: a sort of scientific mafficking. We had been so oppressed by the notion that everything that happened in the world was the arbitrary personal act of an arbitrary personal god of dangerously jealous and cruel personal character, so that even the relief of the pains of childbirth and the operating table by chloroform was objected to as an interference with his arrangements which he would probably resent, that we just jumped at Darwin. When Napoleon was asked what would happen when he died, he said that Europe would express its intense relief with a great “Ouf!”

===Chinese===

长颈鹿的脖子就这样伸过了整个天穹，叫人们相信自己在那里望见的是诸神的黄昏。因为这种选择既然能把一头羚羊变成长颈鹿，也就想见能把一池变形虫变成法兰西科学院。拉马克的路——生命、意志、抱负与成就的路——固然仍走得通；这条新开出来的路——饥饿、死亡、愚蠢、错觉、偶然与苟活的路——也同样走得通：而且十有八九，许多看上去出于巧妙设计的变形，实际上正是循着这条路来的。倘若不是先头讲了我复兴以利亚论战方法那段看似闲文的故事，人家准会问我：这个凿开绝望深渊的人，怎么非但没有作为玷辱种族荣誉、毁灭世界目的的罪人被人用石头打死、钉上十字架，反倒被奉为解救者、救主、先知、赎罪者、启蒙者、救星、授希望者、开新纪元者；而可怜的拉马克却被扫到一旁，当作一个粗陋而过时的瞎猜家，几乎不配提作他那位走错了的前驱。有了我那段轶事，解释就一望而知了。这深渊干的第一件事，便是吞掉佩利，吞掉那位无秩序的设计者，吞掉雪莱的全能恶魔，连同自人类的希望转向科学这位真正救主以来，堵塞住每一条向上向前去路的其余全部伪宗教垃圾。这坑看上去如此方便，以致起初谁也没有留意到，它不是别的，正是无底坑——如今它成了实实在在的恐怖。因为达尔文虽然还给自己灵魂在坑边留了一条绕行的路，他的追随者们不久便把那条路拦腰挖断，横贯了整个通途。然而在当时，只剩得一片狂欢：一场科学界的“马弗京式狂欢”。我们被那个观念压迫得太久了——世上发生的一切，都是一位任性之神任性的人格行为，那位神明兼有危险地嫉妒而残忍的性情，以致连用氯仿解除分娩之痛与手术台之苦，都有人反对，说是干涉了他的安排、多半要惹他怀恨——于是我们一见达尔文便扑了上去。有人问拿破仑，他死后会怎么样；他答道，欧洲会发出一声响亮的“呼——！”，一吐如释重负之情。

===Original===

: Well, when Darwin killed the god who objected to chloroform, everybody who had ever thought about it said “Ouf!” Paley was buried fathoms deep with his watch, now fully accounted for without any divine artificer at all. We were so glad to be rid of both that we never gave a thought to the consequences. When a prisoner sees the door of his dungeon open, he dashes for it without stopping to think where he shall get his dinner outside. The moment we found that we could do without Shelley’s almighty fiend intellectually, he went into the gulf that seemed only a dustbin with a suddenness that made our own lives one of the most astonishing periods in history. If I had told that uncle of mine that within thirty years from the date of our conversation I should be exposing myself to suspicions of the grossest superstition by questioning the sufficiency of Darwin; maintaining the reality of the Holy Ghost; declaring that the phenomenon of the Word becoming Flesh was occurring daily, he would have regarded me as the most extravagant madman our family had ever produced. Yet it was so. In 1906 I might have vituperated Jehovah more heartily than ever Shelley did without eliciting a protest in any circle of thinkers, or shocking any public audience accustomed to modern discussion; but when I described Darwin as “an intelligent and industrious pigeon fancier,” that blasphemous levity, as it seemed, was received with horror and indignation. The tide has now turned; and every puny whipster may say what he likes about Darwin; but anyone who wants to know what it was to be a Lamarckian during the last quarter of the nineteenth century has only to read Mr. Festing Jones’s memoir of Samuel Butler to learn how completely even a man of genius could isolate himself by antagonizing Darwin on the one hand and the Church on the other.

===Chinese===

可不是：当达尔文把那位反对氯仿的上帝杀死之后，凡是想过这问题的人，都喊了一声“呼——！”。佩利连同他的表被埋进了几英寻深的水底，那块表如今无须任何神圣工匠，就被解释得干干净净。我们庆幸把两位一并摆脱，根本无暇想到后果。囚犯一见牢门洞开，便夺门而出，顾不得想出去以后上哪儿吃饭。我们一发现自己可以不必靠雪莱那位全能恶魔来维持理智，那位恶魔便一头栽进了那个看上去不过是垃圾桶的深渊，栽得那么突然，使我们自己的一生成了历史上最叫人惊异的时期之一。倘若当年我告诉我那位叔叔：从我们那次谈话算起，不出三十年，我就会因为质疑达尔文的充分性而招来最粗暴的迷信的嫌疑；就会坚持圣灵的真实；就会宣称“道成肉身”的现象每天都在发生——他准会把我看作我们家族出产过的最离谱的疯子。然而事实如此。1906年，我可以把耶和华骂得比雪莱任何时候都狠，也不会招来任何一个思想家圈子里的抗议，也不会吓坏任何听惯了现代讨论的公众；可是当我说达尔文是“一位聪明勤勉的鸽子迷”时，那句看似轻薄的渎神之言，却被人报以骇然与义愤。如今风向转了；随便哪个不入流的碎嘴子都可以对达尔文说三道四；可谁要知道在十九世纪最后四分之一年代里当一名拉马克主义者是什么滋味，只消读一读费斯廷·琼斯先生写的塞缪尔·巴特勒的传记，就会明白：一个天才，一面开罪达尔文、一面开罪教会，能把自己孤立到何等彻底的地步。

### Why Darwin Converted the Crowd / 达尔文为何使大众皈依

### Why Darwin Converted the Crowd /

### Why Darwin Converted the Crowd /

### Why Darwin Converted the Crowd / 
===Original===

I am well aware that in describing the effect of Darwin’s discovery on naturalists and on persons capable of serious reflection on the nature and attributes of God, I am leaving the vast mass of the British public out of account. I have pointed out elsewhere that the British nation does not consist of atheists and Plymouth Brothers; and I am not now going to pretend that it ever consisted of Darwinians and Lamarckians. The average citizen is irreligious and unscientific: you talk to him about cricket and golf, market prices and party politics, not about evolution and relativity, transubstantiation and predestination. Nothing will knock into his head the fateful distinction between Evolution as promulgated by Erasmus Darwin, and Circumstantial (so-called Natural) Selection as revealed by his grandson. Yet the doctrine of Charles reached him, though the doctrine of Erasmus had passed over his head. Why did not Erasmus Darwin popularize the word Evolution as effectively as Charles?

===Chinese===

我很清楚，在描述达尔文的发现对博物学家们、对那些能够认真思考上帝的本性与属性的人们所起的作用时，我把英国大众撇在了一边。我在别处已经指出过，不列颠民族并不由无神论者和普利茅斯弟兄会组成；我现在也不打算装作它曾经由达尔文主义者和拉马克主义者组成。普通公民既不虔信宗教，也不沾科学：你跟他谈的是板球和高尔夫、市价和党派政治，不是进化和相对论、变体论和预定论。把老埃拉斯谟·达尔文所宣布的进化，同他孙子所揭示的环境选择（所谓自然选择）这二者之间的重大区别，是怎么也敲不进他的脑袋的。然而查尔斯的学说传到了他那里，虽然埃拉斯谟的学说从他头顶飘了过去。为什么埃拉斯谟·达尔文没能像查尔斯那样把“进化”这个词普及开来呢？

===Original===

The reason was, I think, that Circumstantial Selection is easier to understand, more visible and concrete, than Lamarckian evolution. Evolution as a philosophy and physiology of the will is a mystical process, which can be apprehended only by a trained, apt, and comprehensive thinker. Though the phenomena of use and disuse, of wanting and trying, of the manufacture of weight lifters and wrestlers from men of ordinary strength, are familiar enough as facts, they are extremely puzzling as subjects of thought, and lead you into metaphysics the moment you try to account for them. But pigeon fanciers, dog fanciers, gardeners, stock breeders, or stud grooms, can understand Circumstantial Selection, because it is their business to produce transformation by imposing on flowers and animals a Selection From Without. All that Darwin had to say to them was that the mere chapter of accidents is always doing on a huge scale what they themselves are doing on a very small scale. There is hardly a laborer attached to an English country house who has not taken a litter of kittens or puppies to the bucket, and drowned all of them except the one he thinks the most promising. Such a man has nothing to learn about the survival of the fittest except that it acts in more ways than he has yet noticed; for he knows quite well, as you will find if you are not too proud to talk to him, that this sort of selection occurs naturally (in Darwin’s sense) too: that, for instance, a hard winter will kill off a weakly child as the bucket kills off a weakly puppy. Then there is the farm laborer. Shakespeare’s Touchstone, a court-bred fool, was shocked to find in the shepherd a natural philosopher, and opined that he would be damned for the part he took in the sexual selection of sheep. As to the production of new species by the selection of variations, that is no news to your gardener. Now if you are familiar with these three processes: the survival of the fittest, sexual selection, and variation leading to new kinds, there is nothing to puzzle you in Darwinism.

===Chinese===

我想，原因在于：环境选择比拉马克式的进化更容易懂、更看得见、更具体。作为一种意志的哲学和生理学的进化，是一个神秘的过程，只有受过训练、资质相宜、思虑周全的思想家才能把握。使用与废弃、想要与尝试、把寻常力气的人造成举重家和角力家——这些现象作为事实固然人人熟见，作为思考的题目却极其费解，你一想解释它们，立刻就陷入形而上学。可是鸽子迷、狗迷、园丁、牲畜育种人、马夫，都懂得环境选择，因为借着从外部施加选择来改造花草牲畜，正是他们的本行。达尔文要对他们说的不过是：单纯的一连串偶然，正以巨大的规模做着他们自己以极小规模做着的事。在英国乡村宅邸里当差的劳工，几乎没有谁不曾把一窝小猫或小狗提到水桶边，全淹死，只留一只他认为最有出息的。这样的人关于适者生存，再没什么可学的，只除了它起作用的方式比他已经注意到的更多；因为他知道得很清楚（只要你肯放下架子同他谈谈就会发现），这类选择也自然而然地发生（照达尔文的意思）：比如，一个严冬会弄死一个羸弱的孩子，正如水桶弄死一只羸弱的小狗。再有就是农庄雇工。莎士比亚笔下的试金石，一位宫廷里养出来的傻子，看见牧羊人竟是位自然哲学家，大为震惊，还断定他参与绵羊的性选择，死后必下地狱。至于靠选择变异来育出新种，这在你的园丁那里也不是新闻。这三套功夫——适者生存、性选择、导致新种的变异——你若都熟悉了，达尔文主义里就没有什么能难住你的了。

===Original===

That was the secret of Darwin’s popularity. He never puzzled anybody. If very few of us have read *The Origin of Species* from end to end, it is not because it overtaxes our mind, but because we take in the whole case and are prepared to accept it long before we have come to the end of the innumerable instances and illustrations of which the book mainly consists. Darwin becomes tedious in the manner of a man who insists on continuing to prove his innocence after he has been acquitted. You assure him that there is not a stain on his character, and beg him to leave the court; but he will not be content with enough evidence: he will have you listen to all the evidence that exists in the world. Darwin’s industry was enormous. His patience, his perseverance, his conscientiousness reached the human limit. But he never got deeper beneath or higher above his facts than an ordinary man could follow him. He was not conscious of having raised a stupendous issue, because, though it arose instantly, it was not his business. He was conscious of having discovered a process of transformation and modification which accounted for a great deal of natural history. But he did not put it forward as accounting for the whole of natural history. He included it under the heading of Evolution, though it was only pseudo-evolution at best; but he revealed it as *a* method of evolution, not as *the* method of evolution. He did not pretend that it excluded other methods, or that it was the chief method. Though he demonstrated that many transformations which had been taken as functional adaptations (the current phrase for Lamarckian evolution) either certainly were or conceivably might be due to Circumstantial Selection, he was careful not to claim that he had superseded Lamarck or disproved Functional Adaptation. In short, he was not a Darwinian, but an honest naturalist working away at his job with so little preoccupation with theological speculation that he never quarrelled with the theistic Unitarianism into which he was born, and remained to the end the engagingly simple and socially easygoing soul he had been in his boyhood, when his elders doubted whether he would ever be of much use in the world.

===Chinese===

这便是达尔文得人心的秘密。他从不让任何人犯难。如果我们当中很少有人把《物种起源》从头读到尾，那不是因为它的内容超出了我们的脑力，而是因为远在读到那书主要内容的无数例证和图解之前，我们就把整个案子领教完了，准备照单全收。达尔文的啰嗦，像一个人在被宣判无罪之后还坚持继续自证清白。你向他担保他的名誉毫无污点，恳求他离开法庭；可他不满足于足够的证据：他非要你听尽世上现存的全部证据不可。达尔文的勤勉是惊人的。他的耐心、坚忍、认真达到了人类的极限。可是他钻到事实底下、或者升到事实之上的地方，从来没有深过一个普通人跟得上的程度。他没有意识到自己掀起了一个天大的问题，因为问题虽然立刻就冒了出来，却不是他的本行。他意识到的是自己发现了一个变化与变异的过程，它解释了博物学里的一大堆事情。可他没有把它端出来当作整个博物学的解释。他把它归在进化的名目之下，虽然它至多只能算伪进化；但他把它公之于众时，说的是进化的一种方法，不是进化的唯一方法。他没有假装它排除了别的方法，也没有说它是首要的方法。虽然他证明了有许多向来被当作机能性适应（拉马克式进化的通行说法）的变化，要么确实出于、要么可能出于环境选择，他却小心地不去宣称自己已经取代拉马克、驳倒机能适应。总而言之，他不是什么达尔文主义者，而是一位老老实实的博物学家，埋头做自己的本行，对神学思辨全不上心，以至从不曾同他生来便信奉的有神论一位论闹翻，到老还是小时候那个讨人喜欢、随和好处的性子——那时候他的长辈们都怀疑他将来在这世上能不能有什么出息。

### How We Rushed Down a Steep Place / 我们怎样冲下陡坡

### How We Rushed Down a Steep Place /

### How We Rushed Down a Steep Place /

### How We Rushed Down a Steep Place / 
===Original===

Not so the rest of us intellectuals. We all began going to the devil with the utmost cheerfulness. Everyone who had a mind to change, changed it. Only Samuel Butler, on whom Darwin had acted homeopathically, reacted against him furiously; ran up the Lamarckian flag to the topgallant peak; declared with penetrating accuracy that Darwin had “banished mind from the universe”; and even attacked Darwin’s personal character, unable to bear the fact that the author of so abhorrent a doctrine was an amiable and upright man. Nobody would listen to him. He was so completely submerged by the flowing tide of Darwinism that when Darwin wanted to clear up the misunderstanding on which Butler was basing his personal attacks, Darwin’s friends, very foolishly and snobbishly, persuaded him that Butler was too ill-conditioned and negligible to be answered. That they could not recognize in Butler a man of genius mattered little: what did matter was that they could not understand the provocation under which he was raging. They actually regarded the banishment of mind from the universe as a glorious enlightenment and emancipation for which he was ignorantly ungrateful. Even now, when Butler’s eminence is unchallenged, and his biographer, Mr. Festing Jones, is enjoying a vogue like that of Boswell or Lockhart, his memoirs show him rather as a shocking example of the bad controversial manners of our country parsonages than as a prophet who tried to head us back when we were gaily dancing to our damnation across the rainbow bridge which Darwinism had thrown over the gulf which separates life and hope from death and despair. We were intellectually intoxicated with the idea that the world could make itself without design, purpose, skill, or intelligence: in short, without life. We completely overlooked the difference between the modification of species by adaptation to their environment and the appearance of new species: we just threw in the word “variations” or the word “sports” (fancy a man of science talking of an unknown factor as a sport instead of as *x*!) and left them to “accumulate” and account for the difference between a cockatoo and a hippopotamus.

===Chinese===

我们这班知识分子却不然。我们都开始以最高的兴致奔向堕落。凡是有主意可换的人，都把主意换了。只有塞缪尔·巴特勒——达尔文在他身上起了顺势疗法的作用——对他狂怒地反戈一击；把拉马克的旗子升上了上桅顶；以犀利的确切宣称达尔文“把心智逐出了宇宙”；甚至攻击达尔文的个人品格，因为他忍受不了这样一个事实：一种如此可憎的学说的作者，竟是一位和善正直的人。谁也不肯听他。他被达尔文主义的滚滚潮流整个淹没，以致当达尔文想要澄清巴特勒人身攻击所依据的那场误会时，达尔文的朋友们非常愚蠢而势利地劝他说，巴特勒那人品行太差、无足轻重，不值得答理。他们认不出巴特勒是个天才，这还不要紧；要紧的是他们不明白是什么惹得他那样暴怒。他们竟把“心智逐出宇宙”看作一场光辉的启蒙和解放，而巴特勒对之不知感恩。甚至在今天，巴特勒的地位已无可争议，他的传记作者费斯廷·琼斯先生正享受着鲍斯韦尔或洛克哈特式的流行，他的回忆录却仍把他显示为一个乡下牧师住宅里坏论战风气的骇人样板，而不是一位先知——当我们兴高采烈地跳着舞、跨过达尔文主义架在生与希望同死与绝望之间的深渊上的那道彩虹桥、一路跳向自己的沉沦时，他曾试图把我们唤回头。我们在理智上陶醉于这样一个念头：世界可以无须设计、目的、技巧或智能而自己把自己造出来：简而言之，无须生命。我们全然忽略了“物种因适应环境而变异”与“新物种的出现”之间的分别：我们只把“变异”呀、“突变异种”呀这类字眼（想想看，一位科学界的人管一个未知因子叫“异种”而不叫x！）扔进去，随它们去“累积”，去解释鹦鹉同河马之间的全部区别。

===Original===

Such phrases set us free to revel in demonstrating to the Vitalists and Bible worshippers that if we once admit the existence of any kind of force, however unintelligent, and stretch out the past to unlimited time for such force to operate accidentally in, that force may conceivably, by the action of Circumstantial Selection, produce a world in which every function has an organ perfectly adapted to perform it, and therefore presents every appearance of having been designed, like Paley’s watch, by a conscious and intelligent artificer for the purpose. We took a perverse pleasure in arguing, without the least suspicion that we were reducing ourselves to absurdity, that all the books in the British Museum library might have been written word for word as they stand on the shelves if no human being had ever been conscious, just as the trees stand in the forest doing wonderful things without consciousness.

And the Darwinians went far beyond denying consciousness to trees. Weismann insisted that the chick breaks out of its eggshell automatically; that the butterfly, springing into the air to avoid the pounce of the lizard, “does not wish to avoid death; knows nothing about death,” what has happened being simply that a flight instinct evolved by Circumstantial Selection reacts promptly to a visual impression produced by the lizard’s movement. His proof is that the butterfly immediately settles again on the flower, and repeats the performance every time the lizard springs, thus showing that it learns nothing from experience, and﻿—Weismann concludes﻿—is not conscious of what it does.

===Chinese===

这些字眼放了我们的大假，任凭我们向活力论者和《圣经》崇拜者们得意洋洋地证明：只要我们承认存在任何一种力——无论多么没有智能——再把过去拉伸成无限长的时间，任凭这种力在其中偶然地起作用，那么这种力便可以想见地、靠着环境选择的作用，造出一个世界来，其中每一种官能都配有一件完美合用的器官，因而处处显出像佩利的表那样、由一位有意识有智慧的匠人抱着那个目的设计出来的样子。我们以乖张的乐趣争辩说，大英博物馆图书馆里所有的书，可以一字不差地照着书架上的样子写成，哪怕从来没有任何一个人有过意识，正如林中的树木毫无意识却做着奇妙的事情。——争辩时丝毫没有疑心我们正在把自己逼进荒谬。

达尔文主义者走得远不止否认树木有意识。魏斯曼坚持说，小鸡破壳是自动的；蝴蝶腾空避开蜥蜴的扑击，“并不是想躲死；对死一无所知”，事情不过是：环境选择演化出的一种飞行本能，对蜥蜴的动作所产生的视觉印象做出了迅速的反应。他的证据是：蝴蝶随即又落回花上，蜥蜴每扑一次它就重演一次，可见它没有从经验学到任何东西，因而——魏斯曼断定——它对自己做的事并无意识。

===Original===

It should hardly have escaped so curious an observer that when the cat jumps up on the dinner table, and you put it down, it instantly jumps up again, and finally establishes its right to a place on the cloth by convincing you that if you put it down a hundred times it will jump up a hundred and one times; so that if you desire its company at dinner you can have it only on its own terms. If Weismann really thought that cats act thus without any consciousness or any purpose, immediate or ulterior, he must have known very little about cats. But a thoroughgoing Weismannite, if any such still survive from those mad days, would contend that I am not at present necessarily conscious of what I am doing; that my writing of these lines, and your reading of them, are effects of Circumstantial Selection; that I heed know no more about Darwinism than a butterfly knows of a lizard’s appetite; and that the proof that I actually am doing it unconsciously is that as I have spent forty years in writing in this fashion without, as far as I can see, producing any visible effect on public opinion, I must be incapable of learning from experience, and am therefore a mere automaton. And the Weismannite demonstration of this would of course be an equally unconscious effect of Circumstantial Selection.

===Chinese===

像魏斯曼这样好奇的观察者，本不该看不见：猫跳上餐桌，你把它放下去，它立刻又跳上来，末了终于叫你信了它上桌布的权利——你放下去一百回，它就跳上来一百零一回；所以你若想请它共进晚餐，只能按它开的条件。倘若魏斯曼当真以为猫这样做全无意识、全无目的——不论眼前的还是日后的——那他对猫一定知道得很少。可是，倘若那些疯狂的日子里还有哪位彻头彻尾的魏斯曼信徒幸存至今，他会争辩说：我此刻未必意识得到自己在做什么；我写下这几行字，你读这几行字，都是环境选择的结果；我对达尔文主义不必比一只蝴蝶对蜥蜴的胃口知道得更多；而证明我确实是在无意识地做这件事的是：我写这类文字已经写了四十年，据我看来没有对公众舆论产生任何可见的效果，可见我必定无从从经验中学习，因而只是一架自动机。当然，那位魏斯曼信徒对这一点的论证，本身也同样是环境选择的一个无意识结果。

### Darwinism Not Finally Refutable / 达尔文主义无法彻底驳倒

### Darwinism Not Finally Refutable /

### Darwinism Not Finally Refutable /

### Darwinism Not Finally Refutable / 
===Original===

Do not too hastily say that this is inconceivable. To Circumstantial Selection all mechanical and chemical reactions are possible, provided you accept the geologists’ estimates of the great age of the earth, and therefore allow time enough for the circumstances to operate. It is true that mere survival of the fittest in the struggle for existence plus sexual selection fail as hopelessly to account for Darwin’s own life work as for my conquest of the bicycle; but who can prove that there are not other soulless factors, unnoticed or undiscovered, which only require imagination enough to fit them to the evolution of an automatic Jesus or Shakespeare? When a man tells you that you are a product of Circumstantial Selection solely, you cannot finally disprove it. You can only tell him out of the depths of your inner conviction that he is a fool and a liar. But as this, though British, is uncivil, it is wiser to offer him the counter-assurance that you are the product of Lamarckian evolution, formerly called Functional Adaptation and now Creative Evolution, and challenge him to disprove *that*, which he can no more do than you can disprove Circumstantial Selection, both forces being conceivably able to produce anything if you only give them rope enough. You may also defy him to act for a single hour on the assumption that he may safely cross Oxford Street in a state of unconsciousness, trusting to his dodging reflexes to react automatically and promptly enough to the visual impression produced by a motor bus, and the audible impression produced by its hooter. But if you allow yourself to defy him to explain any particular action of yours by Circumstantial Selection, he should always be able to find some explanation that will fit the case if only he is ingenious enough and goes far enough to find it. Darwin found several such explanations in his controversies. Anybody who really wants to believe that the universe has been produced by Circumstantial Selection cooperating with a force as inhuman as we conceive magnetism to be can find a logical excuse for his belief if he tries hard enough.

===Chinese===

先别急着说这不可思议。对环境选择来说，一切机械反应和化学反应都是可能的，只要你接受地质学家们对地球高龄的估计，因而给种种境遇留出足够的运作时间。不错，单凭生存斗争中的适者生存再加上性选择，要解释达尔文毕生的工作，同要解释我之征服自行车一样无望；可是谁能证明，不存在别的无灵魂的因子——未被注意的或未被发现——只要有足够的想象力，把它们安到一位自动的耶稣或莎士比亚的进化上就行？当一个人对你说，你纯粹是环境选择的产物，你无法彻底驳倒他。你只能从内心信念的深处告诉他，他是傻瓜和说谎者。可是这话虽是英国脾气，终究失礼，更聪明的办法是回敬他一句：你是拉马克式进化的产物——旧称机能适应、今称创造进化——然后请他来驳倒这个：他驳不倒，正如你驳不倒环境选择；两种力，只要你给足绳子，都可想见地能造出任何东西。你还可以激他：请他按“可以放心地昏迷着穿过牛津街”的假设行动哪怕一个钟头，只靠他躲避的反应机能，对公共汽车造成的视觉印象和它的喇叭造成的听觉印象自动而及时地做出反应。可要是你放任自己激他用环境选择来解释你的任何一个具体动作，那么只要他够灵巧、肯走得够远，他总能找到某种说得通的解释。达尔文在论战中就找到过好几个这样的解释。谁要是当真愿意相信宇宙是环境选择同一种像我们所设想的磁力那样无人的力合作造出来的，只要他下足够的功夫，总能给自己的信仰找到逻辑上的借口。

### Three Blind Mice / 三只瞎老鼠

### Three Blind Mice /

### Three Blind Mice /

### Three Blind Mice / 
===Original===

The stultification and damnation which ensued are illustrated by a comparison of the ease and certainty with which Butler’s mind moved to humane and inspiring conclusions with the grotesque stupidities and cruelties of the idle and silly controversy which arose among the Darwinians as to whether acquired habits can be transmitted from parents to offspring. Consider, for example, how Weismann set to work on that subject. An Evolutionist with a live mind would first have dropped the popular expression “acquired habits,” because to an Evolutionist there are no other habits and can be no others, a man being only an amoeba with acquirements. He would then have considered carefully the process by which he himself had acquired his habits. He would have assumed that the habits with which he was born must have been acquired by a similar process. He would have known what a habit is: that is, an Action voluntarily attempted until it has become more or less automatic and involuntary; and it would never have occurred to him that injuries or accidents coming from external sources against the will of the victim could possibly establish a habit; that, for instance, a family could acquire a habit of being killed in railway accidents.

And yet Weismann began to investigate the point by behaving like the butcher’s wife in the old catch. He got a colony of mice, and cut off their tails. Then he waited to see whether their children would be born without tails. They were not, as Butler could have told him beforehand. He then cut off the children’s tails, and waited to see whether the grandchildren would be born with at least rather short tails. They were not, as I could have told him beforehand. So with the patience and industry on which men of science pride themselves, he cut off the grandchildren’s tails too, and waited, full of hope, for the birth of curtailed great-grandchildren. But their tails were quite up to the mark, as any fool could have told him beforehand. Weismann then gravely drew the inference that acquired habits cannot be transmitted. And yet Weismann was not a born imbecile. He was an exceptionally clever and studious man, not without roots of imagination and philosophy in him which Darwinism killed as weeds.

===Chinese===

接下来的自愚和沉沦，可以这样看：把巴特勒的心智何等轻易、何等确然地走向仁厚而振奋人心的结论，同达尔文主义者之间那场关于“获得性习性能否由父母传给子女”的闲而愚蠢的争论里种种荒唐的蠢行与残行，比一比。且看魏斯曼是怎样着手研究这个题目的。一位头脑活络的进化论者，首先就会丢掉“获得性习性”这个流行说法，因为对进化论者来说，习性没有不是获得的，也不可能有不是获得的，人不过是一只有了种种习得的变形虫。他接着会仔细考量自己获得自己那些习性的过程。他会假定，自己生来便有的那些习性，必定也是经类似的过程获得的。他会知道习性是什么：一种自愿尝试的动作，直到它变得或多或少自动化、不随意；他绝不会想到，来自外界的、违背当事者意愿的伤害或事故竟能确立一种习性；比如，一家人能养成一种在铁路事故中丧命的习惯。

然而魏斯曼着手考察这个论点时，却行得像那支老滑稽轮唱曲里的屠夫娘子。他弄到一窝老鼠，把尾巴剁掉。然后等着看它们的崽子会不会生来就没尾巴。没有——这巴特勒本可以事先告诉他。他再把崽子们的尾巴剁掉，等着看孙辈会不会生来至少尾巴短些。没有——这我可以事先告诉他。于是，凭着科学人士引以自豪的耐心与勤勉，他把孙辈的尾巴也剁了，满怀希望地等着看断尾的重孙辈出世。可它们的尾巴完全达标——随便哪个傻子都能事先告诉他。魏斯曼于是郑重地推出结论：获得性习性不能遗传。然而魏斯曼并非天生的低能。他是个异常聪明、异常好学的人，并非没有想象力和哲学的根苗，只是被达尔文主义当作杂草除了。

===Original===

How was it that he did not see that he was not experimenting with habits or characteristics at all? How had he overlooked the glaring fact that his experiment had been tried for many generations in China on the feet of Chinese women without producing the smallest tendency on their part to be born with abnormally small feet? He must have known about the bound feet even if he knew nothing of the mutilations, the clipped ears and docked tails, practised by dog fanciers and horse breeders on many generations of the unfortunate animals they deal in. Such amazing blindness and stupidity on the part of a man who was naturally neither blind nor stupid is a telling illustration of what Darwin unintentionally did to the minds of his disciples by turning their attention so exclusively towards the part played in Evolution by accident and violence operating with entire callousness to suffering and sentiment.

A vital conception of Evolution would have taught Weismann that biological problems are not to be solved by assaults on mice. The scientific form of his experiment would have been something like this. First, he should have procured a colony of mice highly susceptible to hypnotic suggestion. He should then have hypnotized them into an urgent conviction that the fate of the musque world depended on the disappearance of its tail, just as some ancient and forgotten experimenter seems to have convinced the cats of the Isle of Man. Having thus made the mice desire to lose their tails with a life-or-death intensity, he would very soon have seen a few mice born with little or no tail. These would be recognized by the other mice as superior beings, and privileged in the division of food and in sexual selection. Ultimately the tailed mice would be put to death as monsters by their fellows, and the miracle of the tailless mouse completely achieved.

===Chinese===

他怎么会没有看出来，他根本就不是在实验习性或性状？他怎么会漏掉那个刺眼的事实：他这项实验，在中国妇女的小脚上已经试过多少代，却从没有使她们生出半分天生小脚的倾向？关于缠足，他总该知道；就算他不知道狗迷马贩们世世代代在他们经手的可怜牲畜身上行的那些残割——剪耳朵、断尾巴——他也总该知道缠足。一个天生既不瞎又不蠢的人，竟有这般惊人的盲目和愚蠢，正好生动地说明：达尔文把弟子们的注意力如此排他地引向意外和暴力——它们对痛苦与感情全然无动于衷——在进化中所起的作用，无意中把他们的头脑弄成了什么样子。

一种有生气的进化观本该教会魏斯曼：生物学问题不是靠对老鼠施暴解决的。他的实验的科学形式应当大致如下。首先，他该弄到一窝极易受催眠暗示的老鼠。然后该催眠它们，使它们深信：鼠类的命运全系于尾巴的消失——就像上古某位被遗忘的实验者似乎曾经说服了马恩岛的猫那样。这样使老鼠们以你死我活的强度渴望失去尾巴之后，他很快就会看见几只几乎没有尾巴的老鼠出生。它们会被别的老鼠认作高等生物，在分食和性选择上受到优待。末了，有尾巴的老鼠会被同类当作怪物处死，无尾老鼠的奇迹便大功告成。

===Original===

The objection to this experiment is not that it seems too funny to be taken seriously, and is not cruel enough to overawe the mob, but simply that it is impossible because the human experimenter cannot get at the mouse’s mind. And that is what is wrong with all the barren cruelties of the laboratories. Darwin’s followers did not think of this. Their only idea of investigation was to imitate “Nature” by perpetrating violent and senseless cruelties, and watch the effect of them with a paralyzing fatalism which forbade the smallest effort to use their minds instead of their knives and eyes, and established an abominable tradition that the man who hesitates to be as cruel as Circumstantial Selection itself is a traitor to science. For Weismann’s experiment upon the mice was a mere joke compared to the atrocities committed by other Darwinians in their attempts to prove that mutilations could not be transmitted. No doubt the worst of these experiments were not really experiments at all, but cruelties committed by cruel men who were attracted to the laboratory by the fact that it was a secret refuge left by law and public superstition for the amateur of passionate torture. But there is no reason to suspect Weismann of Sadism. Cutting off the tails of several generations of mice is not voluptuous enough to tempt a scientific Nero. It was a mere piece of one-eyedness; and it was Darwin who put out Weismann’s humane and sensible eye. He blinded many another eye and paralyzed many another will also. Ever since he set up Circumstantial Selection as the creator and ruler of the universe, the scientific world has been the very citadel of stupidity and cruelty. Fearful as the tribal god of the Hebrews was, nobody ever shuddered as they passed even his meanest and narrowest Little Bethel or his proudest war-consecrating cathedral as we shudder now when we pass a physiological laboratory. If we dreaded and mistrusted the priest, we could at least keep him out of the house; but what of the modern Darwinist surgeon whom we dread and mistrust ten times more, but into whose hands we must all give ourselves from time to time? Miserably as religion had been debased, it did at least still proclaim that our relation to one another was that of a fellowship in which we were all equal and members one of another before the judgment-seat of our common father.

===Chinese===

这项实验的可议之处，不在它滑稽得叫人没法认真对待，也不在它不够残酷、镇不住众人，而单纯在于它办不到：人间的实验者够不着老鼠的心思。而这正是一切实验室里徒劳的暴行之病根。达尔文的追随者们想不到这一层。他们做研究的唯一念头，就是靠施行狂暴而无意义的残害来模仿“自然”，然后带着一种叫人瘫痪的宿命论旁观其效果——这种宿命论禁止他们付出最微小的努力去用脑子，而不用刀子和眼睛，还立下了一条可憎的传统：谁对下不去像环境选择本身那样狠的手迟疑，谁就是科学的叛徒。因为魏斯曼对老鼠的实验，比起别的达尔文主义者为证明残割不能遗传而犯下的种种暴行，不过是一句玩笑。无疑，其中最坏的那些根本算不上实验，而是残忍的人犯下的残行——实验室之所以招引他们，正因为它在法律和公众的迷信庇护下，仍是留给热衷酷刑的业余者的一处秘密避难所。但没有理由怀疑魏斯曼有虐待狂。剁掉好几代老鼠的尾巴，还不够香艳，诱惑不了一位科学界的尼禄。那不过是一种独眼之盲；而戳瞎魏斯曼那只人道的、明白的眼睛的，正是达尔文。他还戳瞎了许多别的眼睛，麻痹了许多别的意志。自他把环境选择立为宇宙的创造者和主宰以来，科学界一直是愚蠢与残暴的正牌堡垒。希伯来人的部落之神尽管可怕，可人们走过他哪怕最寒酸的小礼拜堂、最骄傲的祝圣大教堂，也从来没有像我们今天走过一间生理实验室时那样不寒而栗。我们惧怕又提防牧师，至少还能把他挡在门外；可是现代的达尔文主义外科医生，我们惧怕提防十倍，却还得时时把自己交到他手里，这又怎么说呢？宗教虽然被败坏得不成样子，至少还宣告过：我们彼此的关系是一种情谊，在共同的天父的审判座前，人人平等，互为肢体。

===Original===

Darwinism proclaimed that our true relation is that of competitors and combatants in a struggle for mere survival, and that every act of pity or loyalty to the old fellowship is a vain and mischievous attempt to lessen the severity of the struggle and preserve inferior varieties from the efforts of Nature to weed them out. Even in Socialist Societies which existed solely to substitute the law of fellowship for the law of competition, and the method of providence and wisdom for the method of rushing violently down a steep place into the sea, I found myself regarded as a blasphemer and an ignorant sentimentalist because whenever the Neo-Darwinian doctrine was preached there I made no attempt to conceal my intellectual contempt for its blind coarseness and shallow logic, or my natural abhorrence of its sickening inhumanity.

===Chinese===

达尔文主义却宣告：我们的真正关系，是一场单纯求生存的斗争中的竞争者和角斗士；而对旧日情谊的每一分怜悯或忠诚，都是徒劳而有害的企图，想减轻斗争的严酷，掩护劣等品种逃脱自然除莠的努力。甚至在那些专为以情谊之法取代竞争之法、以审慎明智之法取代一头栽下陡坡冲进海里之法而存在的社会主义团体里，我也发现自己被当作渎神者和无知的滥情者，因为每逢新达尔文主义在那里被宣讲，我从不掩饰我理智上对其盲目粗鄙、逻辑浅薄的不屑，和我天性上对其令人作呕的不人道的憎恶。

### The Greatest of These Is Self-Control / 其中最大的是自制

### The Greatest of These Is Self-Control /

### The Greatest of These Is Self-Control /

### The Greatest of These Is Self-Control / 
===Original===

As there is no place in Darwinism for free will, or any other sort of will, the Neo-Darwinists held that there is no such thing as self-control. Yet self-control is just the one quality of survival value which Circumstantial Selection must invariably and inevitably develop in the long run. Uncontrolled qualities may be selected for survival and development for certain periods and under certain circumstances. For instance, since it is the ungovernable gluttons who strive the hardest to get food and drink, their efforts would develop their strength and cunning in a period of such scarcity that the utmost they could do would not enable them to overeat themselves. But a change of circumstances involving a plentiful supply of food would destroy them. We see this very thing happening often enough in the case of the healthy and vigorous poor man who becomes a millionaire by one of the accidents of our competitive commerce, and immediately proceeds to dig his grave with his teeth. But the self-controlled man survives all such changes of circumstance, because he adapts himself to them, and eats neither as much as he can hold nor as little as he can scrape along on, but as much as is good for him. What is self-control? It is nothing but a highly developed vital sense, dominating and regulating the mere appetites. To overlook the very existence of this supreme sense; to miss the obvious inference that it is the quality that distinguishes the fittest to survive; to omit, in short, the highest moral claim of Evolutionary Selection: all this, which the Neo-Darwinians did in the name of Natural Selection, showed the most pitiable want of mastery of their own subject, the dullest lack of observation of the forces upon which Natural Selection works.

===Chinese===

达尔文主义里既然没有自由意志的位置，也没有任何别的意志的位置，新达尔文主义者便主张根本没有自制这回事。然而自制恰恰是一种具有生存价值的品质，从长远看，环境选择必定一成不变、不可避免地把它发展出来。不受节制的品质，可能在某些时期、某些境况下被选中而得以生存和发展。比如，既然拼死拼活抢吃喝的是那些管不住嘴的馋鬼，那么在一个匮乏到他们使出浑身解数也吃不撑的时期，他们的气力和狡黠便会得到发展。可是一旦境况改变、食物充足，他们便完了。这种事我们见得够多了：一个健壮而精力充沛的穷人，靠竞争性商业的一场意外成了百万富翁，立刻动手用牙齿给自己掘墓。可是自制的人经得起这一切境况之变，因为他随机应变，吃既不吃到撑，也不省到刮，只吃到于他相宜的分量。什么是自制？不过是一种高度发达的生命感，统辖并调节单纯的口腹之欲。看不见这种至高官能的存在；错过那个一望而知的推论——它正是区分谁最配生存的品质；一句话，漏掉进化选择的最高道德主张：这一切，新达尔文主义者都在自然选择的名义下干了出来，足见他们对本行何等可怜地缺乏掌握，对自然选择赖以起作用的种种力量，观察得何等迟钝。

### A Sample of Lamarcko-Shavian Invective / 一段拉马克—萧伯纳式谩骂的标本

### A Sample of Lamarcko-Shavian Invective /

### A Sample of Lamarcko-Shavian Invective /

### A Sample of Lamarcko-Shavian Invective / 
===Original===

The Vitalist philosophers made no such mistakes. Nietzsche, for example, thinking out the great central truth of the *Will to Power* instead of cutting off mouse-tails, had no difficulty in concluding that the final objective of this Will was power over self, and that the seekers after power over others and material possessions were on a false scent.

The stultification naturally became much worse as the first Darwinians died out. The prestige of these pioneers, who had the older evolutionary culture to build on, and were in fact no more Darwinian in the modern sense than Darwin himself, ceased to dazzle us when Huxley and Tyndall and Spencer and Darwin passed away, and we were left with the smaller people who began with Darwin and took in nothing else. Accordingly, I find that in the year 1906 I indulged my temper by hurling invectives at the Neo-Darwinians in the following terms.

“I really do not wish to be abusive; but when I think of these poor little dullards, with their precarious hold of just that corner of evolution that a blackbeetle can understand﻿—with their retinue of twopenny-halfpenny Torquemadas wallowing in the infamies of the vivisector’s laboratory, and solemnly offering us as epoch-making discoveries their demonstrations that dogs get weaker and die if you give them no food; that intense pain makes mice sweat; and that if you cut off a dog’s leg the three-legged dog will have a four-legged puppy, I ask myself what spell has fallen on intelligent and humane men that they allow themselves to be imposed on by this rabble of dolts, blackguards, impostors, quacks, liars, and, worst of all, credulous conscientious fools. Better a thousand times Moses and Spurgeon (a then famous preacher) back again. After all, you cannot understand Moses without imagination nor Spurgeon without metaphysics; but you can be a thoroughgoing Neo-Darwinian without imagination, metaphysics, poetry, conscience, or decency. For ‘Natural Selection’ has no moral significance: it deals with that part of evolution which has no purpose, no intelligence, and might more appropriately be called accidental selection, or better still, Unnatural Selection, since nothing is more unnatural than an accident. If it could be proved that the whole universe had been produced by such Selection, only fools and rascals could bear to live.”

===Chinese===

活力论哲学家们没有犯这类错误。比如尼采，他不去剁老鼠尾巴，而是把“权力意志”这个伟大的中心真理想了个透，于是轻而易举地得出结论：这种意志的最终目标是支配自己；而追求支配他人和物质财货的人，则找错了方向。

随着第一代达尔文主义者相继凋零，这自愚自然愈演愈烈。那些开路人自有较早的进化文化可作根基，事实上也并不比达尔文本人更具有现代意义上的达尔文主义色彩；赫胥黎、廷德尔、斯宾塞和达尔文相继去世之后，他们名望的光环不再眩惑我们，我们身边只剩下些小一号的人物，从达尔文起步，旁的什么也没吸收。于是我发现，1906年，我曾用下面这样的字句冲新达尔文主义者发泄我的火气，聊以快意。

“我实在不愿恶语伤人；可是一想到这班可怜的小庸才，他们摇摇晃晃抓着的，只是进化里连一只蟑螂都能懂的那个角落——他们身后跟着一群不值几个钱的托尔克马达，在活体解剖实验室的种种丑行里打滚，还郑重其事地把他们的‘划时代发现’端给我们：狗不给吃的会变弱、会死；剧痛叫老鼠出汗；剁掉狗一条腿，三条腿的狗会生出四条腿的小狗——我免不了问自己：究竟是什么咒落到了聪明而仁厚的人们头上，叫他们任由这帮蠢货、恶棍、骗子、庸医、说谎者，还有最糟的、轻信而凭良心办事的傻子牵着鼻子走？宁可让摩西和司布真（当年一位有名的布道家）再回来一千次。毕竟，没有想象力你懂不了摩西，没有形而上学你懂不了司布真；可是没有想象力、没有形而上学、没有诗、没有良心、没有体面，你照样可以当一个彻头彻尾的新达尔文主义者。因为‘自然选择’没有任何道德意义：它处理的是进化中没有目的、没有智能的那一部分，把它叫作偶然选择其实更贴切，或者更干脆叫作非自然选择，因为没有比偶然更不自然的东西了。倘若能证明整个宇宙就是由这种选择造出来的，那就只有傻瓜和恶棍才受得了活下去。”

### The Humanitarians and the Problem of Evil / 人道主义者与恶的问题

### The Humanitarians and the Problem of Evil /

### The Humanitarians and the Problem of Evil / 
===Original===

Yet the humanitarians were as delighted as anybody with Darwinism at first. They had been perplexed by the Problem of Evil and the Cruelty of Nature. They were Shelleyists, but not atheists. Those who believed in God were at a terrible disadvantage with the atheist. They could not deny the existence of natural facts so cruel that to attribute them to the will of God is to make God a demon. Belief in God was impossible to any thoughtful person without belief in the Devil as well. The painted Devil, with his horns, his barbed tail, and his abode of burning brimstone, was an incredible bogey; but the evil attributed to him was real enough; and the atheists argued that the author of evil, if he exists, must be strong enough to overcome God, else God is morally responsible for everything he permits the Devil to do. Neither conclusion delivered us from the horror of attributing the cruelty of nature to the workings of an evil will, or could reconcile it with our impulses towards justice, mercy, and a higher life.

A complete deliverance was offered by the discovery of Circumstantial Selection: that is to say, of a method by which horrors having every appearance of being elaborately planned by some intelligent contriver are only accidents without any moral significance at all. Suppose a watcher from the stars saw a frightful accident produced by two crowded trains at full speed crashing into one another! How could he conceive that a catastrophe brought about by such elaborate machinery, such ingenious preparation, such skilled direction, such vigilant industry, was quite unintentional? Would he not conclude that the signalmen were devils?

===Chinese===

人道主义者们起初同样为达尔文主义欢欣鼓舞。他们本来被恶的问题和自然的残忍折磨得不知所措。他们是雪莱派，但不是无神论者。信神的人在无神论者面前吃大亏。他们无法否认那些残酷得骇人的自然事实——把它们归于上帝的意志，就等于把上帝说成魔鬼。对任何有思想的人来说，信上帝而不连带信魔鬼，是不可能的。画上那个长角、拖着带刺的尾巴、住在燃烧硫磺里的魔鬼，固然是叫人难以相信的妖怪；可记在他名下的恶却千真万确；无神论者于是争辩说：恶的作者倘若存在，必定强得过上帝，否则上帝就得为他容许魔鬼做的一切在道义上负责。两个结论都救不了我们：既不能使我们摆脱“把自然的残忍归之于一个邪恶意志的作为”这一恐怖，也无法叫它与我们对正义、仁慈和更高生活的冲动相调和。

环境选择的发现提供了一种彻底的解脱：也就是说，它发现了一种方法，靠它，那些处处显得由某个聪明的谋划者精心布置出来的恐怖，原来不过是全无道德意义的意外。设想一位从星辰上俯瞰的旁观者，看见两列满载的火车全速相撞，酿成一场可怕的事故！他怎么能设想这样一场灾祸——凭着那么精巧的机械、那么费心的准备、那么娴熟的指挥、那么不解的勤勉做成的——竟是完全无心的？他难道不会断定那些扳道工是魔鬼吗？

===Original===

Well, Circumstantial Selection is largely a theory of collisions: that is, a theory of the innocence of much apparently designed devilry. In this way Darwin brought intense relief as well as an enlarged knowledge of facts to the humanitarians. He destroyed the omnipotence of God for them; but he also exonerated God from a hideous charge of cruelty. Granted that the comfort was shallow, and that deeper reflection was bound to show that worse than all conceivable devil-deities is a blind, deaf, dumb, heartless, senseless mob of forces that strike as a tree does when it is blown down by the wind, or as the tree itself is struck by lightning. That did not occur to the humanitarians at the moment: people do not reflect deeply when they are in the first happiness of escape from an intolerably oppressive situation. Like Bunyan’s pilgrim they could not see the wicket gate, nor the Slough of Despond, nor the castle of Giant Despair; but they saw the shining light at the end of the path, and so started gaily towards it as Evolutionists.

And they were right; for the problem of evil yields very easily to Creative Evolution. If the driving power behind Evolution is omnipotent only in the sense that there seems no limit to its final achievement; and if it must meanwhile struggle with matter and circumstance by the method of trial and error, then the world must be full of its unsuccessful experiments. Christ may meet a tiger, or a High Priest arm-in-arm with a Roman Governor, and be the unfittest to survive under the circumstances. Mozart may have a genius that prevails against Emperors and Archbishops, and a lung that succumbs to some obscure and noxious property of foul air. If all our calamities are either accidents or sincerely repented mistakes, there is no malice in the Cruelty of Nature and no Problem of Evil in the Victorian sense at all. The theology of the women who told us that they became atheists when they sat by the cradles of their children and saw them strangled by the hand of God is succeeded by the theology of Blanco Posnet, with his “It was early days when He made the croup, I guess. It was the best He could think of then; but when it turned out wrong on His hands He made you and me to fight the croup for Him.”

===Chinese===

可不是嘛，环境选择很大程度上正是一门相撞的理论：也就是一种理论，证明许多表面上出于设计的恶魔行径其实无罪。就这样，达尔文给人道主义者们带来了强烈的宽慰，也带来了扩充了的事实知识。他为他们摧毁了上帝的全能；可他也替上帝洗脱了一桩骇人听闻的残忍罪名。诚然这宽慰是浅薄的，深一层的反思必定会指出：比一切想得到的恶魔神祇更糟的，是一伙又瞎又聋又哑、无心无肺、无知无觉的力，它们打下来，如同树被风刮倒时打下来一样，也如同树本身挨雷劈一样。人道主义者们当时没有想到这一层：人们刚从不堪忍受的重压下逃出来、正处在头一阵欢喜里的时候，是不作深思的。他们像班扬笔下的香客，还望不见那扇小门，望不见绝望泥潭，望不见绝望巨人的城堡；可是他们望见了小路尽头那盏发亮的灯，于是便以进化论者的身份，欢欢喜喜地朝它走去。

他们走得对；因为恶的问题，到了创造进化手里便迎刃而解。倘若进化背后的推动力只是在“它的最终成就看来没有止境”这个意义上全能；倘若它同时还必须用尝试与犯错的方法同物质与境遇搏斗，那么世界上必定满是它不成功的实验。基督可能遇上一只老虎，或者遇上一位和罗马总督挽臂而行的大祭司，在那样的境况下最不适于生存。莫扎特可能有一种天才，斗得过皇帝们和大主教们，却有一叶肺，败给了污浊空气里某种阴暗有毒的成分。倘若我们的一切灾祸不是意外便是诚心悔过的失误，那么自然的残忍里便没有恶意，维多利亚时代意义上的恶的问题也就根本不存在了。那些坐在孩子摇篮边、眼看他们被上帝之手扼死、便就此成了无神论者的妇女们的神学，如今让位给了布兰科·波斯奈特的神学——“我想，他造哮吼的时候还是早期的日子。那是他当时想得出的最好的办法；可是等它在手里做坏了，他就造了你和我，来替他跟哮吼作战。”

### How One Touch of Darwin Makes the Whole World Kin / 一点达尔文，四海皆亲

### How One Touch of Darwin Makes the Whole World Kin /

### How One Touch of Darwin Makes the Whole World Kin / 
===Original===

Another humanitarian interest in Darwinism was that Darwin popularized Evolution generally, as well as making his own special contribution to it. Now the general conception of Evolution provides the humanitarian with a scientific basis, because it establishes the fundamental equality of all living things. It makes the killing of an animal murder in exactly the same sense as the killing of a man is murder. It is sometimes necessary to kill men as it is always necessary to kill tigers; but the old theoretic distinction between the two acts has been obliterated by Evolution. When I was a child and was told that our dog and our parrot, with whom I was on intimate terms, were not creatures like myself, but were brutal whilst I was reasonable, I not only did not believe it, but quite consciously and intellectually formed the opinion that the distinction was false; so that afterwards, when Darwin’s views were first unfolded to me, I promptly said that I had found out all that for myself before I was ten years old; and I am far from sure that my youthful arrogance was not justified; for this sense of the kinship of all forms of life is all that is needed to make Evolution not only a conceivable theory, but an inspiring one. St. Anthony was ripe for the Evolution theory when he preached to the fishes, and St. Francis when he called the birds his little brothers. Our vanity, and our snobbish conception of Godhead as being, like earthly kingship, a supreme class distinction instead of the rock on which Equality is built, had led us to insist on God offering us special terms by placing us apart from and above all the rest of his creatures. Evolution took that conceit out of us; and now, though we may kill a flea without the smallest remorse, we at all events know that we are killing our cousin. No doubt it shocks the flea when the creature that an almighty Celestial Flea created expressly for the food of fleas, destroys the jumping lord of creation with his sharp and enormous thumbnail; but no flea will ever be so foolish as to preach that in slaying fleas Man is applying a method of Natural Selection which will finally evolve a flea so swift that no man can catch him, and so hardy of constitution that Insect Powder will have no more effect on him than strychnine on an elephant.

===Chinese===

人道主义者们对达尔文主义的另一重兴趣在于：达尔文普及了进化这个总概念，同时也作出了他自己的特殊贡献。而进化这个总概念，给仁爱精神提供了一个科学基础，因为它确立了一切生物的根本平等。它使杀一只动物成为谋杀，同杀一个人是谋杀在完全相同的意义上。杀人有时是必要的，正如杀虎总是必要的；可是这两桩行为之间旧日的理论区分，已被进化抹掉了。我小时候听人说，同我亲密相处的我们的狗和我们的鹦鹉并非和我一样的生灵，它们是畜生而我是理性的，我不但不信，还相当自觉、相当理智地形成了一种看法：这个区分是假的；所以后来初次听人讲解达尔文的观点时，我脱口便说，这一切我在十岁以前就自己发现了；而且我远不敢断定我少年人的这份狂妄没有道理；因为对一切生命形态的亲缘感，正是使进化不仅成为一种可信的理论、而且成为一种催人奋起的理论所需要的一切。圣安东尼向鱼儿布道的时候，已经到了成熟待收于进化论的时节；圣法兰西斯称鸟儿为小兄弟的时候也是。我们的虚荣心，加上我们那种势利的神性观——把神格看成同尘世的王位一样，是一种至高的等级区分，而不是平等所赖建立的那块磐石——曾经引得我们坚持：上帝必须给我们特殊的待遇，把我们放在、并且高抬到其余一切受造物之外之上。进化把这份自负从我们身上拿掉了；如今，我们尽管可以毫不懊悔地捏死一只跳蚤，至少我们知道：我们杀的是自己的表亲。毫无疑问，当那只被全能的天上跳蚤特地为跳蚤们造出来当食物的动物，用他那又尖又大的拇指甲捏死这位蹦蹦跳跳的受造物之王时，跳蚤们是震惊的；可是没有一只跳蚤会蠢到宣讲：人在杀跳蚤时是在运用一种自然选择的方法，最终会进化出一只跳蚤，快得没有一个人捉得住它，体格壮得杀虫粉对它不比马钱子碱对大象更管用。

### Why Darwin Pleased the Socialists / 达尔文为何讨社会主义者喜欢

### Why Darwin Pleased the Socialists /

### Why Darwin Pleased the Socialists / 
===Original===

The Humanitarians were not alone among the agitators in their welcome to Darwin. He had the luck to please everybody who had an axe to grind. The Militarists were as enthusiastic as the Humanitarians, the Socialists as the Capitalists. The Socialists were specially encouraged by Darwin’s insistence on the influence of environment. Perhaps the strongest moral bulwark of Capitalism is the belief in the efficacy of individual righteousness. Robert Owen made desperate efforts to convince England that her criminals, her drunkards, her ignorant and stupid masses, were the victims of circumstance: that if we would only establish his new moral world we should find that the masses born into an educated and moralized community would be themselves educated and moralized. The stock reply to this is to be found in Lewes’s *Life of Goethe*. Lewes scorned the notion that circumstances govern character. He pointed to the variety of character in the governing rich class to prove the contrary. Similarity of circumstance can hardly be carried to a more desolating dead level than in the case of the individuals who are born and bred in English country houses, and sent first to Eton or Harrow, and then to Oxford or Cambridge, to have their minds and habits formed. Such a routine would destroy individuality if anything could. Yet individuals come out from it as different as Pitt from Fox, as Lord Russell from Lord Gurzon, as Mr. Winston Churchill from Lord Robert Cecil. This acceptance of the congenital character of the individual as the determining factor in his destiny had been reinforced by the Lamarckian view of Evolution. If the giraffe can develop his neck by wanting and trying, a man can develop his character in the same way. The old saying, “Where there is a will, there is a way,” condenses Lamarck’s theory of functional adaptation into a proverb. This felt bracingly moral to strong minds, and reassuringly pious to feeble ones. There was no more effective retort to the Socialist than to tell him to reform himself before he pretends to reform society. If you were rich, how pleasant it was to feel that you owed your riches to the superiority of your own character! The industrial revolution had turned numbers of greedy dullards into monstrously rich men.

===Chinese===

在欢迎达尔文的鼓动家当中，人道主义者们并不孤单。他的运气好，凡有私事要办的人都叫他称了心。军国主义者同人道主义者一样起劲，社会主义者同资本家一样起劲。社会主义者尤其受到达尔文对环境影响的强调的鼓舞。资本主义最坚固的道德壁垒，也许要数“个人德行自有其效验”这个信仰。罗伯特·欧文曾拼命想叫英国相信：她的罪犯、她的酒鬼、她愚昧愚蠢的民众，都是境遇的受害者；只要我们肯建立他的新道德世界，就会发现生在一个受了教育、有了道德的共同体里的民众，自己也就会有教育、有道德。对这一套，现成的反驳见刘易斯的《歌德传》。刘易斯嗤笑境遇决定性格之说。他指着统治的富人阶级里性格的五花八门来反证。境遇的雷同，再也难得比这更荒凉死平的了：生在英国乡间巨宅里、长在英国乡间巨宅里，先送伊顿或哈罗，再送牛津或剑桥，让他们的头脑和习惯在那里成形。这样的规程倘若真有什么能磨掉个性，它早就磨掉了。可是从里面走出来的人，个个不同：皮特不同于福克斯，罗素勋爵不同于寇松勋爵，温斯顿·丘吉尔先生不同于罗伯特·塞西尔勋爵。这种把个人的天生性格认作其命运决定因素的意见，又被拉马克式的进化观加固了。长颈鹿既然能靠想要和尝试长出脖子，人也能照此长出自己的性格。那句老话“有志者事竟成”，把拉马克的机能适应说浓缩成了一句谚语。这在强者听来提气提德，在弱者听来安心安魄。对社会主义者最有效的回敬，莫过于劝他先改造自己，再假装改造社会。倘若你有钱，想到自己的财富是出于自己品格的优越，那该多么惬意！工业革命已经把成群的贪心蠢汉变成了富得骇人的富翁。

===Original===

Nothing could be more humiliating and threatening to them than the view that the falling of a shower of gold into their pockets was as pure an accident as the falling of a shower of hail on their umbrellas, and happened alike to the just and unjust. Nothing could be more flattering and fortifying to them than the assumption that they were rich because they were virtuous.

Now Darwinism made a clean sweep of all such self-righteousness. It more than justified Robert Owen by discovering in the environment of an organism an influence on it more potent than Owen had ever claimed. It implied that street arabs are produced by slums and not by original sin: that prostitutes are produced by starvation wages and not by feminine concupiscence. It threw the authority of science on the side of the Socialist who said that he who would reform himself must first reform society. It suggested that if we want healthy and wealthy citizens we must have healthy and wealthy towns; and that these can exist only in healthy and wealthy countries. It could be led to the conclusion that the type of character which remains indifferent to the welfare of its neighbors as long as its own personal appetite is satisfied is the disastrous type, and the type which is deeply concerned about its environment the only possible type for a permanently prosperous community. It showed that the surprising changes which Robert Owen had produced in factory children by a change in their circumstances which does not seem any too generous to us nowadays were as nothing to the changes﻿—changes not only of habits but of species, not only of species but of orders﻿—which might conceivably be the work of environment acting on individuals without any character or intellectual consciousness whatever. No wonder the Socialists received Darwin with open arms.

===Chinese===

对他们来说，再没有比这个看法更丢脸、更威胁人的了：一场金雨落进他们口袋，同一场冰雹打在他们的伞上一样，是纯粹的偶然，义人歹人一样摊得上。也再没有比这个假定更奉承人、更壮胆的了：他们有钱，是因为他们有德。

而达尔文主义把这一切自义一扫而光。它在一个生物体的环境里发现了一种对生物的影响，其效力远超欧文曾经声言的一切，这就比正当还多地成全了罗伯特·欧文。它意味着：街头野孩子是贫民窟的产物，不是原罪的产物；妓女是饥饿工资的产物，不是女性淫欲的产物。它把科学的权威扔到了这样说的社会主义者一边：谁要改造自己，必先改造社会。它提示：我们要健康而富足的公民，就必须有健康而富足的城镇；而这样的城镇只能存在于健康而富足的国家里。它还能引到这样的结论：那种只求填饱自家肚皮、对邻人福祉漠不关心的性格类型，是致祸的类型；而那种对自身环境深切挂怀的类型，才是一个长治久安的共同体唯一可能的类型。它表明：罗伯特·欧文靠改变境遇——那改变在今天的我们看来还说不上怎样慷慨——在工厂童工身上引起的惊人变化，同环境可能做出的变化相比简直不算什么：那变化不但及于习惯，而且及于物种；不但及于物种，而且及于目——环境作用在毫无性格、毫无理智意识的个体身上，也未尝不能做出这等事来。难怪社会主义者们张开双臂迎接达尔文。

### Darwin and Karl Marx / 达尔文与卡尔·马克思

### Darwin and Karl Marx /

### Darwin and Karl Marx / 
===Original===

Besides, the Socialists had an evolutionary prophet of their own, who had discredited Manchester as Darwin discredited the Garden of Eden. Karl Marx had proclaimed in his *Communist Manifesto* of 1848 (now enjoying Scriptural authority in Russia) that civilization is an organism evolving irresistibly by circumstantial selection; and he published the first volume of his *Das Kapital* in 1867. The revolt against anthropomorphic idolatry, which was, as we have seen, the secret of Darwin’s success, had been accompanied by a revolt against the conventional respectability which covered not only the brigandage and piracy of the feudal barons, but the hypocrisy, inhumanity, snobbery, and greed of the bourgeoisie, who were utterly corrupted by an essentially diabolical identification of success in life with big profits. The moment Marx showed that the relation of the bourgeoisie to society was grossly immoral and disastrous, and that the whited wall of starched shirt fronts concealed and defended the most infamous of all tyrannies and the basest of all robberies, he became an inspired prophet in the mind of every generous soul whom his book reached. He had said and proved what they wanted to have proved; and they would hear nothing against him. Now Marx was by no means infallible: his economics, half borrowed, and half homemade by a literary amateur, were not, when strictly followed up, even favorable to Socialism. His theory of civilisation had been promulgated already in Buckle’s *History of Civilization*, a book as epoch-making in the minds of its readers as *Das Kapital*. There was nothing about Socialism in the widely read first volume of *Das Kapital*: every reference it made to workers and capitalists showed that Marx had never breathed industrial air, and had dug his case out of bluebooks in the British Museum. Compared to Darwin, he seemed to have no power of observation: there was not a fact in *Das Kapital* that had not been taken out of a book, nor a discussion that had not been opened by somebody else’s pamphlet. No matter: he exposed the bourgeoisie and made an end of its moral prestige. That was enough: like Darwin he had for the moment the World Will by the ear.

===Chinese===

况且，社会主义者们自己还有一位进化论的先知，他已把曼彻斯特搞臭，正像达尔文把伊甸园搞臭一样。卡尔·马克思在1848年的《共产党宣言》（如今在俄罗斯享受着圣经般的权威）里宣告：文明是一个有机体，靠环境选择不可抗拒地进化着；1867年，他又出版了《资本论》第一卷。那场对拟人偶像崇拜的反叛——我们已经看到，那正是达尔文成功的秘密——是同另一场反叛相伴而来的：对循规蹈矩的体面的反叛，那体面不但遮掩着封建男爵们的打劫与海盗行径，还遮掩着资产阶级的伪善、非人、势利与贪婪——资产阶级被一种本质上是恶魔式的“人生成功等同于巨额利润”的观念彻底腐化了。马克思一指出资产阶级同社会的关系在道义上是彻头彻尾的不道德和灾难性的，一指出浆硬的衬衫前胸那道粉墙后面，遮掩并保卫着一切暴政中最臭名昭著的暴政、一切抢劫中最卑劣的抢劫，他在每一本他的书所抵达的慷慨灵魂的心目中，便成了一位得到神启的先知。他说出了、证明了他们正想有人来证明的东西；于是谁说他半句坏话，他们都不听。其实马克思绝非无谬：他的经济学，一半借来的，一半由一位文学业余家自己炮制，认真推敲起来，甚至并不利于社会主义。他的文明学说，早就在巴克尔那部《文明史》里发布过了——那部书在它的读者心目中，同《资本论》一样开了一个纪元。读者甚众的《资本论》第一卷里其实没有一个字讲到社会主义：它提到工人和资本家的每一处，都表明马克思从未呼吸过工业的空气，他的案卷是从大英博物馆的蓝皮书里掘出来的。同达尔文一比，他似乎毫无观察力：《资本论》里没有一个事实不是从书里抄来的，没有一场论证不是由别人的小册子先开的头。都不要紧：他揭露了资产阶级，终结了它的道德威望。这就够了：像达尔文一样，他一时抓住了世界意志的耳朵。

===Original===

Marx had, too, what Darwin had not: implacability and a fine Jewish literary gift, with terrible powers of hatred, invective, irony, and all the bitter qualities bred, first in the oppression of a rather pampered young genius (Marx was the spoilt child of a well-to-do family) by a social system utterly uncongenial to him, and later on by exile and poverty. Thus Marx and Darwin between them toppled over two closely related idols, and became the prophets of two new creeds.

===Chinese===

马克思还有一样达尔文没有的东西：绝不宽容的脾性，和一份出色的犹太文学天才，带着可怕的仇恨、谩骂、讥讽的力量，以及一切由苦水泡出来的刻毒——先是在一个与他不合的社会制度下，一位备受娇惯的少年天才（马克思是个殷实人家宠坏的孩子）受的压抑，随后是流亡与贫穷。就这样，马克思和达尔文联手掀翻了两尊血脉相通的偶像，成了两个新信条的先知。

### Why Darwin Pleased the Profiteers Also / 达尔文为何也讨牟利者喜欢

### Why Darwin Pleased the Profiteers Also /

### Why Darwin Pleased the Profiteers Also / 
===Original===

But how, at this rate, did Darwin succeed with the capitalists too? It is not easy to make the best of both worlds when one of the worlds is preaching a Class War, and the other vigorously practising it. The explanation is that Darwinism was so closely related to Capitalism that Marx regarded it as an economic product rather than as a biological theory. Darwin got his main postulate, the pressure of population on the available means of subsistence, from the treatise of Malthus on Population, just as he got his other postulate of a practically unlimited time for that pressure to operate from the geologist Lyell, who made an end of Archbishop Ussher’s Biblical estimate of the age of the earth as 4004 BC plus AD. The treatises of the Ricardian economists on the Law of Diminishing Return, which was only the Manchester School’s version of the giraffe and the trees, were all very fiercely discussed when Darwin was a young man. In fact the discovery in the eighteenth century by the French Physiocrats of the economic effects of Commercial Selection in soils and sites, and by Malthus of a competition for subsistence which he attributed to pressure of population on available subsistence, had already brought political science into that unbreathable atmosphere of fatalism which is the characteristic blight of Darwinism. Long before Darwin published a line, the Ricardo-Malthusian economists were preaching the fatalistic Wages Fund doctrine, and assuring the workers that Trade Unionism is a vain defiance of the inexorable laws of political economy, just as the Neo-Darwinians were presently assuring us that Temperance Legislation is a vain defiance of Natural Selection, and that the true way to deal with drunkenness is to flood the country with cheap gin and let the fittest survive. Cobdenism is, after all, nothing but the abandonment of trade to Circumstantial Selection.

===Chinese===

可是照这么说，达尔文怎么又连资本家也讨好了呢？两个世界一个宣讲阶级战争、一个起劲地实践阶级战争，要两头讨好可不容易。解释是：达尔文主义同资本主义的关系太密切了，以致马克思干脆把它看作一个经济学产物，而非生物学理论。达尔文的主要公设——人口对可用的生存资料的压力——得自马尔萨斯的《人口论》，正如他的另一条公设——那个压力有实际上无限的时间可以运作——得自地质学家赖尔，是赖尔了结了厄谢尔大主教以《圣经》推算的地球年龄：公元前4004年加上公元后。达尔文青年时代，李嘉图派经济学家们关于报酬递减律的种种论著——那不过是曼彻斯特学派版的长颈鹿和树木——正吵得火热。事实上，早在十八世纪，法国重农派已经发现了商业选择在土壤和区位上的经济效应，马尔萨斯发现了对生存资料的竞争——他把它归因于人口对可用生存资料的压力——政治学从此便被带进了那股叫人窒息的宿命论空气里，那正是达尔文主义特有的瘴疠。远在达尔文发表一行字以前，李嘉图—马尔萨斯派的经济学家们已经在宣讲宿命论的工资基金学说，向工人们保证：工联主义是对政治经济学铁律的徒然挑衅；正如新达尔文主义者们不久向我们保证：禁酒立法是对自然选择的徒然挑衅，对付酗酒的正道，是让廉价杜松子酒淹满全国，让适者生存。科布登主义，说到底，不过是把贸易丢给环境选择而已。

===Original===

It is hardly possible to exaggerate the importance of this preparation for Darwinism by a vast political and clerical propaganda of its moral atmosphere. Never in history, as far as we know, had there been such a determined, richly subsidized, politically organized attempt to persuade the human race that all progress, all prosperity, all salvation, individual and social, depend on an unrestrained conflict for food and money, on the suppression and elimination of the weak by the strong, on Free Trade, Free Contract, Free Competition, Natural Liberty, Laisser-faire: in short, on “doing the other fellow down” with impunity, all interference by a guiding government, all organization except police organization to protect legalized fraud against fisticuffs, all attempt to introduce human purpose and design and forethought into the industrial welter, being “contrary to the laws of political economy.” Even the proletariat sympathized, though to them Capitalist liberty meant only wage slavery without the legal safeguards of chattel slavery. People were tired of governments and kings and priests and providences, and wanted to find out how Nature would arrange matters if she were let alone. And they found it out to their cost in the days when Lancashire used up nine generations of wage slaves in one generation of their masters. But their masters, becoming richer and richer, were very well satisfied, and Bastiat proved convincingly that Nature had arranged Economic Harmonies which would settle social questions far better than theocracies or aristocracies or mobocracies, the real *deus ex machina* being unrestrained plutocracy.

===Chinese===

一场庞大的政治与教士的宣传，预先替达尔文主义布置好了这股道德空气——这一准备工作的重要性，怎么夸张也不过分。据我们所知，历史上还从不曾有过这样一场决心坚定、资金雄厚、政治上有组织的运动，来劝说人类：一切进步、一切繁荣、一切拯救，个人的也罢社会的也罢，全靠一场不受约束的食物与金钱之争，靠强者对弱者的压制与消灭，靠自由贸易、自由契约、自由竞争、自然自由、放任主义：一句话，靠肆无忌惮地“整垮对方”；而引导性的政府的一切干预、除保护合法欺诈不被拳头侵犯的警察组织之外的一切组织、把人类的目的设计与远虑引进工业混沌的一切尝试，都“违背政治经济学的规律”。连无产阶级也附和，虽然资本家的自由对他们只意味着工资奴役——连动产奴隶制的法律保障也没有。人们厌倦了政府、国王、牧师和天意，想看看放任自然、由她自去安排会是怎样。后来他们吃了苦头才看明白：那是在兰开夏用老板一代的工夫耗尽工资奴隶九代的日子。可是老板们越搞越富，心满意足，巴斯夏还令人信服地证明了：自然已经安排好种种“经济和谐”，解决社会问题，远比神权政治、贵族政治或暴民政治高明；真正的解围之神，是不受约束的财阀政治。

### The Poetry and Purity of Materialism / 唯物主义的诗与纯洁

### The Poetry and Purity of Materialism /

### The Poetry and Purity of Materialism / 
===Original===

Thus the stars in their courses fought for Darwin. Every faction drew a moral from him; every catholic hater of faction founded a hope on him; every blackguard felt justified by him; and every saint felt encouraged by him. The notion that any harm could come of so splendid an enlightenment seemed as silly as the notion that the atheists would steal all our spoons. The physicists went further than the Darwinians. Tyndall declared that he saw in Matter the promise and potency of all forms of life, and with his Irish graphic lucidity made a picture of a world of magnetic atoms, each atom with a positive and a negative pole, arranging itself by attraction and repulsion in orderly crystalline structure.
