Such a picture is dangerously fascinating to thinkers oppressed by the bloody disorders of the living world. Craving for purer subjects of thought, they find in the contemplation of crystals and magnets a happiness more dramatic and less childish than the happiness found by the mathematicians in abstract numbers, because they see in the crystals beauty and movement without the corrupting appetites of fleshly vitality. In such Materialism as that of Lucretius and Tyndall there is a nobility which produces poetry: John Davidson found his highest inspiration in it. Even its pessimism as it faces the cooling of the sun and the return of the icecaps does not degrade the pessimist: for example, the Quincy Adamses, with their insistence on modern democratic degradation as an inevitable result of solar shrinkage, are not dehumanized as the vivisectionists are. Perhaps nobody is at heart fool enough to believe that life is at the mercy of temperature: Dante was not troubled by the objection that Brunetto could not have lived in the fire nor Ugolino in the ice.

===Chinese===

就这样，星辰都循着轨道为达尔文而战。每一宗派都从他那里抽出一套道德；每一憎恨宗派的大公教徒都在他身上立起一个希望；每一个恶棍都觉得得了他的辩护；每一位圣徒都觉得得了他的鼓励。这样一场光辉的启蒙居然会带来什么害处——这念头在当时显得同“无神论者会偷走我们全部汤匙”的念头一样傻。物理学家们走得更远。廷德尔宣称他在物质中看到了一切生命形态的允诺和潜能，并以他那爱尔兰式的鲜明透彻，画出一幅磁性原子的世界图景：每个原子都有正负两极，靠吸引与排斥排列成井然的晶体结构。这样一幅图景，对于被血淋淋的生命世界的无秩序压得喘不过气来的思想家们，有一种危险的魅惑。渴望更纯洁的思考对象的人们，在凝视晶体和磁石中找到一种幸福，比数学家们在抽象数字里找到的幸福更有戏剧性、也不那么孩子气，因为他们从晶体中看到了美与运动，却没有血肉生命那种腐蚀性的欲望。在卢克莱修和廷德尔那种唯物主义里，有一种能生出诗来的高贵：约翰·戴维森便从中汲取了他最高的灵感。连它面对太阳冷却、冰盖重临时的悲观，也不贬低悲观者：比如昆西·亚当斯们，尽管坚称现代民主的败坏是太阳收缩的必然结果，也不曾像活体解剖家们那样失去人性。大概没有谁打心底里蠢到相信生命听凭温度摆布：但丁就从未为这个反驳伤过脑筋——布鲁内托不可能活在火里，乌戈利诺也不可能活在冰里。

===Original===

But the physicists found their intellectual vision of the world incommunicable to those who were not born with it. It came to the public simply as Materialism; and Materialism lost its peculiar purity and dignity when it entered into the Darwinian reaction against Bible fetishism. Between the two of them religion was knocked to pieces; and where there had been a god, a cause, a faith that the universe was ordered however inexplicable by us its order might be, and therefore a sense of moral responsibility as part of that order, there was now an utter void. Chaos had come again. The first effect was exhilarating: we had the runaway child’s sense of freedom before it gets hungry and lonely and frightened. In this phase we did not desire our God back again. We printed the verses in which William Blake, the most religious of our great poets, called the anthropomorphic idol Old Nobodaddy, and gibed at him in terms which the printer had to leave us to guess from his blank spaces. We had heard the parson droning that God is not mocked; and it was great fun to mock Him to our hearts’ content and not be a penny the worse. It did not occur to us that Old Nobodaddy, instead of being a ridiculous fiction, might be only an impostor, and that the exposure of this Koepenik Captain of the heavens, far from proving that there was no real captain, rather proved the contrary: that, in short, Nobodaddy could not have impersonated anybody if there had not been Somebodaddy to impersonate. We did not see the significance of the fact that on the last occasion on which God had been “expelled with a pitchfork,” men so different as Voltaire and Robespierre had said, the one that if God did not exist it would be necessary to invent him, and the other that after an honest attempt to dispense with a Supreme Being in practical politics, some such hypothesis had been found quite indispensable, and could not be replaced by a mere Goddess of Reason. If these two opinions were quoted at all, they were quoted as jokes at the expense of Nobodaddy. We were quite sure for the moment that whatever lingering superstition might have daunted these men of the eighteenth century, we Darwinians could do without God, and had made a good riddance of Him.

===Chinese===

可是物理学家们发现，他们对世界的理智视象，没法传给那些生来不曾拥有它的人。传到公众那里，它干脆就是唯物主义；而唯物主义一进入那场反对《圣经》拜物教的达尔文主义反动，便失掉了它特有的纯洁与尊严。两下合力，宗教被砸得粉碎：原先有上帝、有第一因、有“宇宙有其秩序——哪怕那秩序我们无法解释”的信仰、并因此有作为秩序一部分的道德责任感的地方，如今是一片空无。混沌又来了。头一阵效果是令人振奋的：我们有的是逃家孩子在挨饿、孤单、害怕之前的那种自由感。在这个阶段里，我们并不想把上帝要回来。我们印行威廉·布莱克——我们大诗人中最虔信宗教的一位——的诗，他把那拟人的偶像唤作“老没爸爸”，用种种字句讥诮他，那些字句害得排字工只得以空白留给我们去猜。我们听惯了牧师絮叨上帝不受嘲弄；如今尽兴嘲弄他而毫发无损，真是其乐无穷。我们没有想到：“老没爸爸”未必是一个可笑的虚构，他也许只是一名冒牌货；而这位天上的基彭尼克上尉被拆穿，远不能证明没有真正的船长，反倒证明了相反的一面：一句话，倘若没有一个“有爸爸”可供假冒，“没爸爸”就什么人也假冒不成。我们也没有看到下面这件事的意义：上次上帝“被粪叉赶走”的时候，伏尔泰和罗伯斯庇尔这样两个迥然不同的人，一个说，倘若上帝不存在，那就必须把他发明出来；另一个说，在实际政治里老老实实试过一番不要最高存在的日子之后，发现这样一个假设实在不可缺少，光靠一位理性女神顶不下来。这两句意见即使偶尔被引用，也只是当作取笑“没爸爸”的笑话。我们当时满有把握：不论十八世纪的这些人物还被什么残余的迷信吓住过，我们达尔文主义者没有上帝也过得去，甩掉他甩得干净利落。

### The Viceroys of the King of Kings / 万王之王的总督们

### The Viceroys of the King of Kings /

### The Viceroys of the King of Kings / 
===Original===

Now in politics it is much easier to do without God than to do without his viceroys and vicars and lieutenants; and we begin to miss the lieutenants long before we begin to miss their principal. Roman Catholics do what their confessors advise without troubling God; and Royalists are content to worship the King and ask the policeman. But God’s trustiest lieutenants often lack official credentials. They may be professed atheists who are also men of honor and high public spirit. The old belief that it matters dreadfully to God whether a man thinks himself an atheist or not, and that the extent to which it matters can be stated with exactness as one single damn, was an error: for the divinity is in the honor and public spirit, not in the mouthed *credo* or *non credo*. The consequences of this error became grave when the fitness of a man for public trust was tested, not by his honor and public spirit, but by asking him whether he believed in Nobodaddy or not. If he said yes, he was held fit to be a Prime Minister, though, as our ablest Churchman has said, the real implication was that he was either a fool, a bigot, or a liar. Darwin destroyed this test; but when it was only thoughtlessly dropped, there was no test at all; and the door to public trust was open to the man who had no sense of God because he had no sense of anything beyond his own business interests and personal appetites and ambitions. As a result, the people who did not feel in the least inconvenienced by being no longer governed by Nobodaddy soon found themselves very acutely inconvenienced by being governed by fools and commercial adventurers. They had forgotten not only God but Goldsmith, who had warned them that “honor sinks where commerce long prevails.”

===Chinese===

在政治上，没有上帝容易对付，没有他的总督们、代理们和副手们却难对付得多；我们开始想念那些副手，远在开始想念他们的首领之前。天主教徒照听忏悔神父的劝告行事，并不去麻烦上帝；保王党人拜国王、问警察，也就满足。可是上帝最可靠的副手们往往没有官方的委任状。他们可以是公然的无神论者，同时也是有荣誉感和高度公共精神的人。那种老信仰——以为一个人自认无神论者与否，对上帝是夭大的事，而且其轻重可以精确表述为“下地狱一回”——是一个错误：因为神性在荣誉和公共精神里，不在口头上的“我信”或“我不信”。这个错误的后果变得严重起来，是在考察一个人是否堪当公职时，不问他的荣誉感和公共精神，而问他信不信“没爸爸”。他若答“信”，便算够格当首相，虽然正如我们最能干的教会人士说过的，这真正的含义是：他不是傻瓜，便是顽固派，便是说谎者。达尔文毁掉了这道考察；可它一旦只是被人漫不经心地丢开，便什么考察也没有了；公职的大门便向这种人敞开：他对上帝毫无感觉，因为他对自家生意利益和个人欲望野心以外的一切都毫无感觉。结果，那些丝毫不因不再受“没爸爸”治理而感到不便的人们，很快发现自己大受其苦——他们受治理于蠢货和商业冒险家。他们忘掉的不仅是上帝，还有哥尔德斯密斯，他早警告过他们：“商风盛处，荣誉消沉。”

===Original===

The lieutenants of God are not always persons: some of them are legal and parliamentary fictions. One of them is Public Opinion. The pre-Darwinian statesmen and publicists were not restrained directly by God; but they restrained themselves by setting up an image of a Public Opinion which would not tolerate any attempt to tamper with British liberties. Their favorite way of putting it was that any Government which proposed such and such an infringement of such and such a British liberty would be hurled from office in a week. This was not true: there was no such public opinion, no limit to what the British people would put up with in the abstract, and no hardship short of immediate and sudden starvation that it would not and did not put up with in the concrete. But this very helplessness of the people had forced their rulers to pretend that they were not helpless, and that the certainty of a sturdy and unconquerable popular resistance forbade any trifling with Magna Carta or the Petition of Rights or the authority of parliament. Now the reality behind this fiction was the divine sense that liberty is a need vital to human growth. Accordingly, though it was difficult enough to effect a political reform, yet, once parliament had passed it, its wildest opponent had no hope that the Government would cancel it, or shelve it, or be bought off from executing it. From Walpole to Campbell-Bannerman there was no Prime Minister to whom such renagueing or trafficking would ever have occurred, though there were plenty who employed corruption unsparingly to procure the votes of members of parliament for their policy.

===Chinese===

上帝的副手们不总是人：有些是法律和议会里的虚构。其中之一便是舆论。达尔文之前的政治家和政治评论家们，并不直接受上帝约束；可是他们竖起一尊舆论的偶像来约束自己——那舆论容不得任何人染指不列颠的自由。他们最得意的说法是：哪一届政府胆敢如此这般侵犯如此这般的一项不列颠自由，不出一个星期就得被轰下台。这不是实话：这样的舆论并不存在；抽象地说，不列颠人民什么都忍得下；具体地说，只要不是立竿见影、猝不及防的饥饿，什么苦他们没有忍过、什么苦他们忍不下？可是人民这种全然的无告，反倒逼得统治者们假装人民并非无告，假装有一种坚不可摧的人民抵抗的确定性，禁止任何人拿《大宪章》《权利请愿书》或议会的权威开玩笑。而这套虚构背后的实在，是那种神圣的感觉：自由是人类成长的生命所需。因此，尽管推动一场政治改革已属不易，可是一旦议会通过了它，它最激烈的反对者也不指望政府会取消它、搁置它，或者受贿而不执行它。从沃波尔到坎贝尔—班纳曼，从没有哪位首相起过这等赖账或买卖的念头，尽管滥用贿赂去为自家的政策收买议员票数的首相大有人在。

### Political Opportunism in Excelsis / 登峰造极的政治机会主义

### Political Opportunism in Excelsis /

### Political Opportunism in Excelsis / 
===Original===

The moment Nobodaddy was slain by Darwin, Public Opinion, as divine deputy, lost its sanctity. Politicians no longer told themselves that the British public would never suffer this or that: they allowed themselves to know that for their own personal purposes, which are limited to their ten or twenty years on the front benches in parliament, the British public can be humbugged and coerced into believing and suffering everything that it pays to impose on them, and that any false excuse for an unpopular step will serve if it can be kept in countenance for a fortnight: that is, until the terms of the excuse are forgotten. The people, untaught or mistaught, are so ignorant and incapable politically that this in itself would not greatly matter; for a statesman who told them the truth would not be understood, and would in effect mislead them more completely than if he dealt with them according to their blindness instead of to his own wisdom. But though there is no difference in this respect between the best demagogue and the worst, both of them having to present their cases equally in terms of melodrama, there is all the difference in the world between the statesman who is humbugging the people into allowing him to do the will of God, in whatever disguise it may come to him, and one who is humbugging them into furthering his personal ambition and the commercial interests of the plutocrats who own the newspapers and support him on reciprocal terms. And there is almost as great a difference between the statesman who does this naively and automatically, or even does it telling himself that he is ambitious and selfish and unscrupulous, and the one who does it on principle, believing that if everyone takes the line of least material resistance the result will be the survival of the fittest in a perfectly harmonious universe. Once produce an atmosphere of fatalism on principle, and it matters little what the opinions or superstitions of the individual statesmen concerned may be.

===Chinese===

“没爸爸”一被达尔文杀死，舆论作为神圣代理便失去了它的圣性。政客们不再对自己念叨“不列颠公众绝容不得这容不得那”了；他们听凭自己知道：就他们个人的目的而言——那些目的不外乎他们在议会前座上的那十年二十年——不列颠公众是可以被蒙骗、被胁迫，去相信、去忍受一切值得强加给他们的东西的；不得人心的一步，随便找个什么假借口都行，只要这借口能撑过两个星期：也就是说，撑到借口的措辞被人忘掉。人民既没有受过教育、又受了错误的教育，在政治上如此无知、如此无能，以致这本身倒也无关紧要；因为对人民讲真话的政治家不会被理解，实际上反而比顺着人民的盲目行事——而不是照他自己的明智行事——更彻底地误导他们。可是，尽管在这方面最好的煽动家和最坏的煽动家没有分别——两人都得同样用情节剧的腔调陈述案情——一个把人民哄得容许他去做上帝的意志（不论那意志化作什么伪装来到他面前）的政治家，和一个把人民哄着去推进他的个人野心、去服务于拥有报纸并按互惠条件支持他的财阀们的商业利益的政治家之间，却有天壤之别。而天真地、机械地做这件事的政治家，甚至一面做一面对自己说“我野心、自私、不择手段”的政治家，同按原则做这件事的政治家——他相信只要人人都走物质阻力最小的路线，结果便是一个完美和谐的宇宙里的适者生存——之间，也差不多有同样大的分别。一旦按原则造出了宿命论的空气，那么当事的各位政治家个人抱什么意见或迷信，就无关紧要了。

===Original===

A Kaiser who is a devout reader of sermons, a Prime Minister who is an emotional singer of hymns, and a General who is a bigoted Roman Catholic may be the executants of the policy; but the policy itself will be one of unprincipled opportunism; and all the Governments will be like the tramp who walks always with the wind and ends as a pauper, or the stone that rolls down the hill and ends as an avalanche: their way is the way to destruction.

===Chinese===

一位把布道文当虔修读物的德皇，一位以唱圣歌动情的首相，一位信奉罗马天主教的偏执将军，都可以充当这种政策的执行人；可政策本身将是一种无原则的机会主义；而所有这些政府都将像那个永远顺着风走、最后沦为乞丐的流浪汉，像那块滚下山坡、最后滚成雪崩的石头：他们的路是通向毁灭的路。

### The Betrayal of Western Civilization / 西方文明的背叛

### The Betrayal of Western Civilization / 
===Original===

Within sixty years from the publication of Darwin’s *Origin of Species* political opportunism had brought parliaments into contempt; created a popular demand for direct action by the organized industries (“Syndicalism”); and wrecked the centre of Europe in a paroxysm of that chronic terror of one another, that cowardice of the irreligious, which, masked in the bravado of militarist patriotism, had ridden the Powers like a nightmare since the Franco-Prussian war of 1870-71. The sturdy old cosmopolitan Liberalism vanished almost unnoticed. At the present moment all the new ordinances for the government of our Grown Colonies contain, as a matter of course, prohibitions of all criticism, spoken or written, of their ruling officials, which would have scandalized George III and elicited Liberal pamphlets from Catherine II. Statesmen are afraid of the suburbs, of the newspapers, of the profiteers, of the diplomatists, of the militarists, of the country houses, of the trade unions, of everything ephemeral on earth except the revolutions they are provoking; and they would be afraid of these if they were not too ignorant of society and history to appreciate the risk, and to know that a revolution always seems hopeless and impossible the day before it breaks out, and indeed never does break out until it seems hopeless and impossible; for rulers who think it possible take care to insure the risk by ruling reasonably. This brings about a condition fatal to all political stability: namely, that you never know where to have the politicians. If the fear of God was in them it might be possible to come to some general understanding as to what God disapproves of; and Europe might pull together on that basis. But the present panic, in which Prime Ministers drift from election to election, either fighting or running away from everybody who shakes a fist at them, makes a European civilization impossible. Such peace and prosperity as we enjoyed before the war depended on the loyalty of the Western States to their own civilization. That loyalty could find practical expression only in an alliance of the highly civilized Western Powers against the primitive tyrannies of the East. Britain, Germany, France, and the United States of America could have imposed peace on the world, and nursed modern civilization in Russia, Turkey, and the Balkans.

===Chinese===

达尔文《物种起源》出版后不出六十年，政治机会主义已使各国议会声名扫地；激起了有组织产业直接行动的大众呼声（“工团主义”）；并在一阵慢性互相恐怖的痉挛里——那种无宗教者的怯懦，披着军国主义爱国心的大言不惭的外衣，自1870-71年普法战争以来就像一场噩梦骑在列强身上——把欧洲的中心砸了个稀烂。那老牌的世界主义自由主义，几乎无人察觉地消失了。眼下，我们各成熟殖民地的一切新政令，都理所当然地载有禁止一切口头的或书面的、针对其执政官员的批评的条款——这样的条款会叫乔治三世为之骇然，会引出叶卡捷琳娜二世的自由主义小册子。政治家们怕郊区、怕报纸、怕牟利者、怕外交官、怕军国主义者、怕乡间巨宅、怕工会，怕地上一切朝生暮死的东西，唯独不怕他们正在挑起的那几场革命；他们若不是对社会和历史无知到不知风险为何物、不知革命在爆发的前一天看来永远是无望而不可能的——而且非等到看来无望而不可能，革命也的确从不爆发——他们连革命也会怕的；因为觉得革命有可能的统治者，自会靠治理得合理来给这个风险上保险。这就造成了一种对一切政治稳定都是致命的局面：你永远摸不着政客的准头。倘若他们心里有对上帝的敬畏，也许还有可能就“上帝不赞成什么”达成某种普遍的谅解，欧洲也许还能以此为基础同心协力。可是眼下的这场恐慌——首相们从这次选举漂到那次选举，凡是冲他们挥拳头的，不是打就是逃——使欧洲文明成为不可能。我们战前享有的那点和平与繁荣，全靠西方各国对自己文明的忠诚。那种忠诚，只有落实为高度文明的西方列强结盟、共同对付东方的原始暴政，才有实际的表现。不列颠、德意志、法兰西和美利坚合众国，本可以合力把和平强加给世界，在俄罗斯、土耳其和巴尔干扶植起现代文明。

===Original===

Every meaner consideration should have given way to this need for the solidarity of the higher civilization. What actually happened was that France and England, through their clerks the diplomatists, made an alliance with Russia to defend themselves against Germany; Germany made an alliance with Turkey to defend herself against the three; and the two unnatural and suicidal combinations fell on one another in a war that came nearer to being a war of extermination than any wars since those of Timur the Tartar; whilst the United States held aloof as long as they could, and the other States either did the same or joined in the fray through compulsion, bribery, or their judgment as to which side their bread was buttered. And at the present moment, though the main fighting has ceased through the surrender of Germany on terms which the victors have never dreamt of observing, the extermination by blockade and famine, which was what forced Germany to surrender, still continues, although it is certain that if the vanquished starve the victors will starve too, and Europe will liquidate its affairs by going, not into bankruptcy, but into chaos.

Now all this, it will be noticed, was fundamentally nothing but an idiotic attempt on the part of each belligerent State to secure for itself the advantage of the survival of the fittest through Circumstantial Selection. If the Western Powers had selected their allies in the Lamarckian manner intelligently, purposely, and vitally, *ad majorem Dei gloriam*, as what Nietzsche called good Europeans, there would have been a League of Nations and no war. But because the selection relied on was purely circumstantial opportunist selection, so that the alliances were mere marriages of convenience, they have turned out, not merely as badly as might have been expected, but far worse than the blackest pessimist had ever imagined possible.

===Chinese===

一切次要的考虑，本都该让位给这种高等文明团结的需要。实际发生的却是：法兰西和英格兰经由它们的录事——外交官们——同俄罗斯结盟，防备德意志；德意志同土耳其结盟，防备那三家；这两桩违反常情、自寻死路的组合互相扑到一起，打了一场比鞑靼人帖木儿以来的任何战争都更接近灭绝战争的战争；美利坚合众国则能躲多久躲多久，其余各国不是照此办理，便是出于强迫、贿赂，或对“面包哪边抹黄油”的判断而加入厮杀。而眼下，虽然主要战事已因德意志投降而停止——投降的条件，战胜国做梦也没想过要遵守——那逼迫德意志投降的封锁与饥馑的灭绝却仍在继续，尽管事情明摆着：战败者挨饿，战胜者也得挨饿，欧洲了结它的家当，靠的不是破产，而是坠入混沌。

请注意，这一切归根结底，无非是每个交战国企图凭环境选择为自己捞到适者生存之利的一次蠢不可及的尝试。倘若西方列强当初照拉马克的方式——聪明地、有目的地、有生命地，为上帝更大的荣耀（ad majorem Dei gloriam），做尼采所谓“好欧洲人”——来挑选盟友，那早就有了国际联盟，也没有战争了。可是它们倚仗的选择纯粹是环境的机会主义选择，那些联盟不过是些便利的婚姻，于是结局不但坏得如其所料，而且坏得比最黑的悲观主义者所曾想象的还糟。

### Circumstantial Selection in Finance / 金融中的环境选择

### Circumstantial Selection in Finance / 
===Original===

How it will all end we do not yet know. When wolves combine to kill a horse, the death of the horse only sets them fighting one another for the choicest morsels. Men are no better than wolves if they have no better principles: accordingly, we find that the Armistice and the Treaty have not extricated us from the war. A handful of Serbian regicides flung us into it as a sporting navvy throws a bull pup at a cat; but the Supreme Council, with all its victorious legions and all its prestige, cannot get us out of it, though we are heartily sick and tired of the whole business, and know now very well that it should never have been allowed to happen. But we are helpless before a slate scrawled with figures of National Debts. As there is no money to pay them because it was all spent on the war (wars have to be paid for on the nail) the sensible thing to do is to wipe the slate and let the wrangling States distribute what they can spare, on the sound communist principle of from each according to his ability, to each according to his need. But no: we have no principles left, not even commercial ones; for what sane commercialist would decree that France must not pay for her failure to defend her own soil; that Germany must pay for her success in carrying the war into the enemy’s country; and that as Germany has not the money to pay, and under our commercial system can make it only by becoming once more a commercial competitor of England and France, which neither of them will allow, she must borrow the money from England, or America, or even from France: an arrangement by which the victorious creditors will pay one another, and wait to get their money back until Germany is either strong enough to refuse to pay or ruined beyond the possibility of paying? Meanwhile Russia, reduced to a scrap of fish and a pint of cabbage soup a day, has fallen into the hands of rulers who perceive that Materialist Communism is at all events more effective than Materialist Nihilism, and are attempting to move in an intelligent and ordered manner, practising a very strenuous Intentional Selection of workers as fitter to survive than idlers; whilst the Western Powers are drifting and colliding and running on the rocks, in the hope that if they continue to do their worst they will get Naturally Selected for survival without the trouble of thinking about it.

===Chinese===

这一切如何收场，我们还不知道。狼合伙杀死一匹马之后，马的死不过是叫它们为最肥的几块互相撕咬。人若没有更好的原则，并不比狼好：于是我们发现，停战协定和和约并没有把我们从战争里解救出来。一小撮塞尔维亚弑君者把我们扔进了战争，像个好玩的挖土工把一只小斗牛狗朝猫扔过去；可是最高会议，带着它全部的胜利军团和全部威望，却没法把我们弄出来，尽管我们对这整桩事早已恶心透顶、厌倦透顶，而且现在心里明白得很：当初根本不该容它发生。可是在一块涂满国债数字的石板上，我们毫无办法。既然钱都花在战争上了，没有钱来还（战争的账是必须当场付清的），那么聪明的办法就是把石板擦干净，让争吵的各国按健全的共产主义原则——各尽所能，按需分配——去分发它们拿得出的东西。可是不行：我们连原则都荡然无存了，连商业原则也没有了；因为哪个神志清醒的商业主义者会规定：法兰西未能保卫自己的国土，不必付账；德意志把战争打进了敌国，打了胜仗，倒必须付账；而德意志没有钱付，在我们的商业制度下，它只有重新成为英格兰和法兰西的商业竞争者才能挣出钱来——这两家又都不答应——于是它必须向英格兰、向美利坚、甚至向法兰西借这笔钱：这样一笔安排，胜利的债主们互相付钱，等着把钱收回，一直等到德意志要么强得拒付，要么烂得再无支付的可能？与此同时，俄罗斯，沦落到一天一小块鱼、一品脱白菜汤的地步，落进了这样一些统治者手里：他们看出唯物的共产主义无论如何比唯物的虚无主义更有效，正力图按聪明而有序的方式行动，极其起劲地实践一种有意选择——选劳动者，认为他们比游手好闲者更配生存；而西方列强却随波逐流、四处相撞、触礁搁浅，指望只要继续把最糟的事做绝，自会顺顺当当不用动脑子就被自然选择留下。

### The Homeopathic Reaction Against Darwinism / 对达尔文主义的顺势疗法式反动

### The Homeopathic Reaction Against Darwinism / 
===Original===

When, like the Russians, our Nihilists have it urgently borne in on them, by the brute force of rising wages that never overtake rising prices, that they are being Naturally Selected for destruction, they will perhaps remember that “Don’t Care came to a bad end,” and begin to look round for a religion. And the whole purpose of this book is to show them where to look. For, throughout all the godless welter of the infidel half-century, Darwinism has been acting not only directly but homeopathically, its poison rallying our vital forces not only to resist it and cast it out, but to achieve a new Reformation and put a credible and healthy religion in its place. Samuel Butler was the pioneer of the reaction as far as the casting out was concerned; but the issue was confused by the physiologists, who were divided on the question into Mechanists and Vitalists. The Mechanists said that life is nothing but physical and chemical action; that they have demonstrated this in many cases of so-called vital phenomena; and that there is no reason to doubt that with improved methods they will presently be able to demonstrate it in all of them. The Vitalists said that a dead body and a live one are physically and chemically identical, and that the difference can be accounted for only by the existence of a Vital Force. This seems simple; but the Anti-Mechanists objected to be called Vitalists (obviously the right name for them) on two contradictory grounds. First, that vitality is scientifically inadmissible, because it cannot be isolated and experimented with in the laboratory. Second, that force, being by definition anything that can alter the speed or direction of matter in motion (briefly, that can overcome inertia), is essentially a mechanistic conception. Here we had the New Vitalist only half extricated from the Old Mechanist, objecting to be called either, and unable to give a clear lead in the new direction. And there was a deeper antagonism. The Old Vitalists, in postulating a Vital Force, were setting up a comparatively mechanical conception as against the divine idea of the life breathed into the clay nostrils of Adam, whereby he became a living soul.

===Chinese===

等到有一天，我们的虚无主义者们也像俄国人那样，被那只涨价的工资永远追不上涨价的物价的蛮力，迫在眉睫地砸明白了他们正被自然选择选中去毁灭的时候，他们也许会记起“‘无所谓’没有好下场”，开始四下张望找一门宗教。而这本书的全部用意，就是指点他们往哪里看。因为，在这不信神的半个世纪那场无神的混沌当中，达尔文主义一直在起直接的作用，也在起顺势疗法的作用：它的毒把我们的生命力集结起来，不但去抵抗它、排出它，而且去成就一场新的宗教改革，把一门可信而健全的宗教放进它的位置。就“排出”这一层说，塞缪尔·巴特勒是这场反动的先驱；可是这问题又被生理学家们搅乱了，他们在这问题上分成了机械论者和活力论者。机械论者说，生命不过是物理和化学的作用；说他们已经在许多所谓的生命现象里证明了这一点；说没有理由怀疑，方法一改进，他们不久就能在一切现象里证明它。活力论者说，死尸和活尸在物理和化学上完全相同，其分别只能用存在一种活力来解释。这看来简单；可是反机械论者们却从两个互相矛盾的理由出发，拒绝人家叫他们活力论者（这明明白白是他们的正名）。第一，活力在科学上不可承认，因为它无法分离出来、无法在实验室里实验。第二，力，照定义是一切能改变运动中物质的速度或方向的东西（简言之，能克服惰性的东西），本质上是个机械论概念。于是我们看到，新活力论者只从旧机械论者身上挣脱了一半，既不肯叫这个，又不肯叫那个，也没法在新方向上给出一个明白的引领。而还有一层更深的对立。旧活力论者设定一种活力，等于竖起一个相对机械的概念，来对抗那个神圣的观念——生命之气吹进亚当的泥坯鼻孔，他就成了活的魂。

===Original===

The New Vitalists, filled by their laboratory researches with a sense of the miraculousness of life that went far beyond the comparatively uninformed imaginations of the authors of the Book of Genesis, regarded the Old Vitalists as Mechanists who had tried to fill up the gulf between life and death with an empty phrase denoting an imaginary physical force.

These professional faction fights are ephemeral, and need not trouble us here. The Old Vitalist, who was essentially a Materialist, has evolved into the New Vitalist, who is, as every genuine scientist must be, finally a metaphysician. And as the New Vitalist turns from the disputes of his youth to the future of his science, he will cease to boggle at the name Vitalist, or at the inevitable, ancient, popular, and quite correct use of the term Force to denote metaphysical as well as physical overcomers of inertia.

===Chinese===

新活力论者们，被他们的实验室研究灌满了一种生命感——其神异远远超出《创世记》的作者们那些相对孤陋的想象——把旧活力论者看成机械论者：他们曾试图用一句空话、一个虚构的物理力，把生死之间的鸿沟填平。

这些行帮门户之争朝生暮死，不必在此劳神。本质上原是唯物论者的旧活力论者，已经进化成新活力论者；而新活力论者，正如每一位真正的科学家所必须是的那样，归根结底是位形而上学者。等新活力论者从青年时代的争论转向他的科学的未来时，他就不会再对“活力论者”这个名目、对“力”这个词既古老又大众而且完全正当的用法——用它指称形而上学的、也指称物理的克服惰性者——疙疙瘴瘴了。

===Original===

Since the discovery of Evolution as the method of the Life Force the religion of metaphysical Vitalism has been gaining the definiteness and concreteness needed to make it assimilable by the educated critical man. But it has always been with us. The popular religions, disgraced by their Opportunist cardinals and bishops, have been kept in credit by canonized saints whose secret was their conception of themselves as the instruments and vehicles of divine power and aspiration: a conception which at moments becomes an actual experience of ecstatic possession by that power. And above and below all have been millions of humble and obscure persons, sometimes totally illiterate, sometimes unconscious of having any religion at all, sometimes believing in their simplicity that the gods and temples and priests of their district stood for their instinctive righteousness, who have kept sweet the tradition that good people follow a light that shines within and above and ahead of them, that bad people care only for themselves, and that the good are saved and blessed and the bad damned and miserable. Protestantism was a movement towards the pursuit of a light called an inner light because every man must see it with his own eyes and not take any priest’s word for it or any Church’s account of it. In short, there is no question of a new religion, but rather of redistilling the eternal spirit of religion and thus extricating it from the sludgy residue of temporalities and legends that are making belief impossible, though they are the stock-in-trade of all the Churches and all the Schools.

===Chinese===

自从发现进化是生命力的方法以来，形而上学的活力论的宗教一直在获得那种使受过教育的批判的人能够吸收它的确定性和具体性。其实它从来同我们同在。各大众宗教，被它们那些机会主义的红衣主教和主教们败坏了名声，却靠封了圣的圣徒们维持着信用——这些圣徒的秘密，在于他们把自己看作神圣力量与神圣愿望的工具和载体的那个观念：这观念在有些时刻会变成被那种力量狂喜地攫住的实际体验。而在这一切之上、之下，是千百万卑微无闻的人——有的全然不识字，有的意识不到自己有任何宗教，有的天真地以为本地的神祇、庙宇和祭司就代表着他们本能的正直——他们一直保全着那条甜美的传统：好人跟随一盏在他们里面、在他们上面、在他们前头照耀的光；坏人只顾自己；好人得救、蒙福，坏人沉沦、受苦。新教是一场朝着一盏光的追求的运动，那光叫作内在之光，因为每个人都必须亲眼看见它，而不是听信任何牧师的话、任何教会的说法。一句话，问题不在于一门新宗教，而在于把宗教的永恒精神重新蒸馏一遍，从而把它从那些俗务和传说的渣滓里解救出来——正是那些渣滓传说叫信仰变得不可能，虽然它们是一切教会、一切学派的家底。

### Religion and Romance / 宗教与传奇

### Religion and Romance / 
===Original===

It is the adulteration of religion by the romance of miracles and paradises and torture chambers that makes it reel at the impact of every advance in science, instead of being clarified by it. If you take an English village lad, and teach him that religion means believing that the stories of Noah’s Ark and the Garden of Eden are literally true on the authority of God himself, and if that boy becomes an artisan and goes into the town among the sceptical city proletariat, then, when the jibes of his mates set him thinking, and he sees that these stories cannot be literally true, and learns that no candid prelate now pretends to believe them, he does not make any fine distinctions: he declares at once that religion is a fraud, and parsons and teachers hypocrites and liars. He becomes indifferent to religion if he has little conscience, and indignantly hostile to it if he has a good deal.

The same revolt against wantonly false teaching is happening daily in the professional classes whose recreation is reading and whose intellectual sport is controversy. They banish the Bible from their houses, and sometimes put into the hands of their unfortunate children Ethical and Rationalist tracts of the deadliest dullness, compelling these wretched infants to sit out the discourses of Secularist lecturers (I have delivered some of them myself), who bore them at a length now forbidden by custom in the established pulpit. Our minds have reacted so violently towards provable logical theorems and demonstrable mechanical or chemical facts that we have become incapable of metaphysical truth, and try to cast out incredible and silly lies by credible and clever ones, calling in Satan to cast out Satan, and getting more into his clutches than ever in the process. Thus the world is kept sane less by the saints than by the vast mass of the indifferent, who neither act nor react in the matter. Butler’s preaching of the gospel of Laodicea was a piece of common sense founded on his observation of this.

===Chinese===

使宗教在科学的每一次冲击面前摇晃、而不是被那冲击澄清的，正是宗教里掺进的神迹、天堂和刑讯室的故事。你带一个英国乡村小伙子，教他宗教就是相信挪亚方舟和伊甸园的故事凭着上帝自己的权威字字属实；等这孩子当了工匠进了城，混在多疑的城市无产者中间，同伴们的嘲笑引得他一想，看出这些故事不可能字字属实，又听说如今没有一位坦率的主教还装作相信它们，他是不作什么精细区分的：他立刻宣布宗教是骗局，牧师和教师是伪君子和说谎者。他良心少，便对宗教冷淡；良心多，便对宗教愤然敌视。

同样这种对恣意妄为的谬误教导的反叛，每天都在以读书为消遣、以论战为智识运动的职业阶层里发生。他们把《圣经》逐出家门，有时还把枯燥得要死的伦理会和理性会的小册子塞进他们不幸的孩子们手里，逼这些可怜的小东西坐听完世俗主义讲演者的长篇大论（有的还是我做的），那长度如今连国教讲坛上的惯例都不容许了。我们的头脑向可证明的逻辑定理和可演示的机械或化学事实反弹得那样猛烈，以至于我们已经无能接受形而上学的真理，只会用可信而聪明的谎言去驱逐不可信而愚蠢的谎言，召撒旦来赶撒旦，结果在撒旦的爪子里陷得比先前更深。就这样，维系这世界神智清醒的，与其说是圣徒们，不如说是那一大群对此既不行动也不反应的冷漠者。巴特勒宣讲的老底嘉福音，正是建立在他对这一层的观察上的一篇常识。

===Original===

But indifference will not guide nations through civilization to the establishment of the perfect city of God. An indifferent statesman is a contradiction in terms; and a statesman who is indifferent on principle, a Laisser-faire or Muddle-Through doctrinaire, plays the deuce with us in the long run. Our statesmen must get a religion by hook or crook; and as we are committed to Adult Suffrage it must be a religion capable of vulgarization. The thought first put into words by the Mills when they said “There is no God; but this is a family secret,” and long held unspoken by aristocratic statesmen and diplomatists, will not serve now; for the revival of civilization after the war cannot be effected by artificial breathing: the driving force of an undeluded popular consent is indispensable, and will be impossible until the statesman can appeal to the vital instincts of the people in terms of a common religion. The success of the Hang the Kaiser cry at the last General Election shows us very terrifyingly how a common irreligion can be used by myopic demagogy; and common irreligion will destroy civilization unless it is countered by common religion.

===Chinese===

可是冷漠不能引导各个民族穿过文明、到达上帝完美之城的建立。一个冷漠的政治家是个自相矛盾的说法；而一个原则上冷漠的政治家，一个放任主义或混过去主义的教条家，迟早要跟我们捣大乱。我们的政治家必须想方设法弄到一门宗教；既然我们已投身于成人普选，它就必须是一门能够俗化的宗教。穆勒父子当年用话语首先道出的那个念头——“没有上帝；但这是家内的秘密”——以及贵族政治家和外交官们长期缄口持守的同一念头，如今行不通了；因为战后的文明复兴不可能靠人工呼吸来完成：一种不受蒙蔽的大众同意的驱动力是不可缺少的，而只要政治家还不能以一种共同宗教的语言诉诸人民的生命本能，它就不可能出现。上次大选里“吊死德皇”那一嗓子的大获成功，极其吓人地向我们显示了一种共同的非宗教如何能被近视的煽动家利用；而共同的非宗教将毁灭文明，除非用共同的宗教来对付它。

### The Danger of Reaction / 反动的危险

### The Danger of Reaction / 
===Original===

And here arises the danger that when we realize this we shall do just what we did half a century ago, and what Pliable did in *The Pilgrim’s Progress* when Christian landed him in the Slough of Despond: that is, run back in terror to our old superstitions. We jumped out of the frying-pan into the fire; and we are just as likely to jump back again, now that we feel hotter than ever. History records very little in the way of mental activity on the part of the mass of mankind except a series of stampedes from affirmative errors into negative ones and back again. It must therefore be said very precisely and clearly that the bankruptcy of Darwinism does not mean that Nobodaddy was Somebodaddy *with* “body, parts, and passions” after all; that the world was made in the year 4004 BC; that damnation means a eternity of blazing brimstone; that the Immaculate Conception means that sex is sinful and that Christ was parthenogenetically brought forth by a virgin descended in like manner from a line of virgins right back to Eve; that the Trinity is an anthropomorphic monster with three heads which are yet only one head; that in Rome the bread and wine on the altar become flesh and blood, and in England, in a still more mystical manner, they do and they do not; that the Bible is an infallible scientific manual, an accurate historical chronicle, and a complete guide to conduct; that we may lie and cheat and murder and then wash ourselves innocent in the blood of the lamb on Sunday at the cost of a credo and a penny in the plate, and so on and so forth. Civilization cannot be saved by people not only crude enough to believe these things, but irreligious enough to believe that such belief constitutes a religion. The education of children cannot safely be left in their hands. If dwindling sects like the Church of England, the Church of Rome, the Greek Church, and the rest, persist in trying to cramp the human mind within the limits of these grotesque perversions of natural truths and poetic metaphors, then they must be ruthlessly banished from the schools until they either perish in general contempt or discover the soul that is hidden in every dogma. The real Class War will be a war of intellectual classes; and its conquest will be the souls of the children.

===Chinese===

而这里就生出一种危险：当我们明白这一层时，我们会恰恰重演半个世纪前的所为，重演《天路历程》里“柔顺”的所为——基督徒把他带进了绝望泥潭，他便吓得跑了回去：也就是说，恐怖地跑回我们旧的迷信里去。我们从煎锅跳进了火里；如今我们觉得比先前更烫，也就同样可能再跳回去。历史记载的人类大众的心智活动寥寥，除了一连串从肯定的错误窜进否定的错误、又从否定的错误窜回肯定的错误的奔逃。因此必须说得非常确切、非常明白：达尔文主义的破产，并不意味着“没爸爸”到底还是那位“有爸爸”，当真有“身体、部分和激情”；并不意味着世界造于公元前4004年；并不意味着下地狱就是在硫磺烈火里永世烧烤；并不意味着圣母无染成胎意味着性是有罪的、基督是由一位童贞女单性生出、而她同样由一系童贞女一路单性追溯到夏娃；并不意味着三位一体是一个长着三颗头、三颗头又只是一颗头的拟人怪物；并不意味着在罗马，祭坛上的饼和酒变成肉和血，而在英格兰，以更神秘的方式，它们既变成了又没变成；并不意味着《圣经》是一部绝无谬误的科学手册、一部精确的编年史、一部完备的行为指南；并不意味着我们可以撒谎、欺骗、杀人，然后在礼拜天花上一句信经、盘里一枚便士，在羔羊的血里把自己洗成清白之身，如此等等。文明救不了自己，如果指望的人不但粗陋到相信这些东西，而且非宗教到以为这种相信就构成一门宗教。儿童的教育不能放心交在他们手里。倘若英国国教会、罗马教会、希腊教会等等这些日见萎缩的宗派，硬要继续把人的头脑禁锢在这些对自然真理和诗性比喻的荒唐歪曲之内，那就必须无情地把它们逐出学校，直到它们或者在普遍的鄙夷里灭亡，或者发现在每一条教义里藏着的那颗灵魂。真正的阶级战争将是一场智识阶级的战争；它要征服的，是孩子们的灵魂。

### A Touchstone for Dogma / 教义的试金石

### A Touchstone for Dogma / 
===Original===

The test of a dogma is its universality. As long as the Church of England preaches a single doctrine that the Brahman, the Buddhist, the Mussulman, the Parsee, and all the other sectarians who are British subjects cannot accept, it has no legitimate place in the counsels of the British Commonwealth, and will remain what it is at present, a corrupter of youth, a danger to the State, and an obstruction to the Fellowship of the Holy Ghost. This has never been more strongly felt than at present, after a war in which the Church failed grossly in the courage of its profession, and sold its lilies for the laurels of the soldiers of the Victoria Cross. All the cocks in Christendom have been crowing shame on it ever since; and it will not be spared for the sake of the two or three faithful who were found even among the bishops. Let the Church take it on authority, even my authority (as a professional legend maker) if it cannot see the truth by its own light: no dogma can be a legend. A legend can pass an ethnical frontier as a legend, but not as a truth; whilst the only frontier to the currency of a sound dogma as such is the frontier of capacity for understanding it.

===Chinese===

检验一条教义的标准是它的普遍性。只要英国国教会还宣讲哪怕一条婆罗门教徒、佛教徒、穆斯林、帕西教徒以及所有身为不列颠臣民的其他宗派教徒所不能接受的教义，它在不列颠联邦的议事机构里就没有合法的位置，并且将继续是它今天这个样子：腐蚀青年的人、国家的危险、圣灵团契的障碍。这一层，从来不像现在这样被痛感受到——一场战争刚刚过去，教会在其职业的勇气上惨败，把它的百合花卖给了维多利亚十字勋章军人的桂冠。自那以后，基督教世界所有的公鸡一直对着它啼叫羞耻；为了连主教们当中也找得出的那两三个忠信者，它也得不到宽赦。让教会凭权威接受这一点吧——倘若它不能凭自己的光看见真理，哪怕凭我的权威（作为一个职业造传奇的人）：没有一条教义能是传奇。传奇可以以传奇的身份跨过族群的边界，却不能以真理的身份；而一条健全教义的通行界限，只在理解它的能力的界限。

===Original===

This does not mean that we should throw away legend and parable and drama: they are the natural vehicles of dogma; but woe to the Churches and rulers who substitute the legend for the dogma, the parable for the history, the drama for the religion! Better by far declare the throne of God empty than set a liar and a fool on it. What are called wars of religion are always wars to destroy religion by affirming the historical truth or material substantiality of some legend, and killing those who refuse to accept it as historical or substantial. But who has ever refused to accept a good legend with delight as a legend? The legends, the parables, the dramas, are among the choicest treasures of mankind. No one is ever tired of stories of miracles. In vain did Muhammad repudiate the miracles ascribed to him: in vain did Christ furiously scold those who asked him to give them an exhibition as a conjurer: in vain did the saints declare that God chose them not for their powers but for their weaknesses; that the humble might be exalted, and the proud rebuked. People will have their miracles, their stories, their heroes and heroines and saints and martyrs and divinities to exercise their gifts of affection, admiration, wonder, and worship, and their Judases and devils to enable them to be angry and yet feel that they do well to be angry. Every one of these legends is the common heritage of the human race; and there is only one inexorable condition attached to their healthy enjoyment, which is that no one shall believe them literally. The reading of stories and delighting in them made Don Quixote a gentleman: the believing them literally made him a madman who slew lambs instead of feeding them. In England today good books of Eastern religious legends are read eagerly; and Protestants and Atheists read Roman Catholic legends of the Saints with pleasure. But such fare is shirked by Indians and Roman Catholics. Freethinkers read the Bible: indeed they seem to be its only readers now except the reluctant parsons at the church lecterns, who communicate their discomfort to the congregation by gargling the words in their throats in an unnatural manner that is as repulsive as it is unintelligible. And this is because the imposition of the legends as literal truths at once changes them from parables into falsehoods.

===Chinese===

这并不是说我们该把传奇、寓言和戏剧扔掉：它们是教义的天然载体；可是那些用传奇顶替教义、用寓言顶替历史、用戏剧顶替宗教的教会和统治者们有祸了！宁可宣布上帝的宝座空着，也远胜于在宝座上安一个说谎者和傻子。所谓的宗教战争，从来都是靠断言某个传奇的历史真实性或物质实有性来毁灭宗教的战争：谁拒绝把它当作历史或实物接受，就杀掉谁。可是谁曾拒绝过满怀喜悦地把一个好传奇当作传奇接受呢？那些传奇、寓言、戏剧，是人类最精选的财宝的一部分。神迹的故事谁也听不厌。穆罕默德否认人家安在他头上的神迹，白费劲；基督厉声斥责那些要他像变戏法似的表演一番的人，白费劲；圣徒们声明上帝拣选他们不是因为他们的大能而是因为他们的软弱、叫谦卑者升高而骄傲者受责，也白费劲。人们要他们的神迹、他们的故事、他们的男女英雄、圣徒、殉道者和神祇，好施展他们爱慕、惊叹、敬拜的天赋；也要他们的犹大们和魔鬼们，好让他们发怒而又自觉发怒发得有理。这些传奇，每一条都是全人类的共同遗产；对它们的健康享用，只附带一个不容通融的条件：谁也不许按字面相信它们。读故事、喜爱故事，把堂吉诃德造就成一位绅士；按字面相信它们，把他造就成一个宰杀羔羊而不是喂养羔羊的疯子。今天的英格兰，东方宗教传奇的好书人人争读；新教徒和无神论者也津津有味地读罗马天主教的圣徒传奇。倒是从不缺这类读物的印度人和天主教徒把它们撇在一边。自由思想家们读《圣经》：如今看来，除了讲经台上那些勉为其难的牧师，他们像是它仅有的读者了——那些牧师用一种不自然的腔调在喉咙里含混地嗡噜经文，把他们的不自在传染给会众，既叫人厌恶，又不知所云。这是因为：把传奇当作字面的真理来强加，立刻就把它们从寓言变成了谎言。

===Original===

The feeling against the Bible has become so strong at last that educated people not only refuse to outrage their intellectual consciences by reading the legend of Noah’s Ark, with its funny beginning about the animals and its exquisite end about the birds: they will not read even the chronicles of King David, which may very well be true, and are certainly more candid than the official biographies of our contemporary monarchs.

===Chinese===

对《圣经》的反感，最后竟强烈到这个地步：受过教育的人们不但拒绝亵渎自己的理智良心去读挪亚方舟的故事——它那讲动物的滑稽开头，讲鸟儿的妙极结尾——连大卫王的编年史也不肯读了，而那些记载很可能属实，而且肯定比我们当代君主们的官修传记坦率。

### What to Do with the Legends / 怎样处置诸传奇

### What to Do with the Legends / 
===Original===

What we should do, then, is to pool our legends and make a delightful stock of religious folklore on an honest basis for all mankind. With our minds freed from pretence and falsehood we could enter into the heritage of all the faiths. China would share her sages with Spain, and Spain her saints with China. The Ulster man who now gives his son an unmerciful thrashing if the boy is so tactless as to ask how the evening and the morning could be the first day before the sun was created, or to betray an innocent calf-love for the Virgin Mary, would buy him a bookful of legends of the creation and of mothers of God from all parts of the world, and be very glad to find his laddie as interested in such things as in marbles or Police and Robbers. That would be better than beating all good feeling towards religion out of the child, and blackening his mind by teaching him that the worshippers of the holy virgins, whether of the Parthenon or St. Peter’s, are fire-doomed heathens and idolaters. All the sweetness of religion is conveyed to the world by the hands of storytellers and image-makers. Without their fictions the truths of religion would for the multitude be neither intelligible nor even apprehensible; and the prophets would prophesy and the teachers teach in vain. And nothing stands between the people and the fictions except the silly falsehood that the fictions are literal truths, and that there is nothing in religion but fiction.

===Chinese===

那么，我们该做的，是把我们的传奇汇成一池，在诚实的基础上为全人类攒下一批可爱的宗教民间宝库。头脑一旦从虚伪和谎言里解放出来，我们就能进入一切信仰的遗产。中国会把她的圣贤分给西班牙，西班牙会把她的圣徒分给中国。阿尔斯特人如今要是碰上儿子不知轻重，问起太阳还没造出来之前，晚上和早晨怎么能是头一日；或是露出对童贞马利亚的一段天真初恋——便要把他往死里打；到那时，他却会给儿子买上一大本世界各地的创世传奇和上帝之母们的传奇，而且会十分高兴地发现，他家小子对这些东西的兴趣，同对弹珠或官兵捉强盗的兴趣一样大。这总好过把孩子身上一切对宗教的好感打光，好过教他相信：圣洁童贞女的崇拜者们，不论在帕特农庙还是在圣彼得堂，都是注定下火狱的蛮人和拜偶像者。宗教的一切甜美，都是靠讲故事的人和造像人的手传递给世界的。没有他们的虚构，宗教的真理对大众就既不可理解、甚至不可感受；先知将白白预言，教师将白白施教。而横在人民和这些虚构之间的，只有那条愚蠢的谎话：虚构是字面的真理，宗教里除了虚构一无所有。

### A Lesson from Science to the Churches / 科学给各教会的一课
===Original===

Let the Churches ask themselves why there is no revolt against the dogmas of mathematics though there is one against the dogmas of religion. It is not that the mathematical dogmas are more comprehensible. The law of inverse squares is as incomprehensible to the common man as the Athanasian creed. It is not that science is free from legends, witchcraft, miracles, biographic boostings of quacks as heroes and saints, and of barren scoundrels as explorers and discoverers. On the contrary, the iconography and hagiology of Scientism are as copious as they are mostly squalid. But no student of science has yet been taught that specific gravity consists in the belief that Archimedes jumped out of his bath and ran naked through the streets of Syracuse shouting Eureka, Eureka, or that the law of inverse squares must be discarded if anyone can prove that Newton was never in an orchard in his life. When some unusually conscientious or enterprising bacteriologist reads the pamphlets of Jenner, and discovers that they might have been written by an ignorant but curious and observant nurserymaid, and could not possibly have been written by any person with a scientifically trained mind, he does not feel that the whole edifice of science has collapsed and crumbled, and that there is no such thing as smallpox. It may come to that yet; for hygiene, as it forces its way into our schools, is being taught as falsely as religion is taught there; but in mathematics and physics the faith is still kept pure, and you may take the law and leave the legends without suspicion of heresy. Accordingly, the tower of the mathematician stands unshaken whilst the temple of the priest rocks to its foundation.

===Chinese===

让各教会问问自己：为什么数学的教条无人反叛，而宗教的教条有人反叛。并不是数学的教条更容易懂。平方反比律在常人看来，同亚他那修信经一样不可索解。也不是科学里没有传奇、巫术、神迹，没有把骗子吹成英雄圣徒、把不毛的恶棍捧成探险家发现家的传记贴金。恰恰相反，科学主义的造像学和圣徒传，数量既多，大多又寒碜。可是从来没有一个学科学的学生被教导说：比重就在于相信阿基米德跳出浴盆、赤身裸体跑过叙拉古的街道喊“尤里卡，尤里卡”；也没有人教导说：只要有人能证明牛顿一辈子没进过果园，平方反比律就必须作废。当某位特别较真或特别有闯劲的细菌学家读了詹纳的小册子，发现它们尽可以出自一个无知却好奇而善观察的保姆之手，而绝不可能出自任何受过科学训练的头脑，他并不因此觉得整座科学大厦轰然倒塌、天花病从此不存在。也许有一天会到那步田地；因为卫生学正随着它挤进我们的学校，被教得同那里的宗教一样假；但在数学和物理里，信仰仍保持纯洁，你可以取律法而舍传奇，而不蒙异端的嫌疑。于是，数学家的塔岿然不动，而祭司的庙连根基都在摇晃。

### The Religious Art of the Twentieth Century / 二十世纪的宗教艺术
===Original===

Creative Evolution is already a religion, and is indeed now unmistakeably the religion of the twentieth century, newly arisen from the ashes of pseudo-Christianity, of mere scepticism, and of the soulless affirmations and blind negations of the Mechanists and Neo-Darwinians. But it cannot become a popular religion until it has its legends, its parables, its miracles. And when I say popular I do not mean apprehensible by villagers only. I mean apprehensible by Cabinet Ministers as well. It is unreasonable to look to the professional politician and administrator for light and leading in religion. He is neither a philosopher nor a prophet: if he were, he would be philosophizing and prophesying, and not neglecting both for the drudgery of practical government. Socrates and Coleridge did not remain soldiers, nor could John Stuart Mill remain the representative of Westminster in the House of Commons even when he was willing. The Westminster electors admired Mill for telling them that much of the difficulty of dealing with them arose from their being inveterate liars. But they would not vote a second time for the man who was not afraid to break the crust of mendacity on which they were all dancing; for it seemed to them that there was a volcanic abyss beneath, not having his philosophic conviction that the truth is the solidest standing ground in the end. Your front bench man will always be an exploiter of the popular religion or irreligion. Not being an expert, he must take it as he finds it; and before he can take it, he must have been told stories about it in his childhood and had before him all his life an elaborate iconography of it produced by writers, painters, sculptors, temple architects, and artists of all the higher sorts. Even if, as sometimes happens, he is a bit of an amateur in metaphysics as well as a professional politician, he must still govern according to the popular iconography, and not according to his own personal interpretations if these happen to be heterodox.

===Chinese===

创造进化已经是一门宗教，而且的确明白无误地已是二十世纪的宗教，从伪基督教的灰烬里、从单纯的怀疑主义里、从机械论者和新达尔文主义者那些无灵魂的断言和盲目的否定里新生出来。可是在它有了自己的传奇、寓言、神迹之前，它成不了大众宗教。我说大众，不光是说叫村民能懂。我是说也得叫内阁大臣们能懂。指望职业政治家和行政官在宗教上给光给引领，是没有道理的。他既不是哲学家，也不是先知：他若是，他就去哲学思辨、去说预言了，不会为了实际政务的苦差把两样都丢下。苏格拉底和柯尔律治没有继续当兵；约翰·斯图亚特·穆勒哪怕自己愿意，也做不成威斯敏斯特在下议院的代表。威斯敏斯特的选民们佩服穆勒，因为他告诉他们：同他们打交道的种种难处，多半起于他们是一伙积习难改的说谎者。可是他们不肯第二次投那个人的票——那个人不怕踩破他们大家都在上面跳舞的那层谎言之壳；因为在他们看来，壳底下是火山口般的深渊，而不像他那样坚信真理终归是最坚实的立足之地。你的前座人永远是对大众宗教或大众非宗教的利用者。他既非专家，便只能有什么用什么；而在他能用之前，他必须从小听过关于它的故事，一生面前摆着由作家、画家、雕塑家、庙宇建筑师和一切高等艺术家们造出的关于它的一整套图像。哪怕他——这有时也有——在形而上学上是个票友，同时又是职业政治家，他也还是得照大众的图像行事，而不能照他自己的解释行事，倘若那些解释碰巧是异端的话。

===Original===

It will be seen then that the revival of religion on a scientific basis does not mean the death of art, but a glorious rebirth of it. Indeed art has never been great when it was not providing an iconography for a live religion. And it has never been quite contemptible except when imitating the iconography after the religion had become a superstition. Italian painting from Giotto to Carpaccio is all religious painting; and it moves us deeply and has real greatness. Compare with it the attempts of our painters a century ago to achieve the effects of the old masters by imitation when they should have been illustrating a faith of their own. Contemplate, if you can bear it, the dull daubs of Hilton and Haydon, who knew so much more about drawing and scumbling and glazing and perspective and anatomy and “marvellous foreshortening” than Giotto, the latchet of whose shoe they were nevertheless not worthy to unloose. Compare Mozart’s *Magic Flute*, Beethoven’s *Ninth Symphony*, Wagner’s *Ring*, all of them reachings-forward to the new Vitalist art, with the dreary pseudo-sacred oratorios and cantatas which were produced for no better reason than that Handel had formerly made splendid thunder in that way, and with the stale confectionery, mostly too would-be pious to be even cheerfully toothsome, of Spohr and Mendelssohn, Stainer and Parry, which spread indigestion at our musical festivals until I publicly told Parry the bludgeoning truth about his *Job* and woke him to conviction of sin. Compare Flaxman and Thorwaldsen and Gibson with Phidias and Praxiteles, Stevens with Michelangelo, Bouguereau’s *Virgin* with Cimabue’s, or the best operatic Christs of Scheffer and Müller with the worst Christs that the worst painters could paint before the end of the fifteenth century, and you must feel that until we have a great religious movement we cannot hope for a great artistic one. The disillusioned Raphael could paint a mother and child, but not a queen of Heaven as much less skilful men had done in the days of his great-grandfather; yet he could reach forward to the twentieth century and paint a *Transfiguration of the Son of Man* as they could not.

===Chinese===

由此可见，宗教在科学基础上的复兴，并不意味着艺术的死亡，而是艺术一场光荣的再生。的确，艺术只有在为活宗教提供图像的时候，才曾经伟大。而它只有在宗教已成迷信之后模仿那套图像的时候，才曾经真正可鄙。从乔托到卡帕齐奥的意大利绘画全是宗教绘画；它深深感动我们，具有真正的伟大。拿我们一百年前的画家们比一比：他们本该图解一门自家的信仰，却去靠模仿追求旧大师的效果。你若受得了，就端详端详希尔顿和海登那些沉闷的涂鸦吧：论素描、罩染、上釉、透视、解剖和“精妙的缩短法”，他们比乔托懂得多得多，却连解他鞋带的鞋扣儿都不配。拿莫扎特的《魔笛》、贝多芬的《第九交响曲》、瓦格纳的《指环》——全是朝新活力论艺术的伸探——比一比那些乏味的伪圣神剧和康塔塔：写它们不为别的，只因为韩德尔从前用这种法子打出过漂亮的雷；再比一比斯波尔和门德尔松、斯坦纳和帕里那些走味的甜点心——大多又太想虔诚，连可口都谈不上——它们在我们的音乐节上散布消化不良，直到我当众对帕里说出他那《约伯》的棒喝真话，唤起他对罪的觉悟。拿弗拉克斯曼和托瓦尔森和吉布森比菲狄亚斯和普拉克西特列斯，拿史蒂文斯比米开朗琪罗，拿布格罗的《圣母》比契马布埃的，拿舍弗尔和米勒的最好的歌剧基督们，比十五世纪末以前最糟的画家画得出的最糟的基督，你就必然感到：没有一场伟大的宗教运动，我们便休想有一场伟大的艺术运动。幻灭了的拉斐尔画得出一位圣母圣子，却画不出一位天后，而他技巧差得多的前人们在曾祖父的年代里都画得出；然而他能够伸向前二十世纪，画出一幅《人子变容》，那是他们画不出的。

===Original===

Also, please note, he could decorate a house of pleasure for a cardinal very beautifully with voluptuous pictures of Cupid and Psyche; for this simple sort of Vitalism is always with us, and, like portrait painting, keeps the artist supplied with subject-matter in the intervals between the ages of faith; so that your sceptical Rembrandts and Velasquezs are at least not compelled to paint shop fronts for want of anything else to paint in which they can really believe.

===Chinese===

还有，请注意，他能为一位红衣主教装饰一所行乐之宅，用丘比特与普赛克的艳情图画装点得美妙极了；因为这种简单的活力主义从来同我们同在，而且像肖像画一样，在信仰时代的间歇里给艺术家供应着题材；于是你那些怀疑派的伦勃朗和委拉斯开兹们，至少不至于因为画不出真正能信的东西，就被逼得去画铺面。

### The Artist-Prophets / 艺术家—先知们
===Original===

And there are always certain rare but intensely interesting anticipations. Michelangelo could not very well believe in Julius II or Leo X, or in much that they believed in; but he could paint the Superman three hundred years before Nietzsche wrote *Also Sprach Zarathustra* and Strauss set it to music. Michelangelo won the primacy among all modern painters and sculptors solely by his power of showing us superhuman persons. On the strength of his decoration and color alone he would hardly have survived his own death twenty years; and even his design would have had only an academic interest; but as a painter of prophets and sibyls he is greatest among the very greatest in his craft, because we aspire to a world of prophets and sibyls. Beethoven never heard of radioactivity nor of electrons dancing in vortices of inconceivable energy; but pray can anyone explain the last movement of his *Hammerklavier Sonata*, Opus 106, otherwise than as a musical picture of these whirling electrons? His contemporaries said he was mad, partly perhaps because the movement was so hard to play; but we, who can make a pianola play it to us over and over until it is as familiar as “Pop Goes the Weasel,” know that it is sane and methodical. As such, it must represent something; and as all Beethoven’s serious compositions represent some process within himself, some nerve storm or soul storm, and the storm here is clearly one of physical movement, I should much like to know what other storm than the atomic storm could have driven him to this oddest of all those many expressions of cyclonic energy which have given him the same distinction among musicians that Michelangelo has among draughtsmen.

===Chinese===

而总是有一些稀罕却极其耐人寻味的预言。米开朗琪罗很难信尤利乌斯二世或利奥十世，也很难信他们信的许多东西；可他能在尼采写出《查拉图斯特拉如是说》、施特劳斯把它谱成曲之前三百年，就画出了超人。米开朗琪罗在一切现代画家雕塑家中独占魁首，全凭他让我们看见超人人物的本领。单凭他的装饰和色彩，他死后怕连二十年都撑不过去；就连他的素描，也只剩学院派的趣味；可是作为先知和西比尔的画家，他在他那行里最伟大者当中也是头一等，因为我们向往一个先知和西比尔的世界。贝多芬从没听说过放射性，也没听说过电子在不可思议的能量漩涡里跳舞；可是请问，除了把这画成一幅这些回旋电子的音乐图画，谁能解释他《槌子键琴奏鸣曲》（作品第106号）的末乐章？他的同代人说他是疯了，一部分也许因为那个乐章难弹；可是我们这些能让自动钢琴替我们一遍遍弹到它同《砰！黄鼠狼跑了》一样熟的人，知道它神志清楚、条理分明。既然如此，它必定表现着什么；而贝多芬一切严肃的作品都表现他自己内部的某种过程、某种神经风暴或灵魂风暴，这里的风暴又明明白白是一场物理运动的风暴，我很想知道，除了原子风暴，还有什么风暴能把他驱赶到这许许多多旋风式能量的表达中最古怪的一种面前——正是这些表达，使他在音乐家当中享有米开朗琪罗在素描家当中的那种地位。

===Original===

In Beethoven’s day the business of art was held to be “the sublime and beautiful.” In our day it has fallen to be the imitative and voluptuous. In both periods the word passionate has been freely employed; but in the eighteenth century passion meant irresistible impulse of the loftiest kind: for example, a passion for astronomy or for truth. For us it has come to mean concupiscence and nothing else. One might say to the art of Europe what Antony said to the corpse of Caesar: “Are all thy conquests, glories, triumphs, spoils, shrunk to this little measure?” But in fact it is the mind of Europe that has shrunk, being, as we have seen, wholly preoccupied with a busy spring-cleaning to get rid of its superstitions before readjusting itself to the new conception of Evolution.

===Chinese===

在贝多芬的时代，艺术的职分被认为就是“崇高与美”。到了我们这个时代，它沦落为模仿与艳情。两个时代里，“热情”这个字眼都随意使用；但在十八世纪，热情指最高一类不可抗拒的冲动：比如对天文学或对真理的热情。在我们，它已经只指情欲，别无其他。对欧洲的艺术，人们尽可以说安东尼对着凯撒尸体说的那句话：“你的一切征服、光荣、凯旋、战利品，都缩到这小小的尺度里了？”可实际上，缩掉的是欧洲的头脑；如我们所见，它全然忙于一场大扫除，要先把迷信清扫出门，再重新适应进化这个新概念。

### Evolution in the Theatre / 剧场中的进化
===Original===

On the stage (and here I come at last to my own particular function in the matter), Comedy, as a destructive, derisory, critical, negative art, kept the theatre open when sublime tragedy perished. From Molière to Oscar Wilde we had a line of comedic playwrights who, if they had nothing fundamentally positive to say, were at least in revolt against falsehood and imposture, and were not only, as they claimed, “chastening morals by ridicule,” but, in Johnson’s phrase, clearing our minds of can’t, and thereby showing an uneasiness in the presence of error which is the surest symptom of intellectual vitality. Meanwhile the name of Tragedy was assumed by plays in which everyone was killed in the last act, just as, in spite of Molière, plays in which everyone was married in the last act called themselves comedies. Now neither tragedies nor comedies can be produced according to a prescription which gives only the last moments of the last act. Shakespeare did not make *Hamlet* out of its final butchery, nor *Twelfth Night* out of its final matrimony. And he could not become the conscious iconographer of a religion because he had no conscious religion. He had therefore to exercise his extraordinary natural gifts in the very entertaining art of mimicry, giving us the famous “delineation of character” which makes his plays, like the novels of Scott, Dumas, and Dickens, so delightful. Also, he developed that curious and questionable art of building us a refuge from despair by disguising the cruelties of Nature as jokes. But with all his gifts, the fact remains that he never found the inspiration to write an original play. He furbished up old plays, and adapted popular stories, and chapters of history from Holinshed’s *Chronicle* and Plutarch’s biographies, to the stage. All this he did (or did not; for there are minus quantities in the algebra of art) with a recklessness which showed that his trade lay far from his conscience. It is true that he never takes his characters from the borrowed story, because it was less trouble and more fun to him to create them afresh; but nonetheless he heaps the murders and villainies of the borrowed story on his own essentially gentle creations without scruple, no matter how incongruous they may be.

===Chinese===

在舞台上（这里终于要说到我自己在这件事里的本分了），喜剧作为一门破坏的、讥诮的、批评的、否定的艺术，在高悲剧死亡之后维持着剧场不倒。从莫里哀到奥斯卡·王尔德，我们有一连串喜剧作家，他们纵然没有多少根本肯定的东西可说，至少是在反叛虚伪与欺诈，而且不但如他们自称的“以笑骂整饬风化”，还照约翰逊的说法，扫清我们头脑里的伪善，从而在错误面前显出一种不安——那正是智识活力最可靠的征候。与此同时，悲剧之名被一些末幕人人丧命的剧本冒领了，正如不顾莫里哀的先例，末幕人人成亲的剧本自称喜剧。可是悲剧也罢喜剧也罢，都不可能照一张只开列末幕最后时刻的处方炮制出来。莎士比亚不是用《哈姆雷特》结尾那场屠杀造出《哈姆雷特》，也不是用《第十二夜》结尾那场婚配造出《第十二夜》的。而他做不成一门宗教的自觉图像师，因为他没有自觉的宗教。于是他只好把他那非凡的天赋用在模仿这门极有娱乐性的艺术上，给我们那著名的“性格刻画”——正是它，使他的戏同司各特、大仲马和狄更斯的小说一样叫人心喜。他还发展出那门古怪而可疑的艺术：把自然的残忍化装成笑话，替我们搭一座躲避绝望的避难所。可是尽管有这些天赋，事实仍旧是：他从来不曾找到灵感写一出原创的戏。他把旧戏翻新，把流行的故事、把霍林斯赫德《编年史》和普鲁塔克《传记集》里的历史章节改编上舞台。这一切他做得（或者没做；因为艺术的代数里有负数）那样漫不经心，可见他的行当远在他的良心之外。诚然，他从不照搬借来的故事里的人物，因为对他来说，重新创造人物既省事又好玩；可他还是毫无顾忌地把借来的故事里的谋杀和恶行，成堆地压到他自己那些本质温厚的创造物头上，不管它们多么不般配。

===Original===

And all the time his vital need for a philosophy drives him to seek one by the quaint professional method of introducing philosophers as characters into his plays, and even of making his heroes philosophers; but when they come on the stage they have no philosophy to expound: they are only pessimists and railers; and their occasional would-be philosophic speeches, such as “The Seven Ages of Man” and “The Soliloquy on Suicide,” show how deeply in the dark Shakespeare was as to what philosophy means. He forced himself in among the greatest of playwrights without having once entered that region in which Michelangelo, Beethoven, Goethe, and the antique Athenian stage poets are great. He would really not be great at all if it were not that he had religion enough to be aware that his religionless condition was one of despair. His towering *King Lear* would be only a melodrama were it not for its express admission that if there is nothing more to be said of the universe than Hamlet has to say, then “as flies to wanton boys are we to the gods: they kill us for their sport.”

Ever since Shakespeare, playwrights have been struggling with the same lack of religion; and many of them were forced to become mere panders and sensation-mongers because, though they had higher ambitions, they could find no better subject-matter. From Congreve to Sheridan they were so sterile in spite of their wit that they did not achieve between them the output of Molière’s single lifetime; and they were all (not without reason) ashamed of their profession, and preferred to be regarded as mere men of fashion with a rakish hobby. Goldsmith’s was the only saved soul in that pandemonium.

===Chinese===

而他那要一门哲学的生命需要，时时刻刻驱使他去寻一门；用的却是那古怪的行当法子：把哲学家当人物写进戏里，甚至把他的主人公写成哲学家；可是等他们上了台，他们却没有哲学可讲：他们只是悲观者和骂世者；他们偶尔来几段想充哲学的台词，比如“人生的七个时期”和“论自杀的独白”，正好显出莎士比亚对哲学为何物懂得多么浅。他硬挤进了最伟大的剧作家之列，却从不曾踏进米开朗琪罗、贝多芬、歌德和古代雅典舞台诗人们之所以伟大的那个区域。倘若不是他还有足够的宗教感、知道自己的无宗教状态是一种绝望，他其实根本算不上伟大。他那巍峨的《李尔王》，若不是里面明明白白承认了——倘若对宇宙所能说的不比哈姆雷特能说的更多，那么“我们之于诸神，正如顽童之于苍蝇：他们杀我们取乐”——就只是一部情节剧。

自莎士比亚以来，剧作家们一直同这同一种宗教匮乏搏斗；其中许多人被迫沦为单纯的皮条客和轰动事件的贩子，因为尽管志向更高，他们找不到更好的题材。从康格里夫到谢里丹，他们虽有才智却那样不育，两代人加起来的产量不及莫里哀一个人的半个生涯；他们全都（并非无缘无故）以自己的行当为耻，宁愿被看作带点风流嗜好的时髦人物。在那片群魔殿里，哥尔德斯密斯是唯一得救的灵魂。

===Original===

The leaders among my own contemporaries (now veterans) snatched at minor social problems rather than write entirely without any wider purpose than to win money and fame. One of them expressed to me his envy of the ancient Greek playwrights because the Athenians asked them, not for some “new and original” disguise of the half-dozen threadbare plots of the modern theatre, but for the deepest lesson they could draw from the familiar and sacred legends of their country. “Let us all,” he said, “write an *Electra*, an *Antigone*, an *Agamemnon*, and show what we can do with it.” But he did not write any of them, because these legends are no longer religious: Aphrodite and Artemis and Poseidon are deader than their statues. Another, with a commanding position and every trick of British farce and Parisian drama at his fingers’ ends, finally could not write without a sermon to preach, and yet could not find texts more fundamental than the hypocrisies of sham Puritanism, or the matrimonial speculation which makes our young actresses as careful of their reputations as of their complexions. A third, too tenderhearted to break our spirits with the realities of a bitter experience, coaxed a wistful pathos and a dainty fun out of the fairy cloudland that lay between him and the empty heavens. The giants of the theatre of our time, Ibsen and Strindberg, had no greater comfort for the world than we: indeed much less; for they refused us even the Shakespearian-Dickensian consolation of laughter at mischief, accurately called comic relief. Our emancipated young successors scorn us, very properly. But they will be able to do no better whilst the drama remains pre-Evolutionist. Let them consider the great exception of Goethe. He, no richer than Shakespeare, Ibsen, or Strindberg in specific talent as a playwright, is in the empyrean whilst they are gnashing their teeth in impotent fury in the mud, or at best finding an acid enjoyment in the irony of their predicament. Goethe is Olympian: the other giants are infernal in everything but their veracity and their repudiation of the irreligion of their time: that is, they are bitter and hopeless. It is not a question of mere dates. Goethe was an Evolutionist in 1830: many playwrights, even young ones, are still untouched by Creative Evolution in 1920.

===Chinese===

我自己同时代人里的领头者们（如今都是老将了）宁可抓些细小的社会问题，也不肯全无更广的目的、只为金钱和名声写作。其中一位向我表示过他羡慕古希腊的剧作家，因为雅典人向他们要的，不是现代剧场那六七个破烂情节的什么“新颖别致”的化装，而是他们能从本邦熟悉而神圣的传奇里抽出的最深教训。“咱们都来写一出《厄勒克特拉》、一出《安提戈涅》、一出《阿伽门农》，看看能拿它做出什么来，”他说。可他一出也没写，因为这些传奇已不再是宗教：阿佛洛狄忒、阿耳忒弥斯和波塞冬，比他们的雕像死得更彻底。另一位，地位显赫，英式闹剧和巴黎戏剧的十八般武艺样样精熟，末了没有一篇布道可讲就写不了戏，可找到的经文又超不过伪清教的伪善，或那门叫我们的年轻女演员们爱惜名声如同爱惜容颜的婚姻投机。第三位，心肠太软，不肯用辛酸经验的现实来折断我们的精神，便从横在他和空荡荡的天之间的那片仙境云乡里，哄出了绵绵的哀感和精巧的谐趣。我们时代剧场的巨人们，易卜生和斯特林堡，给世界的安慰也不比我们多：其实少得多；因为他们连莎士比亚—狄更斯式的安慰——拿祸事取笑，准确地叫作喜剧性松弛——也拒绝给我们。我们那些解放了的年轻后继者鄙视我们，鄙视得很对。可只要戏剧还停留在前进化论阶段，他们也不会做得更好。让他们想想歌德这个伟大的例外。他作为剧作家的专门才具并不比莎士比亚、易卜生或斯特林堡富余，却已身在高天，而他们还在泥里咬牙切齿、徒然狂怒，至多在自己的窘境反讽里榨出一点酸溜溜的享受。歌德是奥林波斯式的：其余巨人们在一切方面都是地狱式的，除了一点：他们的诚实和他们对自己时代非宗教的拒斥——也就是说，他们是痛苦的、无望的。这不单是个年月的问题。歌德在1830年已是进化论者：而许多剧作家，连年轻的在内，到了1920年还没沾到创造进化的边。

===Original===

Ibsen was Darwinized to the extent of exploiting heredity on the stage much as the ancient Athenian playwrights exploited the *Eumenides*; but there is no trace in his plays of any faith in or knowledge of Creative Evolution as a modern scientific fact. True, the poetic aspiration is plain enough in his Emperor or Galilean; but it is one of Ibsen’s distinctions that nothing was valid for him but science; and he left that vision of the future which his Roman seer calls “the third Empire” behind him as a Utopian dream when he settled down to his serious grapple with realities in those plays of modern life with which he overcame Europe, and broke the dusty windows of every dry-rotten theatre in it from Moscow to Manchester.

===Chinese===

易卜生被达尔文化到这样的程度：在舞台上开发遗传，就像古雅典剧作家们开发复仇女神们一样；可是他的戏里没有一丝对创造进化的信仰或知识的痕迹，把它当作一门现代科学事实来看。诚然，那股诗意的渴望在他的《皇帝与加利利人》里够明显的了；可是易卜生的特色之一，便是对他来说唯有科学才算数；于是他把罗马先知所称“第三帝国”的那幅未来远景抛在了身后，当作一场乌托邦之梦，然后坐下来同现实认真角力——在那些征服了欧洲的现代生活剧里，他把从莫斯科到曼彻斯特每一座干朽剧场的积尘窗户都打破了。

### My Own Part in the Matter / 我在这件事里的本分
===Original===

In my own activities as a playwright I found this state of things intolerable. The fashionable theatre prescribed one serious subject: clandestine adultery: the dullest of all subjects for a serious author, whatever it may be for audiences who read the police intelligence and skip the reviews and leading articles. I tried slum-landlordism, doctrinaire Free Love (pseudo-Ibsenism), prostitution, militarism, marriage, history, current politics, natural Christianity, national and individual character, paradoxes of conventional society, husband hunting, questions of conscience, professional delusions and impostures, all worked into a series of comedies of manners in the classic fashion, which was then very much out of fashion, the mechanical tricks of Parisian “construction” being *de rigueur* in the theatre. But this, though it occupied me and established me professionally, did not constitute me an iconographer of the religion of my time, and thus fulfil my natural function as an artist. I was quite conscious of this; for I had always known that civilization needs a religion as a matter of life or death; and as the conception of Creative Evolution developed I saw that we were at last within reach of a faith which complied with the first condition of all the religions that have ever taken hold of humanity: namely, that it must be, first and fundamentally, a science of metabiology. This was a crucial point with me; for I had seen Bible fetishism, after standing up to all the rationalistic batteries of Hume, Voltaire, and the rest, collapse before the onslaught of much less gifted Evolutionists, solely because they discredited it as a biological document; so that from that moment it lost its hold, and left literate Christendom faithless. My own Irish eighteenth-centuryism made it impossible for me to believe anything until I could conceive it as a scientific hypothesis, even though the abominations, quackeries, impostures, venalities, credulities, and delusions of the camp followers of science, and the brazen lies and priestly pretensions of the pseudo-scientific cure-mongers, all sedulously inculcated by modern “secondary education,” were so monstrous that I was sometimes forced to make a verbal distinction between science and knowledge lest I should mislead my readers.

===Chinese===

在我自己作为剧作家的活动中，我发现这种局面无法忍受。时髦剧场只规定一个严肃题材：偷情通奸。对严肃作家来说，这是天下最乏味的题材——不论它对那些读报只读警务栏、跳过剧评和社论的观众们是怎样。我试过贫民窟房东主义、教条的自由恋爱（伪易卜生主义）、卖淫、军国主义、婚姻、历史、时事政治、自然的基督教、民族性格和个人性格、世俗社会的种种悖论、猎夫、良心问题、职业的错觉与欺诈，统统织进一组古典风致的世态喜剧里——那种风致在当时大大地不合时宜，因为巴黎式的“结构”机械手艺才是剧场的硬性规定。可是这一切虽然占据了我，也在职业上确立了我，却没能使我成为我这个时代的宗教的图像师，从而尽到我这艺术家的天职。这一点我心里很清楚；因为我一直知道，文明之需要宗教，是一件生死攸关的事；而随着创造进化的概念发展起来，我看到我们终于够得着一门信仰了，它符合一切曾经抓住过人类的宗教的第一条件：即它首先并且根本地必须是一门超生物学。这对我是一个关键；因为我亲眼见过《圣经》拜物教，在顶住了休谟、伏尔泰之辈全部理性主义炮火之后，倒在才能差得远的进化论者们的猛攻面前，只因为他们把它当作生物学文献搞臭了它；从那一刻起，它便失去了抓手，留下能读会写的基督教世界没了信仰。我自己那点爱尔兰的十八世纪气质，使我不把一件事想成科学假说便无法相信它，尽管科学的随营人们那一切的丑行、江湖术、欺诈、受贿、轻信和错觉，还有伪科学卖药郎中们的厚颜谎言和祭司架势——这一切都被现代“中等教育”殷勤地灌输着——都那么骇人，以致我有时不得不在言语上把科学和知识分开，免得误导我的读者。

===Original===

But I never forgot that without knowledge even wisdom is more dangerous than mere opportunist ignorance, and that somebody must take the Garden of Eden in hand and weed it properly.

===Chinese===

可是我从未忘记：没有知识，连智慧也比单纯的机会主义的无知更危险；总得有人接过伊甸园来，好好除一除草。

===Original===

Accordingly, in 1901, I took the legend of Don Juan in its Mozartian form and made it a dramatic parable of Creative Evolution. But being then at the height of my invention and comedic talent, I decorated it too brilliantly and lavishly. I surrounded it with a comedy of which it formed only one act, and that act was so completely episodical (it was a dream which did not affect the action of the piece) that the comedy could be detached and played by itself: indeed it could hardly be played at full length owing to the enormous length of the entire work, though that feat has been performed a few times in Scotland by Mr. Esme Percy, who led one of the forlorn hopes of the advanced drama at that time. Also I supplied the published work with an imposing framework consisting of a preface, an appendix called *The Revolutionist’s Handbook*, and a final display of aphoristic fireworks. The effect was so vertiginous, apparently, that nobody noticed the new religion in the centre of the intellectual whirlpool. Now I protest I did not cut these cerebral capers in mere inconsiderate exuberance. I did it because the worst convention of the criticism of the theatre current at that time was that intellectual seriousness is out of place on the stage; that the theatre is a place of shallow amusement; that people go there to be soothed after the enormous intellectual strain of a day in the city: in short, that a playwright is a person whose business it is to make unwholesome confectionery out of cheap emotions. My answer to this was to put all my intellectual goods in the shop window under the sign of *Man and Superman*. That part of my design succeeded. By good luck and acting, the comedy triumphed on the stage; and the book was a good deal discussed. Since then the sweet-shop view of the theatre has been out of countenance; and its critical exponents have been driven to take an intellectual pose which, though often more trying than their old intellectually nihilistic vulgarity, at least concedes the dignity of the theatre, not to mention the usefulness of those who live by criticizing it. And the younger playwrights are not only taking their art seriously, but being taken seriously themselves. The critic who ought to be a newsboy is now comparatively rare.

===Chinese===

于是，1901年，我取过唐璜的传奇，取它的莫扎特式形态，把它编成创造进化的一出戏剧寓言。可是那时我的发明之才和喜剧之才正处在顶峰，把它装点得太华丽、太挥霍了。我给它围上了一部喜剧，它只占其中一幕；而那一幕又完全是插话式的（那是一场梦，并不影响全剧的行动），所以那部喜剧可以拆下来单独演：其实全剧长得骇人，整部戏几乎没法从头演到尾，虽然在苏格兰这个壮举由埃斯米·珀西先生完成过几次——当年先进戏剧的几支孤军之一就是他领的。我还给出版物配上了一副堂皇的框架：一篇序、一部题为《革命家手册》的附录、外加一场格言烟火的终场表演。效果显然太叫人眩晕，结果谁也没有注意到那场智识漩涡中心的新宗教。现在我声明：我翻这些筋斗，并不是单单逞一时之快。我这样做，是因为当时流行的剧场批评最坏的一条成见，就是认为舞台上容不得智识的严肃；认为剧场是浅薄消遣之地；认为人们进城劳累一天、脑力紧绷之后，上那儿是去求舒解的：一句话，剧作家就是个靠廉价情感制造不卫生甜点的人。我对这一切的回答，是把我的全部智识货色都摆进橱窗，挂在《人与超人》的招牌底下。我这项设计的那部分成功了。靠运气和演技，那部喜剧在舞台上打了胜仗；那本书也颇被议论了一阵。从此，剧场的糖果铺论丢了脸；它的批评代言人们被迫摆出一副智识的姿态——那虽然往往比他们旧日智识虚无的粗鄙更叫人受不了，至少承认了剧场的尊严，更不必说承认了靠批评剧场为生者们的用处。而年轻的剧作家们不但认真对待起他们的艺术，自己也被人认真对待了。那种本该去当报童的批评家，如今已比较罕见了。

===Original===

I now find myself inspired to make a second legend of Creative Evolution without distractions and embellishments. My sands are running out; the exuberance of 1901 has aged into the garrulity of 1930; and the war has been a stern intimation that the matter is not one to be trifled with. I abandon the legend of Don Juan with its erotic associations, and go back to the legend of the Garden of Eden. I exploit the eternal interest of the philosopher’s stone which enables men to live forever. I am not, I hope, under more illusion than is humanly inevitable as to the crudity of this my beginning of a Bible for Creative Evolution. I am doing the best I can at my age. My powers are waning; but so much the better for those who found me unbearably brilliant when I was in my prime. It is my hope that a hundred apter and more elegant parables by younger hands will soon leave mine as far behind as the religious pictures of the fifteenth century left behind the first attempts of the early Christians at iconography. In that hope I withdraw and ring up the curtain.

===Chinese===

如今我发觉自己有了灵感，要再造一个创造进化的传奇，这回不再枝蔓、不再藻饰。我的沙漏快漏完了；1901年的勃勃生气，已老化成1930年的絮叨；而那场战争已严厉地晓谕：这件事开不得玩笑。我丢开唐璜的传奇连同它那情欲的联想，回到伊甸园的传奇上来。我开发点金石那永恒的兴趣——那叫人长生不死的东西。至于我这部创造进化圣经的开头何等粗糙，我希望我并未抱着超出人力所不可避免的错觉。我在我这个年纪尽力而为。我的才力在衰减；可是对那些在我盛年嫌我光彩得叫人受不了的人们来说，这样岂不更好。我希望，出自更年轻之手的成百个更贴切、更优雅的寓言，很快就会把我的这一篇远远抛在后面，正如十五世纪的宗教画把早期基督徒们最初的图像尝试远远抛在后面一样。怀着这个希望，我退场，拉开幕来。

## Part I / 第一部
===Original===



===Chinese===

第一部

### Act I / 第一幕

### Act I / 
===Original===

The Garden of Eden. Afternoon. An immense **serpent** is sleeping with her head buried in a thick bed of Johnswort, and her body coiled in apparently endless rings through the branches of a tree, which is already well grown; for the days of creation have been longer than our reckoning. She is not yet visible to anyone unaware of her presence, as her colors of green and brown make a perfect camouflage. Near her head a low rock shows above the Johnswort.

The rock and tree are on the border of a glade in which lies a dead fawn all awry, its neck being broken. **Adam**, crouching with one hand on the rock, is staring in consternation at the dead body. He has not noticed the serpent on his left hand. He turns his face to his right and calls excitedly.

**Adam**：Eve! Eve!

**Eve’s Voice**：What is it, Adam?

**Adam**：Come here. Quick. Something has happened.

**Eve**：*（Running in.）* What? Where? *（**Adam** points to the fawn.）* Oh! *（She goes to it; and he is emboldened to go with her.）* What is the matter with its eyes?

**Adam**：It is not only its eyes. Look. *（He kicks it.）*

**Eve**：Oh don’t! Why doesn’t it wake?

**Adam**：I don’t know. It is not asleep.

**Eve**：Not asleep?

**Adam**：Try.

**Eve**：*（Trying to shake it and roll it over.）* It is stiff and cold.

**Adam**：Nothing will wake it.

**Eve**：It has a queer smell. Pah! *（She dusts her hands, and draws away from it.）* Did you find it like that?

**Adam**：No. It was playing about; and it tripped and went head over heels. It never stirred again. Its neck is wrong. *（He stoops to lift the neck and show her.）*

**Eve**：Don’t touch it. Come away from it.

**Eve**：Adam.

**Adam**：Yes?

**Eve**：Suppose you were to trip and fall, would you go like that?

**Adam**：Ugh! *（He shudders and sits down on the rock.）*

**Eve**：*（Throwing herself on the ground beside him, and grasping his knee.）* You must be careful. Promise me you will be careful.

**Adam**：What is the good of being careful? We have to live here forever. Think of what forever means! Sooner or later I shall trip and fall. It may be tomorrow; it may be after as many days as there are leaves in the garden and grains of sand by the river. No matter: some day I shall forget and stumble.

**Eve**：I too.

===Chinese===

伊甸园。午后。一条巨大的**蛇**正睡着，头埋在一厚丛圣约翰草里，身子在一棵树的枝桠间盘成仿佛没有尽头的环；这树已经长得很成气候了，因为创世的日子比我们历算的要长。还不知道她在场的人看不见她，因为她绿褐相间的颜色构成完美的伪装。在她头旁，一块低矮的岩石露出圣约翰草之上。

岩石和树在一片林间空地的边上，空地上躺着一头死小鹿，姿势歪斜，颈子断了。**亚当**蹲着，一只手按在岩石上，正惊愕地盯着那具死尸。他没有留意左手边的蛇。他把脸转向右边，激动地喊叫。

**亚当**：夏娃！夏娃！

**夏娃的声音**：什么事，亚当？

**亚当**：快来。快。出事了。

**夏娃**：*（跑进来。）*什么？哪儿？*（**亚当**指着那头小鹿。）*哦！*（她走向它；他也有了胆子跟她同去。）*它的眼睛怎么啦？

**亚当**：不光是眼睛。你看。*（他踢了它一脚。）*

**夏娃**：哦，别！它怎么不醒？

**亚当**：我不知道。它不是睡着。

**夏娃**：不是睡着？

**亚当**：你试试。

**夏娃**：*（试着摇它、翻它。）*它又僵又冷。

**亚当**：什么也弄不醒它了。

**夏娃**：它有股怪味儿。呸！*（她掸掸手，从它身边退开。）*你发现它时就是这样的？

**亚当**：不是。它本来在玩耍；绊了一跤，翻了个跟头。就再没动过。它的脖子不对了。*（他俯身去抬起那脖子给她看。）*

**夏娃**：别碰它。离它远点。

**夏娃**：亚当。

**亚当**：嗯？

**夏娃**：倘若你绊倒摔了，你也会变成那样吗？

**亚当**：呃！*（他打个寒噤，在岩石上坐下。）*

**夏娃**：*（扑倒在他身边的地上，抓住他的膝盖。）*你千万要当心。答应我你要当心。

**亚当**：当心有什么用？我们得在这儿活到永远。想想永远是什么意思！早晚我会绊倒摔着。也许就在明天；也许要等到园里的叶子、河边的沙粒那样多的日子之后。不要紧：总有一天我会忘了，一脚踩空。

**夏娃**：我也是。

===Original===

**Adam**：*（Horrified.）* Oh no, no. I should be alone. Alone forever. You must never put yourself in danger of stumbling. You must not move about. You must sit still. I will take care of you and bring you what you want.

**Eve**：*（Turning away from him with a shrug, and hugging her ankles.）* I should soon get tired of that. Besides, if it happened to you, *I* should be alone. I could not sit still then. And at last it would happen to me too.

**Adam**：And then?

**Eve**：Then we should be no more. There would be only the things on all fours, and the birds, and the snakes.

**Adam**：That must not be.

**Eve**：Yes: that must not be. But it might be.

**Adam**：No. I tell you it must not be. I know that it must not be.

**Eve**：We both know it. How do we know it?

**Adam**：There is a voice in the garden that tells me things.

**Eve**：The garden is full of voices sometimes. They put all sorts of thoughts into my head.

**Adam**：To me there is only one voice. It is very low; but it is so near that it is like a whisper from within myself. There is no mistaking it for any voice of the birds or beasts, or for your voice.

**Eve**：It is strange that I should hear voices from all sides and you only one from within. But I have some thoughts that come from within me and not from the voices. The thought that we must not cease to be comes from within.

**Adam**：*（Despairingly.）* But we shall cease to be. We shall fall like the fawn and be broken. *（Rising and moving about in his agitation.）* I cannot bear this knowledge. I will not have it. It must not be, I tell you. Yet I do not know how to prevent it.

**Eve**：That is just what I feel; but it is very strange that you should say so: there is no pleasing you. You change your mind so often.

**Adam**：*（Scolding her.）* Why do you say that? How have I changed my mind?

**Eve**：You say we must not cease to exist. But you used to complain of having to exist always and forever. You sometimes sit for hours brooding and silent, hating me in your heart. When I ask you what I have done to you, you say you are not thinking of me, but of the horror of having to be here forever. But I know very well that what you mean is the horror of having to be here with me forever.

===Chinese===

**亚当**：*（惊恐。）*哦不，不。那我就孤单了。永远孤单。你千万不可以让自己有绊倒的危险。你不许走动。你必须坐着不动。我会照看你，你要什么我拿给你。

**夏娃**：*（耸耸肩转过身去，抱住自己的脚踝。）*那样我很快就腻了。再说，万一这事出在你身上，孤独的就是**我**了。那时候我可坐不住。到头来这事也会出在我身上。

**亚当**：然后呢？

**夏娃**：然后我们就不再有了。就只剩下四条腿的东西、鸟和蛇。

**亚当**：那可不行。

**夏娃**：是啊：那可不行。可它也许会行。

**亚当**：不行。我告诉你它不行。我知道它不行。

**夏娃**：我们都知道。我们怎么知道的？

**亚当**：园子里有个声音，把事情告诉我。

**夏娃**：园子里有时候到处是声音。它们把各式各样的念头放进我脑袋里。

**亚当**：在我听来只有一个声音。它很低；可它那么近，就像我自己里面的一声耳语。绝不会错当成任何鸟兽的声音，也不会错成你的声音。

**夏娃**：真怪，我从四面八方听见声音，你却只听见一个，从里面来的。可我也有些念头是从我自己里面来的，不是从声音来的。我们不能停止存在，这个念头就是从里面来的。

**亚当**：*（绝望地。）*可我们会停止存在。我们会像那头小鹿一样倒下、摔坏。*（站起来，激动地走来走去。）*我受不住这个知识。我不要它。我说了，它不行。可我不知道怎么拦住它。

**夏娃**：这正是我的感受；可奇怪的是这话竟由你说出来：真没法叫你满意。你变卦变得太快了。

**亚当**：*（责备她。）*你为什么这样说？我怎么变卦了？

**夏娃**：你说我们不能停止存在。可你从前老是抱怨非得永远、永远存在下去。你有时一坐几个钟头，闷着想心事，心里恨着我。我问你我把你怎么了，你说你想的不是我，是想非得永远在这儿的那种恐怖。可我心里明白得很，你的意思是：非得永远**同我**在一起的那种恐怖。

===Original===

**Adam**：Oh! That is what you think, is it? Well, you are wrong. *（He sits down again, sulkily.）* It is the horror of having to be with myself forever. I like you; but I do not like myself. I want to be different; to be better, to begin again and again; to shed myself as a snake sheds its skin. I am tired of myself. And yet I must endure myself, not for a day or for many days, but forever. That is a dreadful thought. That is what makes me sit brooding and silent and hateful. Do you never think of that?

**Eve**：No: I do not think about myself: what is the use? I am what I am: nothing can alter that. I think about you.

**Adam**：You should not. You are always spying on me. I can never be alone. You always want to know what I have been doing. It is a burden. You should try to have an existence of your own, instead of occupying yourself with my existence.

**Eve**：I *have* to think about you. You are lazy: you are dirty: you neglect yourself: you are always dreaming: you would eat bad food and become disgusting if I did not watch you and occupy myself with you. And now some day, in spite of all my care, you will fall on your head and become dead.

**Adam**：Dead? What word is that?

**Eve**：*（Pointing to the fawn.）* Like that. I call it dead.

**Adam**：*（Rising and approaching it slowly.）* There is something uncanny about it.

**Eve**：*（Joining him.）* Oh! It is changing into little white worms.

**Adam**：Throw it into the river. It is unbearable.

**Eve**：I dare not touch it.

**Adam**：Then I must, though I loathe it. It is poisoning the air. *（He gathers its hooves in his hand and carries it away in the direction from which **Eve** came, holding it as far from him as possible.）*

**The Serpent**：Eve.

**Eve**：*（Startled.）* Who is that?

**The Serpent**：It is I. I have come to show you my beautiful new hood. See! *（She spreads a magnificent amethystine hood.）*

**Eve**：*（Admiring it.）* Oh! But who taught you to speak?

**The Serpent**：You and Adam. I have crept through the grass, and hidden, and listened to you.

**Eve**：That was wonderfully clever of you.

**The Serpent**：I am the most subtle of all the creatures of the field.

**Eve**：Your hood is most lovely. *（She strokes it and pets **the serpent**.）* Pretty thing! Do you love your godmother Eve?

**The Serpent**：I adore her. *（She licks **Eve’s** neck with her double tongue.）*

===Chinese===
