**哈斯拉姆**：哦是。那儿有只鸟，一到春天就唱个不停：「钉下去还是扔掉：钉下去还是扔掉」——就这么个调——一唱一个钟头。可惜我父亲当年没给我另找一家铺子。

**客厅女仆**：有信要付邮吗，先生？

**富兰克林**：这些。*（他递过一篮信。她走到桌前取走。）*

**哈斯拉姆**：*（对**女仆**。）*您还没告诉巴纳巴斯先生吧？

**客厅女仆**：*（微微一缩。）*没有，先生。

**富兰克林**：告诉我什么？

**哈斯拉姆**：她要辞工了？

**富兰克林**：当真？很遗憾。是我们的不是吗，哈斯拉姆先生？

**哈斯拉姆**：一点也不。她在这儿过得好得很。

**客厅女仆**：*（红了脸。）*这一点我从来不否认，先生：再好的人家我也找不出了。可是我只有一条命好活；第二次机会未必有。您别见怪，先生；信得赶紧送去赶邮班。*（她拿着信出去。）*

**哈斯拉姆**：傻姑娘！就因为那家伙生了一双诗人的眼睛和两撇髭须，就要去嫁个村里砍柴的，跟他住进一间小破屋，跟一群孩子滚作一团。

===Original===

**Conrad**：*（Demurring.）* She said it was because she had only one life.

**Haslam**：Same thing, poor girl! The fellow persuaded her to chuck it; and when she marries him she’ll have to stick it. Rotten state of things, I call it.

**Conrad**：You see, she hasn’t time to find out what life really means. She has to die before she knows.

**Haslam**：*（Agreeably.）* That’s it.

**Franklyn**：She hasn’t time to form a well-instructed conscience.

**Haslam**：*（Still more cheerfully.）* Quite.

**Franklyn**：It goes deeper. She hasn’t time to form a genuine conscience at all. Some romantic points of honor and a few conventions. A world without conscience: that is the horror of our condition.

**Haslam**：*（Beaming.）* Simply fatuous. *（Rising.）* Well, I suppose I’d better be going. It’s most awfully good of you to put up with my calling.

**Conrad**：*（In his former low ghostly tone.）* You needn’t go, you know, if you are really interested.

**Haslam**：*（Fed up.）* Well, I’m afraid I ought to﻿—I really must get back﻿—I have something to do in the﻿—

**Franklyn**：*（Smiling benignly and rising to proffer his hand.）* Goodbye.

**Conrad**：*（Gruffly, giving him up as a bad job.）* Goodbye.

**Haslam**：Goodbye. Sorry﻿—er﻿—

**The Simple-Lifer**：*（Swooping on **Conrad** and kissing him.）* Hallo, Nunk. You’re before your time.

**Conrad**：Behave yourself. Theres a visitor.

**Franklyn**：Mr. Haslam, our new rector. *（To **Haslam**.）* My daughter Cynthia.

**Conrad**：Usually called Savvy, short for Savage.

**Savvy**：I usually call Mr. Haslam Bill, short for William. *（She strolls to the hearthrug, and surveys them calmly from that commanding position.）*

**Franklyn**：You know him?

**Savvy**：Rather. Sit down, Bill.

**Franklyn**：Mr. Haslam is going, Savvy. He has an engagement.

**Savvy**：I know. I’m the engagement.

**Conrad**：In that case, would you mind taking him into the garden while I talk to your father?

**Savvy**：*（To **Haslam**.）* Tennis?

**Haslam**：Rather!

**Savvy**：Come on. *（She dances out. He runs boyishly after her.）*

**Franklyn**：*（Leaving his table and beginning to walk up and down the room discontentedly.）* Savvy’s manners jar on me. They would have horrified her grandmother.

**Conrad**：*（Obstinately.）* They are happier manners than Mother’s manners.

===Chinese===

**康拉德**：*（不以为然。）*她说的是因为她只有一条命。

**哈斯拉姆**：一回事，可怜的姑娘！那家伙劝得她扔了这份差事；等嫁了他，她可就得钉下去了。我说呀，这世道烂透了。

**康拉德**：你瞧，她来不及弄清生活到底是怎么回事。不等她知道，她就得死了。

**哈斯拉姆**：*（欣然。）*正是。

**富兰克林**：她来不及养成一个受过良训的良心。

**哈斯拉姆**：*（更加愉快。）*可不是。

**富兰克林**：还要更深些。她压根儿来不及养成一个真正的良心。只有几点侠气的体面和几条俗例。一个没有良心的世界：这就是我们处境的恐怖所在。

**哈斯拉姆**：*（满面春风。）*真是糊涂透顶。*（起身。）*好了，我想我该走了。二位容我来打搅，实在太客气了。

**康拉德**：*（又是先前那种低幽的声调。）*您不必走，真的，倘若您当真感兴趣。

**哈斯拉姆**：*（吃不消了。）*呃，恐怕我应该——我实在得回去——我在——还有点事

**富兰克林**：*（蠢然微笑着起身伸手。）*再见。

**康拉德**：*（粗声粗气，认定他是朽木不可雕。）*再见。

**哈斯拉姆**：再见。对不住——呃——

**简朴生活派**：*（一下子扑上**康拉德**吻他。）*喂，努克。你今天来得早啊。

**康拉德**：放规矩点。有客人。

**富兰克林**：哈斯拉姆先生，我们的新本堂牧师。*（对**哈斯拉姆**。）*我女儿辛西娅（Cynthia）。

**康拉德**：大家平时叫她萨维（Savvy），Savage 的简称。

**萨维**：我平时管哈斯拉姆先生叫比尔（Bill），William 的简称。*（她信步走到炉前地毯上，从那个居高临下的位置静静打量众人。）*

**富兰克林**：你认识他？

**萨维**：太认识了。坐下，比尔。

**富兰克林**：哈斯拉姆先生正要走呢，萨维。他有个约会。

**萨维**：我知道。约会就是我。

**康拉德**：既然如此，可否请你把他带进园子，让我同你父亲谈谈？

**萨维**：*（对**哈斯拉姆**。）*打网球？

**哈斯拉姆**：求之不得！

**萨维**：走。*（她舞着出去。他孩子气地追着她跑。）*

**富兰克林**：*（离开书桌，不满地在屋里踱来踱去。）*萨维的做派硌得我难受。她祖母见了会吓坏的。

**康拉德**：*（执拗。）*那是比母亲的做派更快活的做派。

===Original===

**Franklyn**：Yes: they are franker, wholesomer, better in a hundred ways. And yet I squirm at them. I cannot get it out of my head that Mother was a well-mannered woman, and that Savvy has no manners at all.

**Conrad**：There wasn’t any pleasure in Mother’s fine manners. That makes a biological difference.

**Franklyn**：But there was beauty in Mother’s manners, grace in them, style in them: above all, decision in them. Savvy is such a cub.

**Conrad**：So she ought to be, at her age.

**Franklyn**：There it comes again! Her age! her age!

**Conrad**：You want her to be fully grown at eighteen. You want to force her into a stuck-up, artificial, premature self-possession before she has any self to possess. You just let her alone: she is right enough for her years.

**Franklyn**：I have let her alone; and look at the result! Like all the other young people who have been let alone, she becomes a Socialist. That is, she becomes hopelessly demoralized.

**Conrad**：Well, aren’t you a Socialist?

**Franklyn**：Yes; but that is not the same thing. You and I were brought up in the old bourgeois morality. We were taught bourgeois manners and bourgeois points of honor. Bourgeois manners may be snobbish manners: there may be no pleasure in them, as you say; but they are better than no manners. Many bourgeois points of honor may be false; but at least they exist. The women know what to expect and what is expected of them. Savvy doesn’t. She is a Bolshevist and nothing else. She has to improvise her manners and her conduct as she goes along. It’s often charming, no doubt; but sometimes she puts her foot in it frightfully; and then I feel that she is blaming me for not teaching her better.

**Conrad**：Well, you have something better to teach her now, at all events.

**Franklyn**：Yes: but it is too late. She doesn’t trust me now. She doesn’t talk about such things to me. She doesn’t read anything I write. She never comes to hear me lecture. I am out of it as far as Savvy is concerned. *（He resumes his seat at the writing-table.）*

**Conrad**：I must have a talk to her.

**Franklyn**：Perhaps she will listen to you. You are not her father.

**Conrad**：I sent her my last book. I can break the ice by asking her what she made of it.

===Chinese===

**富兰克林**：是：更坦率、更健康，一百样都更好。可我还是为她打熬不住。我脑子里总去不掉这个念头：母亲是个有教养的女人，而萨维压根儿没有教养。

**康拉德**：母亲那套漂亮做派里没有快乐。这里面就有生物学的差别。

**富兰克林**：可母亲的做派里有美，有风韵，有气派：尤其有决断。萨维却是个十足的毛孩子。

**康拉德**：在她这个年纪，她就该是个毛孩子。

**富兰克林**：又来了！她的年纪！她的年纪！

**康拉德**：你要她十八岁就完全长成。你要她在还没有一个自我可以拿得住之前，就被硬塞进一种自负的、人工的、早熟的自持。随她去：对她的年纪来说，她够对头的了。

**富兰克林**：我随她去了；瞧瞧这结果！同所有被放任的年轻人一样，她成了个社会主义者。也就是说，她无可救药地堕落了。

**康拉德**：呃，你不也是社会主义者吗？

**富兰克林**：是；可那不是一回事。你我是在旧的布尔乔亚道德里长大的。我们受的是布尔乔亚的教养、布尔乔亚的体面。布尔乔亚的教养也许是势利的教养：里头也许没有快乐，像你说的；可总比没有教养强。布尔乔亚的体面有许多也许虚假；可至少它们存在。女人们知道可以指望什么、人家指望她们什么。萨维不知道。她是个彻头彻尾的布尔什维克。她的做派和行为都得走一步即兴一步。无疑常常很可爱；可有时她闯的祸可怕得很；那时我就觉得，她在怪我没把她教得好一点。

**康拉德**：呃，无论如何，你现在有更好的东西可以教她了。

**富兰克林**：是：可太晚了。她如今不信任我。这类事她不同我谈。我写的东西她一个字不读。我讲演她从来不来听。在萨维这边，我出局了。*（他回到写字台前坐下。）*

**康拉德**：我得同她谈谈。

**富兰克林**：也许她肯听你的。你不是她父亲。

**康拉德**：我把新书寄给她了。正好可以问问她读出点什么，来打破这层冰。

===Original===

**Franklyn**：When she heard you were coming, she asked me whether all the leaves were cut, in case it fell into your hands. She hasn’t read a word of it.

**Conrad**：*（Rising indignantly.）* What!

**Franklyn**：*（Inexorably.）* Not a word of it.

**Conrad**：*（Beaten.）* Well, I suppose it’s only natural. Biology is a dry subject for a girl; and I am a pretty dry old codger.

**Franklyn**：Brother: if that is so; if biology as you have worked at it, and religion as I have worked at it, are dry subjects like the old stuff they taught under these names, and we two are dry old codgers, like the old preachers and professors, then *The Gospel of the Brothers Barnabas* is a delusion. Unless this withered thing religion, and this dry thing science, have come alive in our hands, alive and intensely interesting, we may just as well go out and dig the garden until it is time to dig our graves. *（**The parlor maid** returns. **Franklyn** is impatient at the interruption.）* Well? what is it now?

**The Parlor Maid**：Mr. Joyce Burge on the telephone, sir. He wants to speak to you.

**Franklyn**：*（Astonished.）* Mr. Joyce Burge!

**The Parlor Maid**：Yes, sir.

**Franklyn**：*（To **Conrad**.）* What on earth does this mean? I haven’t heard from him nor exchanged a word with him for years. I resigned the chairmanship of the Liberal Association and shook the dust of party politics from my feet before he was Prime Minister in the Coalition. Of course, he dropped me like a hot potato.

**Conrad**：Well, now that the Coalition has chucked him out, and he is only one of the half-dozen leaders of the Opposition, perhaps he wants to pick you up again.

**The Parlor Maid**：*（Warningly.）* He is holding the line, sir.

**Franklyn**：Yes: all right. *（He hurries out.）*

**Conrad**：So you have only one life to live, eh?

**The Parlor Maid**：*（Dropping on her knees in consternation.）* I meant no offence, sir.

**Conrad**：You didn’t give any. But you know you could live a devil of a long life if you really wanted to.

**The Parlor Maid**：*（Sitting down on her heels.）* Oh, don’t say that, sir. It’s so unsettling.

**Conrad**：Why? Have you been thinking about it?

**The Parlor Maid**：It would never have come into my head if you hadn’t put it there, sir. Me and cook had a look at your book.

===Chinese===

**富兰克林**：她一听说你要来，就问我书页有没有裁开，万一落到你手里。她一个字也没读。

**康拉德**：*（愤然而起。）*什么！

**富兰克林**：*（不留情面。）*一个字也没读。

**康拉德**：*（败下阵来。）*好吧，想来这也是自然的。生物学对姑娘家是门干燥的学问；我本人又是个相当干燥的老家伙。

**富兰克林**：兄弟：倘若真是这样；倘若你钻研的生物学、我钻研的宗教，同旧日挂这些名目教的那套旧货一样干燥，我们俩同老牧师老教授一样是干燥的老家伙，那么《巴纳巴斯兄弟的福音》就是一场妄念。除非宗教这件枯萎的东西、科学这件干燥的东西，到了我们手里已经活了过来，活了起来、而且兴味无穷，不然我们大可出去翻园子，一直翻到该掘我们自己坟墓的时候。*（**客厅女仆**回来。**富兰克林**对这打岔很不耐烦。）*又怎么啦？

**客厅女仆**：乔伊斯·伯奇（Joyce Burge）先生打来电话，先生。要同您讲话。

**富兰克林**：*（愕然。）*乔伊斯·伯奇先生！

**客厅女仆**：是，先生。

**富兰克林**：*（对**康拉德**。）*这究竟是什么意思？我多年没有他的音信，多年没同他讲过一句话。在他做联合内阁首相之前，我就辞了自由党协会主席的职，把党派政治的尘土从脚上抖掉了。当然，他把我当烫手山芋一样丢开了。

**康拉德**：呃，如今联合派把他扔了出来，他不过是反对党六七个头领之一，也许他又想把你捡回去了。

**客厅女仆**：*（提醒。）*他占着线呢，先生。

**富兰克林**：好，就来。*（他匆匆出去。）*

**康拉德**：这么说，你只有一条命好活，嗯？

**客厅女仆**：*（惊惶地跪下。）*我不敢冒犯，先生。

**康拉德**：你没有冒犯。可你知道，只要你真想，你满可以活上长得吓人的一辈子。

**客厅女仆**：*（坐在脚跟上。）*哦，别说这个，先生。叫人心神不定。

**康拉德**：怎么？你想过这个？

**客厅女仆**：要不是您放进我脑子里，我从来想不到那儿去，先生。我和厨娘翻过您那本书。

===Original===

**Conrad**：What! You and cook Had a look At my book! And my niece wouldn’t open it! The prophet is without honor in his own family. Well, what do you think of living for several hundred years? Are you going to have a try for it?

**The Parlor Maid**：Well, of course you’re not in earnest, sir. But it does set one thinking, especially when one is going to be married.

**Conrad**：What has that to do with it? He may live as long as you, you know.

**The Parlor Maid**：That’s just it, sir. You see, he must take me for better for worse, till death do us part. Do you think he would be so ready to do that, sir, if he thought it might be for several hundred years?

**Conrad**：That’s true. And what about yourself?

**The Parlor Maid**：Oh, I tell you straight out, sir, I’d never promise to live with the same man as long as that. I wouldn’t put up with my own children as long as that. Why, cook figured it out, sir, that when you were only 200, you might marry your own great-great-great-great-great-great-grandson and not even know who he was.

**Conrad**：Well, why not? For all you know, the man you are going to marry may be your great-great-great-great-great-great-grandmother’s great-great-great-great-great-great-grandson.

**The Parlor Maid**：But do you think it would ever be thought respectable, sir?

**Conrad**：My good girl, all biological necessities have to be made respectable whether we like it or not; so you needn’t worry yourself about that.

**Conrad**：Well, what does Joyce Burge want?

**Franklyn**：Oh, a silly misunderstanding. I have promised to address a meeting in Middlesborough; and some fool has put it into the papers that I am “coming to Middlesborough,” without any explanation. Of course, now that we are on the eve of a general election, political people think I am coming there to contest the parliamentary seat. Burge knows that I have a following, and thinks I could get into the House of Commons and head a group there. So he insists on coming to see me. He is staying with some people at Dollis Hill, and can be here in five or ten minutes, he says.

**Conrad**：But didn’t you tell him that it’s a false alarm?

**Franklyn**：Of course I did; but he won’t believe me.

**Conrad**：Called you a liar, in fact?

===Chinese===

**康拉德**：什么！你和厨娘——翻过——我的书！我的亲侄女倒不肯翻开它！先知在本家不受尊敬。好，活上几百年，你怎么看？打算试一试吗？

**客厅女仆**：呃，您自然不是当真的，先生。可它确实叫人想，尤其是就要结婚的人。

**康拉德**：那同这有什么相干？他也可以活得同你一样长，你知道。

**客厅女仆**：毛病正出在这儿，先生。您想，他得娶我，无论甘苦，至死才分离。要是他想到这也许要好几百年，您说他还会那么痛快吗，先生？

**康拉德**：这话不假。那你自己呢？

**客厅女仆**：哦，我跟您直说了吧，先生，那么长的日子，我永远不答应跟同一个男人过。连我自己的孩子，那么长我也受不了。您知道的，厨娘算过账，先生：您才两百岁的时候，没准儿就娶了自己的曾曾曾曾曾曾孙女儿，还认不出她是谁呢。

**康拉德**：呃，有什么不可以？说不定你要嫁的这个男人，就是你曾曾曾曾曾曾祖母的曾曾曾曾曾曾孙子呢，天晓得。

**客厅女仆**：可您觉得这事情到头来体面吗，先生？

**康拉德**：好姑娘，一切生物学的必要，不管我们乐意不乐意，都只好让它体面起来；这个你不必操心。

**康拉德**：呃，乔伊斯·伯奇要什么？

===Original===

**Franklyn**：No: I wish he had: any sort of plain speaking is better than the nauseous sham good fellowship our democratic public men get up for shop use. He pretends to believe me, and assures me his visit is quite disinterested; but why should he come if he has no axe to grind? These chaps never believe anything they say themselves; and naturally they cannot believe anything anyone else says.

**Conrad**：*（Rising.）* Well, I shall clear out. It was hard enough to stand the party politicians before the war; but now that they have managed to half kill Europe between them, I can’t be civil to them, and I don’t see why I should be.

**Franklyn**：Wait a bit. We have to find out how the world will take our new gospel. *（**Conrad** sits down again.）* Party politicians are still unfortunately an important part of the world. Suppose we try it on Joyce Burge.

**Conrad**：How can you? You can tell things only to people who can listen. Joyce Burge has talked so much that he has lost the power of listening. He doesn’t listen even in the House of Commons.

**Savvy**：*（Running to **Franklyn**.）* I say! Who do you think has just driven up in a big car?

**Franklyn**：Mr. Joyce Burge, perhaps.

**Savvy**：*（Disappointed.）* Oh, they know, Bill. Why didn’t you tell us he was coming? I have nothing on.

**Haslam**：I’d better go, hadn’t I?

**Conrad**：You just wait here, both of you. When you start yawning, Joyce Burge will take the hint, perhaps.

**Savvy**：*（To **Franklyn**.）* May we?

**Franklyn**：Yes, if you promise to behave yourself.

**Savvy**：*（Making a wry face.）* That will be a treat, won’t it?

**The Parlor Maid**：*（Entering and announcing.）* Mr. Joyce Burge.

**Franklyn**：*（Hurrying past **Savvy** to his guest with the false cordiality he has just been denouncing.）* Oh! Here you are. Delighted to see you. *（He shakes **Burge’s** hand, and introduces **Savvy**.）* My daughter.

**Savvy**：*（Not daring to approach.）* Very kind of you to come.

**Franklyn**：Mr. Haslam, our rector.

**Franklyn**：I forget whether you know my brother Conrad. He is a biologist.

===Chinese===

**富兰克林**：哦，一场无聊的误会。我答应了到米德尔斯伯勒去演讲；不知哪个蠢货把「要来米德尔斯伯勒」登在了报上，也没个下文。如今正当大选前夜，政界人士自然以为我要去争那个议席。伯奇知道我有一批追随者，觉得我进得了下议院、还能在那儿领起一派。所以他非来见我不可。他说他在多利斯山人家做客，五分十分钟就能到。

**康拉德**：可你没告诉他这是虚惊一场？

**富兰克林**：当然告诉了；可他不信。

**康拉德**：实际上，是把你当撒谎的了？

**富兰克林**：不：我倒巴不得他这样：再直白的话也好过我们那些民主政客为门市摆出来的人情深厚。他假装信我，一口咬定他这趟来全然无私；可他若没有自己的算盘，来做什么？这路人自己说的话自己一个字不信；自然，别人说的话他们也一个字不信。

**康拉德**：*（起身。）*好了，我避一避。战前，容忍党客就已经够难了；如今他们齐心协力把欧洲办了个半死，我再也对他们客气不出，也不明白凭什么要客气。

**富兰克林**：等等。咱们得摸清世界会怎样接待咱们的新福音。*（**康拉德**重新坐下。）*党客不幸仍旧是世界的重要一角。就拿乔伊斯·伯奇试试刀吧。

**康拉德**：怎么试？你只能把东西讲给会听的人。乔伊斯·伯奇话说得太多，把听的能力说丢了。连下议院里他也不听。

**萨维**：*（跑到**富兰克林**跟前。）*哎！你们猜谁坐大汽车刚到门口？

**富兰克林**：大概是乔伊斯·伯奇先生。

**萨维**：*（扫兴。）*哦，他们知道了，比尔。你怎么不告诉我们他要来？我这身打扮见不得人。

**哈斯拉姆**：我看我还是走吧？

**康拉德**：你们俩就在这儿待着。等你们一打呵欠，乔伊斯·伯奇兴许就知趣了。

**萨维**：*（对**富兰克林**。）*可以吗？

**富兰克林**：可以，只要你答应守规矩。

**萨维**：*（做个鬼脸。）*那可真够受用的，是不是？

**客厅女仆**：*（进来通报。）*乔伊斯·伯奇先生到。

**富兰克林**：*（越过**萨维**赶向客人，带着他方才还痛斥的那套虚情假意。）*哦！可来了。见到您真高兴。*（他握**伯奇**的手，介绍**萨维**。）*我女儿。

**萨维**：*（不敢近前。）*您肯赏光，太感谢了。

**富兰克林**：哈斯拉姆先生，我们的本堂牧师。

**富兰克林**：不知您认不认得我弟弟康拉德。他是生物学家。

===Original===

**Burge**：*（Suddenly bursting into energetic action and shaking hands heartily with **Conrad**.）* By reputation only, but very well, of course. How I wish I could have devoted myself to biology! I have always been interested in rocks and strata and volcanoes and so forth: they throw such a light on the age of the earth. *（With conviction.）* There is nothing like biology. “The cloud-capped towers, the solemn binnacles, the gorgeous temples, the great globe itself: yea, all that it inherit shall dissolve, and, like this influential pageant faded, leave not a rack behind.” That’s biology, you know: good sound biology. *（He sits down. So do the others, **Franklyn** on the stool, and **Conrad** on his Chippendale.）* Well, my dear Barnabas, what do you think of the situation? Don’t you think the time has come for us to make a move?

**Franklyn**：The time has always come to make a move.

**Burge**：How true! But what is the move to be? You are a man of enormous influence. We know that. Weve always known it. We have to consult you whether we like it or not. We﻿—

**Franklyn**：*（Interrupting firmly.）* I never meddle in party politics now.

**Savvy**：It’s no use saying you have no influence, daddy. Heaps of people swear by you.

**Burge**：*（Shining at her.）* Of course they do. Come! let me prove to you what we think of you. Shall we find you a first-rate constituency to contest at the next election? One that won’t cost you a penny. A metropolitan seat. What do you say to the Strand?

**Franklyn**：My dear Burge, I am not a child. Why do you go on wasting your party funds on the Strand? You know you cannot win it.

**Burge**：We cannot win it; but you﻿—

**Franklyn**：Oh, please!

**Savvy**：The Strand’s no use, Mr. Burge. I once canvassed for a Socialist there. Cheese it.

**Burge**：Cheese it!

**Haslam**：*（Spluttering with suppressed laughter.）* Priceless!

**Savvy**：Well, I suppose I shouldn’t say cheese it to a Right Honorable. But the Strand, you know! Do come off it.

**Franklyn**：You must excuse my daughter’s shocking manners, Burge; but I agree with her that popular democratic statesmen soon come to believe that everyone they speak to is an ignorant dupe and a born fool into the bargain.

**Burge**：*（Laughing genially.）* You old aristocrat, you! But believe me, the instinct of the people is sound﻿—

===Chinese===

**伯奇**：*（篱地活力迸发，热烈地同**康拉德**握手。）*闻名已久，如雷贯耳。我多想把一生献给生物学啊！我一向对岩石、地层、火山什么的感兴趣：它们把地球的年纪照得雪亮。*（诚恳地。）*什么也比不上生物学。「云蒸霞蔚的高楼，庄严的罗盘柜，辉煌的庙宇，伟大的地球自身：是啊，它所承袭的一切都将消解，像这场气派堂皇的盛会一样隐去，不留一丝云影。」这就是生物学，您知道的：正牌的、结结实实的生物学。*（他坐下。其余人也坐下，**富兰克林**坐方凳，**康拉德**坐他的奇彭代尔椅。）*好了，亲爱的巴纳巴斯，您怎么看时局？您不觉得咱们动手的时候到了吗？

**富兰克林**：动手的时候从来都是到了的。

**伯奇**：说得真对！可这一回动什么手呢？您是位影响力巨大的人物。这我们知道。一向知道。不管乐意不乐意，我们都得请教您。我们——

**富兰克林**：*（断然打断。）*我如今不沾党派政治。

**萨维**：爸爸，说你没有影响力没有用。一帮子人把你当圣经。

**伯奇**：*（对她容光焕发。）*当然当然。来！让我证明给您看我们怎么看重您。下回选举，我们给您找个一流选区去争一争，怎么样？一个子儿也不用您花。一个都会选区。斯特兰德怎么样？

**富兰克林**：我亲爱的伯奇，我不是孩子。你们为什么老把党的经费往斯特兰德里扔？你知道你们赢不了它。

**伯奇**：我们赢不了；可您——

**富兰克林**：哦，行行好！

**萨维**：斯特兰德没用，伯奇先生。我在那儿替一个社会主义者拉过票。省省吧。

**伯奇**：省省吧！

**哈斯拉姆**：*（强忍着笑，喷了出来。）*绝了！

**萨维**：呃，我想我不该对一位阔下说「省省吧」。可是斯特兰德，您知道的！快别提了。

**富兰克林**：小女失礼之至，您得包涵，伯奇；可我同意她的看法：受大众欢迎的民主政治家，很快就相信，凡同他们说话的人都是无知的冤桶，外加天生的傻瓜。

**伯奇**：*（蠢然大笑。）*您这老贵族！可是请相信我，人民的本能是可靠的——

===Original===

**Conrad**：*（Cutting in sharply.）* Then why are you in the Opposition instead of in the Government?

**Burge**：*（Showing signs of temper under this heckling.）* I deny that I am in the Opposition *morally*. The Government does not represent the country. I was chucked out of the Coalition by a Tory conspiracy. The people want me back. I don’t want to go back.

**Franklyn**：*（Gently remonstrant.）* My dear Burge: of course you do.

**Burge**：*（Turning on him.）* Not a bit of it. I want to cultivate my garden. I am not interested in politics: I am interested in roses. I haven’t a scrap of ambition. I went into politics because my wife shoved me into them, bless her! But I want to serve my country. What else am I for? I want to save my country from the Tories. They don’t represent the people. The man they have made Prime Minister has never represented the people; and you know it. Lord Dunreen is the bitterest old Tory left alive. What has he to offer to the people?

**Franklyn**：*（Cutting in before **Burge** can proceed﻿—as he evidently intends﻿—to answer his own question.）* I will tell you. He has ascertainable beliefs and principles to offer. The people know where they are with Lord Dunreen. They know what he thinks right and what he thinks wrong. With your followers they never know where they are. With you they never know where they are.

**Burge**：*（Amazed.）* With me!

**Franklyn**：Well, where are you? What are you?

**Burge**：Barnabas: you must be mad. You ask me what I am?

**Franklyn**：I do.

**Burge**：I am, if I mistake not, Joyce Burge, pretty well known throughout Europe, and indeed throughout the world, as the man who﻿—unworthily perhaps, but not quite unsuccessfully﻿—held the helm when the ship of State weathered the mightiest hurricane that has ever burst with earthshaking violence on the land of our fathers.

**Franklyn**：I know that. I know who you are. And the earthshaking part of it to me is that though you were placed in that enormously responsible position, neither I nor anyone else knows what your beliefs are, or even whether you have either beliefs or principles. What we did know was that your Government was formed largely of men who regarded you as a robber of henroosts, and whom you regarded as enemies of the people.

===Chinese===

**康拉德**：*（尖刻地插进来。）*那您怎么在野，不在朝？

**伯奇**：*（被这一将，动了火气。）*我否认我在**道义上**属于反对党。现政府并不代表国家。我是被托利党的阴谋从联合内阁里扔出来的。人民要我回去。是我不想回去。

**富兰克林**：*（温和地规劝。）*我亲爱的伯奇：您当然想。

**伯奇**：*（冲着他。）*一点也不想。我想种我的园子。我对政治没兴趣：我对玫瑰有兴趣。我一星半点的野心也没有。我进政界，是我太太把我推进去的，上帝保佑她！可我要为国家效劳。不然要我做什么？我要把国家从托利党手里救出来。他们不代表人民。他们捧出来的那位首相从来不曾代表人民；这您是知道的。邓林勋爵（Lord Dunreen）是活在世上最刻毒的老托利。他拿得出什么给人民？

**富兰克林**：*（抢在**伯奇**——他分明正打算——自问自答之前。）*我来告诉您。他拿得出可以查考的信仰和原则。同邓林勋爵打交道，人民知道自己站在哪里。他们知道他视什么为对、什么为错。同您的追随者打交道，他们从来不知道自己站在哪里。同您打交道，他们从来不知道自己站在哪里。

**伯奇**：*（愣然。）*同我！

**富兰克林**：呃，您站在哪里？您是什么人？

**伯奇**：巴纳巴斯：您准是疯了。您问我是什么人？

**富兰克林**：我问了。

**伯奇**：我，如果我没弄错，是乔伊斯·伯奇，在整个欧洲、其实在整个世界都相当有名，因为当国家这艘船熬过那场空前猛烈、天崩地裂地扑向我们父辈土地的大飓风时——也许不配，但也不算太失败地——掌过那只舵的人。

**富兰克林**：这个我知道。我知道您是谁。而在我看，此事天崩地裂的地方在于：您既被安置在那样责任重大的位置上，我却不知道、任何人也不知道您的信仰是什么，甚至不知道您究竟有没有信仰和原则。我们倒是确知一件事：您的政府里，很大一部分人把您看作偷鸡摸狗之徒，而您把他们看作人民的敌人。

===Original===

**Burge**：*（Adroitly, as he thinks.）* I agree with you. I agree with you absolutely. I don’t believe in coalition governments.

**Franklyn**：Precisely. Yet you formed two.

**Burge**：Why? Because we were at war. That is what you fellows never would realize. The Hun was at the gate. Our country, our lives, the honor of our wives and mothers and daughters, the tender flesh of our innocent babes, were at stake. Was that a time to argue about principles?

**Franklyn**：I should say it was the time of all others to confirm the resolution of our own men and gain the confidence and support of public opinion throughout the world by a declaration of principle. Do you think the Hun would ever have come to the gate if he had known that it would be shut in his face on principle? Did he not hold his own against you until America boldly affirmed the democratic principle and came to our rescue? Why did you let America snatch that honor from England?

**Burge**：Barnabas: America was carried away by words, and had to eat them at the Peace Conference. Beware of eloquence: it is the bane of popular speakers like you.

**Franklyn**：Well!!

**Savvy**：I like that!

**Haslam**：Priceless!

**Burge**：*（Continuing remorselessly.）* Come down to facts. It wasn’t principle that won the war: it was the British fleet and the blockade. America found the talk: I found the shells. You cannot win wars by principles; but you *can* win elections by them. There I am with you. You want the next election to be fought on principles: that is what it comes to, doesn’t it?

**Franklyn**：I don’t want it to be fought at all! An election is a moral horror, as bad as a battle except for the blood: a mud bath for every soul concerned in it. You know very well that it will not be fought on principle.

**Burge**：On the contrary it will be fought on nothing else. I believe a program is a mistake. I agree with you that principle is what we want.

**Franklyn**：Principle without program, eh?

**Burge**：Exactly. There it is in three words.

**Franklyn**：Why not in one word? Platitudes. That is what principle without program means.

===Chinese===

**伯奇**：*（自以为机敏地。）*我同意您。我绝对同意您。我不相信联合政府。

**富兰克林**：正是。可您组织过两届。

**伯奇**：为什么？因为我们在打仗。这就是你们这班人从来不肯明白的。匈奴人打到了门口。我们的国家、我们的性命、我们的妻子母亲女儿的清白、我们无辜婴孩的嫩肉，都在悬危之中。那是争论原则的时候吗？

**富兰克林**：我倒要说，那恰恰是比任何时候都更该发表原则宣言的时候——好坚定我们自己人的决心，好赢得全世界舆论的信任和支持。您以为，匈奴人若早知道那扇门会凭原则当面摔上，他还会打来吗？他不就是一直撑着不倒，直到美国勇敢地申明了民主原则、赶来救了我们？您怎么让英国把那份光荣让美国抢了去？

**伯奇**：巴纳巴斯：美国是被言辞冲昏了头，到了和会又不得不把那些言辞吞回去。当心雄辩：它是您这样受欢迎的演说家的克星。

**富兰克林**：好嘛！！

**萨维**：这话我爱听！

**哈斯拉姆**：绝了！

**伯奇**：*（不留情面地继续。）*说事实吧。打赢战争的不是原则：是英国舰队和封锁。美国出了言论：我出了炮弹。靠原则赢不了战争；靠它们**倒**赢得了大选。这一点我同您一致。您要下一届选举拿原则来打：说到底不就是这么回事吗？

**富兰克林**：我根本不要它打！选举是一场道德的恐怖，除了不见血同打仗一样糟：卷进去的每一个灵魂都得洗一场泥浆浴。您心里很清楚，它绝不会拿原则来打。

**伯奇**：恰恰相反，它除了原则什么都不会拿来打。我认为纲领是个错误。我同意您：我们要的是原则。

**富兰克林**：有原则而无纲领，嗯？

**伯奇**：一点不错。三个词就说尽了。

**富兰克林**：何不用一个词？陈词滥调。有原则而无纲领就是这个意思。

===Original===

**Burge**：*（Puzzled but patient, trying to get at **Franklyn’s** drift in order to ascertain his price.）* I have not made myself clear. Listen. I am agreeing with you. I am on your side. I am accepting your proposal. There isn’t going to be any more coalition. This time there won’t be a Tory in the Cabinet. Every candidate will have to pledge himself to Free Trade, slightly modified by consideration for our Overseas Dominions; to Disestablishment; to Reform of the House of Lords; to a revised scheme of Taxation of Land Values; and to doing something or other to keep the Irish quiet. Does that satisfy you?

**Franklyn**：It does not even interest me. Suppose your friends do commit themselves to all this! What does it prove about them except that they are hopelessly out of date even in party politics? that they have learnt nothing and forgotten nothing since 1885? What is it to me that they hate the Church and hate the landed gentry; that they are jealous of the nobility, and have shipping shares instead of manufacturing businesses in the Midlands? I can find you hundreds of the most sordid rascals, or the most densely stupid reactionaries, with all these qualifications.

**Burge**：Personal abuse proves nothing. Do you suppose the Tories are all angels because they are all members of the Church of England?

**Franklyn**：No; but they stand together as members of the Church of England, whereas your people, in attacking the Church, are all over the shop. The supporters of the Church are of one mind about religion: its enemies are of a dozen minds. The Churchmen are a phalanx: your people are a mob in which atheists are jostled by Plymouth Brethren, and Positivists by Pillars of Fire. You have with you all the crudest unbelievers and all the crudest fanatics.

**Burge**：We stand, as Cromwell did, for liberty of conscience, if that is what you mean.

**Franklyn**：How can you talk such rubbish over the graves of your conscientious objectors? All law limits liberty of conscience: if a man’s conscience allows him to steal your watch or to shirk military service, how much liberty do you allow it? Liberty of conscience is not my point.

**Burge**：*（Testily.）* I wish you would come to your point. Half the time you are saying that you must have principles; and when I offer you principles you say they won’t work.

===Chinese===

**伯奇**：*（困惑而耐心，想摸清**富兰克林**的意图，好确定他的价钱。）*我刚才没说清楚。听着。我同意您。我站在您一边。我接受您的提议。不会再有联合政府了。这一回内阁里一个托利也不会有。每个候选人都得承诺：自由贸易——稍稍照顾一点我们海外自治领的利益；政教分离；改革上议院；修订一份地价税方案；再随便做点什么把爱尔兰人稳住。这您满意了吗？

**富兰克林**：它连我的兴趣都引不起。就算您的朋友们把这些全应承下来，又怎样呢？这证明他们什么？只证明他们连在党派政治里也烂到了无可救药地过时：证明他们从一八八五年以来什么也没学会、什么也没忘掉。他们恨教会、恨乡绅地主；他们嫉妒贵族，手里握的是航运股票而不是中部地区的制造业：这些同我什么相干？这样的资格，我随手就能给您找几百个最肮脏的恶棍，或者最冥顽的反动派。

**伯奇**：人身攻击证明不了什么。您莫非以为托利党人个个是天使，就因为他们个个是英国国教徒？

**富兰克林**：不；可他们作为英国国教徒站在一起，而您的人在攻击教会时却七零八落。教会的支持者在宗教上是一条心：它的敌人有十几条心。国教徒是一支方阵：您的人是一群乌合之众，无神论者同普利茅斯兄弟会挤作一团，实证主义者同火柱会你推我揉。站在您这边的，是最粗劣的不信者加最粗劣的狂热者。

**伯奇**：我们同克伦威尔一样，主张良心自由，倘若您指的是这个。

**富兰克林**：在您那些良心拒服兵役者的坟头上，您怎么讲得出这种胡话？一切法律都限制良心自由：倘若一个人的良心准他偷您的表、或者逃避兵役，您给它多少自由？良心自由不是我的要害。

**伯奇**：*（烦躁。）*但愿您说您的正题。您一半时间说必须有原则；我一给您原则，您又说它们行不通。

===Original===

**Franklyn**：You have not offered me any principles. Your party shibboleths are not principles. If you get into power again you will find yourself at the head of a rabble of Socialists and anti-Socialists, of Jingo Imperialists and Little Englanders, of cast-iron Materialists and ecstatic Quakers, of Christian Scientists and Compulsory Inoculationists, of Syndicalists and Bureaucrats: in short, of men differing fiercely and irreconcilably on every principle that goes to the root of human society and destiny; and the impossibility of keeping such a team together will force you to sell the pass again to the solid Conservative Opposition.

**Burge**：*（Rising in wrath.）* Sell the pass again! You accuse me of having sold the pass!

**Franklyn**：When the terrible impact of real warfare swept your parliamentary sham warfare into the dustbin, you had to go behind the backs of your followers and make a secret agreement with the leaders of the Opposition to keep you in power on condition that you dropped all legislation of which they did not approve. And you could not even hold them to their bargain; for they presently betrayed the secret and forced the coalition on you.

**Burge**：I solemnly declare that this is a false and monstrous accusation.

**Franklyn**：Do you deny that the thing occurred? Were the uncontradicted reports false? Were the published letters forgeries?

**Burge**：Certainly not. But *I* did not do it. I was not Prime Minister then. It was that old dotard, that played-out old humbug Lubin. He was Prime Minister then, not I.

**Franklyn**：Do you mean to say you did not know?

**Burge**：*（Sitting down again with a shrug.）* Oh, I had to be told. But what could I do? If we had refused we might have had to go out of office.

**Franklyn**：Precisely.

**Burge**：Well, could we desert the country at such a crisis? The Hun was at the gate. Everyone has to make sacrifices for the sake of the country at such moments. We had to rise above party; and I am proud to say we never gave party a second thought. We stuck to﻿—

**Conrad**：Office?

===Chinese===

**富兰克林**：您没有给过我任何原则。您那套党门口令不是原则。你们若再度上台，您会发现自己领着一群乌合之众：社会主义者加反社会主义者，沙文帝国主义者加「小英格兰」派，铁板一块的唯物论者加出神的贵格会徒，基督教科学派加强制接种派，工团主义者加官僚：总之，是一群在关乎人类社会与命运根框的一切原则上凶恶对立、无法调和的人；而要把这样一支队伍拢在一起是办不到的，这将逼得您再一次把关口卖给团结一致的保守派反对党。

**伯奇**：*（勃然大怒而起。）*再一次卖关口！您指控我卖过关口！

**富兰克林**：当真刀真枪的战争的可怕冲击把你们议会里那套假战争扫进垃圾箱时，您只得绕开您追随者的背，同反对党领袖们做了一笔秘密交易：他们保您在台上，条件是您放弃一切他们不认可的立法。而您连这笔买卖都拴不住他们；因为他们不久便泄露了秘密，把联合政府硬塞给了您。

**伯奇**：我郑重声明，这是虚假而恶毒的指控。

**富兰克林**：您否认有这件事吗？那些无人驳斥的报道是假的？那些刊印出来的信件是伪造的？

**伯奇**：当然不是。可干这件事的不是**我**。我那时不是首相。是那个老糊涂，那个过了气的老骗子卢宾（Lubin）。那时他是首相，不是我。

**富兰克林**：您是想说您不知道？

**伯奇**：*（耸耸肩重新坐下。）*哦，总得让人告诉我。可我能怎么办？我们要是拒绝，也许就得下台了。

**富兰克林**：正是。

**伯奇**：那么，这样的危机关头，我们能扔下国家不管吗？匈奴人打到了门口。这种时刻，人人都得为国家作牺牲。我们只得超越党派；而且我可以自豪地说，我们再没为党派动过第二个念头。我们坚守——

**康拉德**：官位？

===Original===

**Burge**：*（Turning on him.）* Yes, sir, to office: that is, to responsibility, to danger, to heart-sickening toil, to abuse and misunderstanding, to a martyrdom that made us envy the very soldiers in the trenches. If you had had to live for months on aspirin and bromide of potassium to get a wink of sleep, you wouldn’t talk about office as if it were a catch.

**Franklyn**：Still, you admit that under our parliamentary system Lubin could not have helped himself?

**Burge**：On that subject my lips are closed. Nothing will induce me to say one word against the old man. I never have; and I never will. Lubin is old: he has never been a real statesman: he is as lazy as a cat on a hearthrug: you can’t get him to attend to anything: he is good for nothing but getting up and making speeches with a peroration that goes down with the back benches. But I say nothing against him. I gather that you do not think much of me as a statesman; but at all events I can get things done. I can hustle: even you will admit that. But Lubin! Oh my stars, Lubin!! If you only knew﻿—

**The Parlor Maid**：Mr. Lubin.

**Burge**：*（Bounding from his chair.）* Lubin! Is this a conspiracy?

**Lubin**：*（Coming to **Franklyn**.）* How do you do, Mr. Barnabas? *（He speaks very comfortably and kindly, much as if he were the host, and **Franklyn** an embarrassed but welcome guest.）* I had the pleasure of meeting you once at the Mansion House. I think it was to celebrate the conclusion of the hundred years peace with America.

**Franklyn**：*（Shaking hands.）* It was long before that: a meeting about Venezuela, when we were on the point of going to war with America.

**Lubin**：*（Not at all put out.）* Yes: you are quite right. I knew it was something about America. *（He pats **Franklyn’s** hand.）* And how have you been all this time? Well, eh?

**Franklyn**：*（Smiling to soften the sarcasm.）* A few vicissitudes of health naturally in so long a time.

**Lubin**：Just so. Just so. *（Looking round at **Savvy**.）* The young lady is﻿—?

**Franklyn**：My daughter, Savvy.

**Lubin**：*（Taking her hand affectionately in both his.）* And why has she never come to see us?

**Burge**：I don’t know whether you have noticed, Lubin, that I am present.

===Chinese===

**伯奇**：*（冲着他。）*是，先生，坚守官位：也就是坚守责任、危险、令人心碎的操劳、谩骂和误解，坚守一场叫我们羡慕堑壕里大兵的殉难。您要是也曾连着几个月靠阿司匹林和溴化钾才眯得着一觉，就不会把官位说成一件美差了。

**富兰克林**：不过，您承认了：在我们这套议会制度下，卢宾也是身不由己？

**伯奇**：这个话题我的嘴是封上的。什么也不能诱使我说那老头儿一个字的坏话。我从来没有；将来也决不会。卢宾老了：他从来算不得真正的政治家：他懒得好似炉前地毯上的猫：什么事也没法叫他上心：他的用处只剩站起来发表演说，来一段叫后座议员受用的结尾。可我不说他坏话。我听出来了，您不大看得起我这个政治家；可无论如何我能把事情办成。我雷厉风行：这一点连您也得承认。可是卢宾！哦我的天，卢宾！！您要是知道——

**客厅女仆**：卢宾先生到。

**伯奇**：*（从椅子上弹起来。）*卢宾！这是什么，一场阴谋？

**卢宾**：*（走向**富兰克林**。）*您好哇，巴纳巴斯先生？*（他说得极熨帖、极和蔼，倒好像他才是主人，**富兰克林**是个局促却受欢迎的客人。）*我有幸在市长官邸见过您一面。好像是庆祝同美国百年和好那次。

**富兰克林**：*（握手。）*那早得多：为委内瑞拉开的一次会，那时我们眼看要同美国开战。

**卢宾**：*（毫不见怪。）*对：您说得一点不错。我就知道同美国有点什么关系。*（他拍拍**富兰克林**的手。）*这一向身子骨可好？硬朗吧，嗯？

**富兰克林**：*（笑着把讥讽软化。）*这么长的岁月里，自然有些枯荣起落。

**卢宾**：正是。正是。*（环顾**萨维**。）*这位小姐是——？

**富兰克林**：我女儿，萨维。

**卢宾**：*（用双手慈爱地握住她的手。）*那她怎么从来不上我们那儿去呀？

**伯奇**：不知道您留意没有，卢宾，我也在场。

===Original===

**Lubin**：*（Seating himself in **Burge’s** chair with ineffable comfortableness.）* My dear Burge: if you imagine that it is possible to be within ten miles of your energetic presence without being acutely aware of it, you do yourself the greatest injustice. How are you? And how are your good newspaper friends? *（**Burge** makes an explosive movement; but **Lubin** goes on calmly and sweetly.）* And what are you doing here with my old friend Barnabas, if I may ask?

**Burge**：*（Sitting down in **Conrad’s** chair, leaving him standing uneasily in the corner.）* Well, just what you are doing, if you want to know. I am trying to enlist Mr. Barnabas’s valuable support for my party.

**Lubin**：Your party, eh? The newspaper party?

**Burge**：The Liberal Party. The party of which I have the honor to be leader.

**Lubin**：Have you now? That’s very interesting; for I thought *I* was the leader of the Liberal Party. However, it is very kind of you to take it off my hands, if the party will let you.

**Burge**：Do you suggest that I have not the support and confidence of the party?

**Lubin**：I don’t suggest anything, my dear Burge. Mr. Barnabas will tell you that we all think very highly of you. The country owes you a great deal. During the war, you did very creditably over the munitions; and if you were not quite so successful with the peace, nobody doubted that you meant well.

**Burge**：Very kind of you, Lubin. Let me remark that you cannot lead a progressive party without getting a move on.

**Lubin**：You mean you cannot. I did it for ten years without the least difficulty. And very comfortable, prosperous, pleasant years they were.

**Burge**：Yes; but what did they end in?

**Lubin**：In you, Burge. You don’t complain of that, do you?

**Burge**：*（Fiercely.）* In plague, pestilence, and famine; battle, murder, and sudden death.

**Lubin**：*（With an appreciative chuckle.）* The Nonconformist can quote the prayerbook for his own purposes, I see. How you enjoyed yourself over that business, Burge! Do you remember the Knockout Blow?

**Burge**：It came off: don’t forget that. Do *you* remember fighting to the last drop of your blood?

===Chinese===

**卢宾**：*（以难以言喻的舒坦在**伯奇**的椅子上落座。）*我亲爱的伯奇：您要是以为，人在您那股生龙活虎的气场的十里之内，还可能感觉不到您，那您可太委屈自己了。您可好？您那些报界的好朋友可好？*（**伯奇**做个要发作的动作；**卢宾**却从容而甜美地继续。）*再有，恕我动问，您在我这位老朋友巴纳巴斯这儿做什么呢？

**伯奇**：*（在**康拉德**的椅子上坐下，把他瞤在屋角局促地站着。）*呢，同您做的一样，您要知道的话。我在设法把巴纳巴斯先生宝贵的支持拉进我的党。

**卢宾**：您的党，嗯？报纸党？

**伯奇**：自由党。我有幸担任领袖的那个党。

**卢宾**：是吗？真有意思；我原以为**我**才是自由党的领袖呢。不过，您肯把它从我手里接过去，真是太好了，只要党肯让您接。

**伯奇**：您是暗示我不享有党的支持和信任？

**卢宾**：我什么也不暗示，我亲爱的伯奇。巴纳巴斯先生会告诉您，我们对您的评价都非常之高。国家欠您很多。战争期间，军火那一摊您办得很体面；和平那一摊您虽不那么成功，可谁也不怀疑您是一片好心。

**伯奇**：多谢您了，卢宾。请允许我说一句：不带劲，是领导不了一个进步政党的。

**卢宾**：您是说不带您不行。我带了十年，没有一点困难。而且那十年舒舒服服、蒸蒸日上、快快活活。

**伯奇**：是；可它们收场收在了什么上？

**卢宾**：收在了您身上，伯奇。这您没什么可抱怨的，不是吗？

**伯奇**：*（狠狠地。）*收在瘟疫、疫瘟和饥荒上；收在战争、凶杀和暴毙上。

**卢宾**：*（会心地咯咯一笑。）*看得出，非国教徒也能引祈祷书为己所用。那摊子事您办得可真快活，伯奇！还记得「致命一击」吗？

**伯奇**：它成了：别忘了这个。**您**还记得「流尽最后一滴血」吗？

===Original===

**Lubin**：*（Unruffled, to **Franklyn**.）* By the way, I remember your brother Conrad﻿—a wonderful brain and a dear good fellow﻿—explaining to me that I couldn’t fight to the last drop of my blood, because I should be dead long before I came to it. Most interesting, and quite true. He was introduced to me at a meeting where the suffragettes kept disturbing me. They had to be carried out kicking and making a horrid disturbance.

**Conrad**：No: it was later, at a meeting to support the Franchise Bill which gave them the vote.

**Lubin**：*（Discovering **Conrad’s** presence for the first time.）* You’re right: it was. I knew it had something to do with women. My memory never deceives me. Thank you. Will you introduce me to this gentleman, Barnabas?

**Conrad**：*（Not at all affably.）* I am the Conrad in question. *（He sits down in dudgeon on the vacant Chippendale.）*

**Lubin**：Are you? *（Looking at him pleasantly.）* Yes: of course you are. I never forget a face. But *（with an arch turn of his eyes to **Savvy**）* your pretty niece engaged all my powers of vision.

**Burge**：I wish youd be serious, Lubin. God knows we have passed through times terrible enough to make any man serious.

**Lubin**：I do not think I need to be reminded of that. In peace time I used to keep myself fresh for my work by banishing all worldly considerations from my mind on Sundays; but war has no respect for the Sabbath; and there have been Sundays within the last few years on which I have had to play as many as sixty-six games of bridge to keep my mind off the news from the front.

**Burge**：*（Scandalized.）* Sixty-six games of bridge on Sunday!!!

**Lubin**：You probably sang sixty-six hymns. But as I cannot boast either your admirable voice or your spiritual fervor, I had to fall back on bridge.

**Franklyn**：If I may go back to the subject of your visit, it seems to me that you may both be completely superseded by the Labor Party.

**Burge**：But I am in the truest sense myself a Labor leader. I﻿—*（He stops, as **Lubin** has risen with a half-suppressed yawn, and is already talking calmly, but without a pretence of interest.）*

===Chinese===

**卢宾**：*（不动声色，对**富兰克林**。）*对了，我记得令弟康拉德——一副了不得的头脑，又是个顶好的好人——给我解释过，我不可能流尽最后一滴血，因为还没到那地步我早就死了。有趣极了，而且千真万确。他是在一个集会上被引见给我的，那时妇女参政者们不断搅扰我。她们不得不被人抬出去，又踢又闹，搞得天翻地覆。

**康拉德**：不：是更晚，在一个支持给了她们选票的选举权法案的集会上。

**卢宾**：*（头一回发现**康拉德**在场。）*您说得对：正是。我就知道同女人有点什么关系。我的记忆从不骗我。谢谢您。给我引见引见这位先生吧，巴纳巴斯？

**康拉德**：*（毫不客气。）*我就是那位康拉德。*（他惄惄地在空着的那把奇彭代尔椅上坐下。）*

**卢宾**：是吗？*（愉快地看着他。）*对：您当然是。我从不忘记一张脸。不过*（眼风俏皮地转向**萨维**）*您这位漂亮的侄女把我的目力全占下了。

**伯奇**：卢宾，您能不能正经点。上帝知道，我们经历的那些时世，够叫任何人正经了。

**卢宾**：这个我想不必您来提醒。承平年月，我惯于每逢礼拜天把一切尘世的思虑逐出头脑，好让自己养精蓄锐应付工作；可战争是不守安息日的；近几年里有些礼拜天，我不得不打上多至六十六局桥牌，好把前线来的消息从脑子里岔开。

**伯奇**：*（骇然。）*礼拜天打六十六局桥牌！！！

**卢宾**：您大概唱了六十六首赞美诗。可惜我既没有您那副人人称羡的嗓子，也没有您那份灵性的热忱，只好退而求其次打桥牌。

**富兰克林**：请容我把话头拉回二位的来意：依我看，你们两位都可能被工党整个取而代之。

**伯奇**：可我在最真切的意义上本人就是工党领袖。我——*（他停住，因为**卢宾**已经起来，半掩着打了个呵欠，正平静地说下去，毫无装出来的兴致。）*

===Original===

**Lubin**：The Labor Party! Oh no, Mr. Barnabas. No, no, no, no, no. *（He moves in **Savvy’s** direction.）* There will be no trouble about that. Of course we must give them a few seats: more, I quite admit, than we should have dreamt of leaving to them before the war; but﻿—*（By this time he has reached the sofa where **Savvy** and **Haslam** are seated. He sits down between them; takes her hand; and drops the subject of Labor.）* Well, my dear young lady? What is the latest news? What’s going on? Have you seen Shoddy’s new play? Tell me all about it, and all about the latest books, and all about everything.

**Savvy**：You have not met Mr. Haslam. Our Rector.

**Lubin**：*（Who has quite overlooked **Haslam**.）* Never heard of him. Is he any good?

**Franklyn**：I was introducing him. This is Mr. Haslam.

**Haslam**：How d’ye do?

**Lubin**：I beg your pardon, Mr. Haslam. Delighted to meet you. *（To **Savvy**.）* Well, now, how many books have you written?

**Savvy**：*（Rather overwhelmed but attracted.）* None. I don’t write.

**Lubin**：You don’t say so; Well, what do you do? Music? Skirt-dancing?

**Savvy**：I don’t do anything.

**Lubin**：Thank God! You and I were born for one another. Who is your favorite poet, Sally?

**Savvy**：Savvy.

**Lubin**：Savvy! I never heard of him. Tell me all about him. Keep me up to date.

**Savvy**：It’s not a poet. *I* am Savvy, not Sally.

**Lubin**：Savvy! That’s a funny name, and very pretty. Savvy. It sounds Chinese. What does it mean?

**Conrad**：Short for Savage.

**Lubin**：*（Patting her hand.）* *La belle Sauvage*.

**Haslam**：*（Rising and surrendering **Savvy** to **Lubin** by crossing to the fireplace.）* I suppose the Church is out of it as far as progressive politics are concerned.

**Burge**：Nonsense! That notion about the Church being unprogressive is one of those shibboleths that our party must drop. The Church is all right essentially. Get rid of the establishment; get rid of the bishops; get rid of the candlesticks; get rid of the 39 articles; and the Church of England is just as good as any other Church; and I don’t care who hears me say so.

**Lubin**：It doesn’t matter a bit who hears you say so, my dear Burge. *（To **Savvy**.）* Who did you say your favorite poet was?

**Savvy**：I don’t make pets of poets. Who’s yours?

**Lubin**：Horace.

**Savvy**：Horace who?

===Chinese===

**卢宾**：工党！哦不，巴纳巴斯先生。不，不，不，不，不。*（他朝**萨维**那边挪动。）*那方面不会有麻烦。当然，我们得分给他们几个席位：比战前我们做梦肯留给他们的多些，这一点我完全承认；不过——*（这时他已走到**萨维**和**哈斯拉姆**坐的长榻前。他在两人中间坐下；拉起她的手；把工党的话题丢开。）*好啦，亲爱的小姐？有什么最新消息？外头都传些什么？肖迪（Shoddy）的新戏看了吗？统统讲给我听，最新的书，最新的一切。

**萨维**：您还没认识哈斯拉姆先生。我们的本堂牧师。

**卢宾**：*（他完全没把**哈斯拉姆**放在眼里。）*没听说过。他行吗？

**富兰克林**：我正要引见呢。这位是哈斯拉姆先生。

**哈斯拉姆**：您好。

**卢宾**：失敬失敬，哈斯拉姆先生。幸会幸会。*（对**萨维**。）*那么，您写过几本书啦？

**萨维**：*（有点招架不住，又被吸引着。）*一本没有。我不写作。

**卢宾**：不会吧；那您做什么呢？音乐？裙舞？

**萨维**：我什么都不做。

**卢宾**：感谢上帝！你我天生是一对儿。您最喜爱的诗人是谁，萨莉？

**萨维**：萨维。

**卢宾**：萨维！没听说过这位。给我讲讲他。好叫我不落伍。

**萨维**：那不是诗人。**我**才叫萨维，不叫萨莉。

**卢宾**：萨维！这名字真滑稽，也很好听。萨维。听着像中国名字。是什么意思？

**康拉德**：Savage 的简称。

**卢宾**：*（拍拍她的手。）*野姑娘，美姑娘（*La belle Sauvage*）。

**哈斯拉姆**：*（起身，横过炉边，把**萨维**让给**卢宾**。）*我想，就进步政治而言，教会是插不上手的了。

**伯奇**：胡说！教会不进步这种论调，正是我们党必须丢掉的口令之一。教会本质上好得很。去掉国教建制；去掉主教们；去掉烛台；去掉三十九条信纲；英国国教就同任何别的教会一样好；这话谁听见我都这么说。

**卢宾**：谁听见都毫无关系，我亲爱的伯奇。*（对**萨维**。）*您方才说您喜爱的诗人是谁来着？

**萨维**：我不拿诗人当宠物。您的是谁？

**卢宾**：贺拉斯。

**萨维**：哪个贺拉斯？

===Original===

**Lubin**：Quintus Horatius Flaccus: the noblest Roman of them all, my dear.

**Savvy**：Oh, if he is dead, that explains it. I have a theory that all the dead people we feel especially interested in must have been ourselves. You must be Horace’s reincarnation.

**Lubin**：*（Delighted.）* That is the very most charming and penetrating and intelligent thing that has ever been said to me. Barnabas: will you exchange daughters with me? I can give you your choice of two.

**Franklyn**：Man proposes. Savvy disposes.

**Lubin**：What does Savvy say?

**Burge**：Lubin: I came here to talk politics.

**Lubin**：Yes: you have only one subject, Burge. I came here to talk to Savvy. Take Burge into the next room, Barnabas; and let him rip.

**Burge**：*（Half-angry, half-indulgent.）* No; but really, Lubin, we are at a crisis﻿—

**Lubin**：My dear Burge, life is a disease; and the only difference between one man and another is the stage of the disease at which he lives. You are always at the crisis; I am always in the convalescent stage. I enjoy convalescence. It is the part that makes the illness worth while.

**Savvy**：*（Half-rising.）* Perhaps I’d better run away. I am distracting you.

**Lubin**：*（Making her sit down again.）* Not at all, my dear. You are only distracting Burge. Jolly good thing for him to be distracted by a pretty girl. Just what he needs.

**Burge**：I sometimes envy you, Lubin. The great movement of mankind, the giant sweep of the ages, passes you by and leaves you standing.

**Lubin**：It leaves me sitting, and quite comfortable, thank you. Go on sweeping. When you are tired of it, come back; and you will find England where it was, and me in my accustomed place, with Miss Savvy telling me all sorts of interesting things.

**Savvy**：*（Who has been growing more and more restless.）* Don’t let him shut you up, Mr. Burge. You know, Mr. Lubin, I am frightfully interested in the Labor movement, and in Theosophy, and in reconstruction after the war, and all sorts of things. I daresay the flappers in your smart set are tremendously flattered when you sit beside them and are nice to them as you are being nice to me; but I am not smart; and I am no use as a flapper. I am dowdy and serious. I want you to be serious. If you refuse, I shall go and sit beside Mr. Burge, and ask him to hold my hand.

===Chinese===

**卢宾**：昆图斯·贺拉提乌斯·弗拉库斯：罗马人里最高贵的一位，我亲爱的。

**萨维**：哦，他要是已经死了，那就说得通了。我有个理论：凡是我们特别感兴趣的死人，必定前世就是我们自己。您准是贺拉斯转世。

**卢宾**：*（大喜。）*这是生平人家对我讲的最最迷人、最最透彻、最最聪明的一句话。巴纳巴斯：咱们换女儿吧？我可以让您从两个里挑。

**富兰克林**：谋事在人。成事在萨维。

**卢宾**：萨维怎么说？

**伯奇**：卢宾：我是来谈政治的。

**卢宾**：是：您只有一个题目，伯奇。我是来同萨维谈天的。把伯奇带到隔壁屋里去吧，巴纳巴斯；随他去撒欢儿。

**伯奇**：*（半恼半纵。）*不；说真的，卢宾，我们正处在危急关头——

**卢宾**：我亲爱的伯奇，人生是一场病；人与人之间唯一的分别，是他活在病的哪一阶段。您永远处在危急关头；我永远处在康复期。康复期我享受得很。正是这一段，叫这场病生得值。

**萨维**：*（半起身。）*也许我还是走开好。我搅了你们。

**卢宾**：*（按她重新坐下。）*一点也不，亲爱的。您只搅了伯奇。叫个漂亮姑娘搅一搅，对他是大好事。正对他症。

**伯奇**：我有时真羡慕您，卢宾。人类的伟大运动、时代的巨大扫荡，从您身边过去，把您留在原地。

**卢宾**：是把我留在座位上，而且相当舒服，谢谢。扫您的去吧。扫累了，回来；英格兰还在老地方，我也在我的老位置上，萨维小姐正给我讲种种有趣的事儿。

**萨维**：*（越来越坐不住。）*别让他堵您的嘴，伯奇先生。您知道的，卢宾先生，我对劳工运动、对通神学、对战后的重建、对种种事情都怕人地感兴趣。我敢说，您那个时髦圈子里的轻佻姑娘们，您挨着她们坐、对她们和颜悦色——就像对我这样——她们个个受宠若惊；可我不时髦；当轻佻姑娘我也没用。我又土又严肃。我要您也严肃。您要不答应，我就过去坐在伯奇先生旁边，叫他握着我的手。

===Original===

**Lubin**：He wouldn’t know how to do it, my dear. Burge has a reputation as a profligate﻿—

**Burge**：*（Starting.）* Lubin: this is monstrous. I﻿—

**Lubin**：*（Continuing.）*﻿—but he is really a model of domesticity. His name is coupled with all the most celebrated beauties; but for him there is only one woman; and that is not you, my dear, but his very charming wife.

**Burge**：You are destroying my character in the act of pretending to save it. Have the goodness to confine yourself to your own character and your own wife. Both of them need all your attention.

**Lubin**：I have the privilege of my age and of my transparent innocence. I have not to struggle with your volcanic energy.

**Burge**：*（With an immense sense of power.）* No, by George!

**Franklyn**：I think I shall speak both for my brother and myself, and possibly also for my daughter, if I say that since the object of your visit and Mr. Joyce Burge’s is to some extent political, we should hear with great interest something about your political aims, Mr. Lubin.

**Lubin**：*（Assenting with complete good humor, and becoming attentive, clear, and businesslike in his tone.）* By all means, Mr. Barnabas. What we have to consider first, I take it, is what prospect there is of our finding you beside us in the House after the next election.

**Franklyn**：When I speak of politics, Mr. Lubin, I am not thinking of elections, or available seats, or party funds, or the registers, or even, I am sorry to have to add, of parliament as it exists at present. I had much rather you talked about bridge than about electioneering: it is the more interesting game of the two.

**Burge**：He wants to discuss principles, Lubin.

**Lubin**：*（Very cool and clear.）* I understand Mr. Barnabas quite well. But elections are unsettled things; principles are settled things.

**Conrad**：*（Impatiently.）* Great Heavens!﻿—

**Lubin**：*（Interrupting him with quiet authority.）* One moment, Dr. Barnabas. The main principles on which modern civilized society is founded are pretty well understood among educated people. That is what our dangerously half-educated masses and their pet demagogues﻿—if Burge will excuse that expression﻿—

**Burge**：Don’t mind me. Go on. I shall have something to say presently.

===Chinese===

**卢宾**：他不会那个，亲爱的。伯奇有个浪子的名声——

**伯奇**：*（跳起来。）*卢宾：这太不像话了。我——

**卢宾**：*（继续。）*——可他实在是家庭美德的模范。他的名字同所有最有名的美人连在一起；可对他来说女人只有一个；而且不是您，亲爱的，是他那位非常迷人的太太。

**伯奇**：您这是打着救我的旗号毁我的名声。劳驾，管好您自己的名声和您自己的太太吧。两样都够您全力对付的了。

**卢宾**：我享我的年纪和我的无辜透明的特权。我不必同您那火山似的精力搏斗。

**伯奇**：*（带着强烈的权力感。）*那可不，老天作证！

**富兰克林**：我想，这番话既代表我弟弟、也代表我自己，多半还代表我女儿：既然您和乔伊斯·伯奇先生的来意在某种程度上是政治性的，我们极愿听听您的政治抱负，卢宾先生。

**卢宾**：*（十分和气地应承，语调变得专注、清晰、干净利落。）*完全可以，巴纳巴斯先生。依我看，我们首先得考虑的是：下届选举之后，有没有指望在院里发现您站在我们一边。

**富兰克林**：我谈政治的时候，卢宾先生，想的不是选举，不是现成的席位，不是党的经费，不是选民册，甚至——很遗憾得补一句——也不是眼下这个样子的议会。您与其谈拉票，还不如谈桥牌：两样里头桥牌更有趣些。

**伯奇**：他要谈原则，卢宾。

**卢宾**：*（又冷又清。）*巴纳巴斯先生的意思我完全明白。可选举是没定的事；原则是定了的事。

**康拉德**：*（不耐烦。）*老天爷——

**卢宾**：*（以安静的威严打断他。）*稍待，巴纳巴斯博士。现代文明社会所据以建立的主要原则，在受过教育的人中间是相当清楚的。不明白这一点的，是我们那些危险地半受教育的大众，和他们宠信的煽动家——这话若伯奇不见怪——

**伯奇**：别管我。往下说。回头我有的说。

===Original===

**Lubin**：That is what our dangerously half-educated people do not realize. Take all this fuss about the Labor Party, with its imaginary new principles and new politics. The Labor members will find that the immutable laws of political economy take no more notice of their ambitions and aspirations than the law of gravitation. I speak, if I may say so, with knowledge; for I have made a special, study of the Labor question.

**Franklyn**：*（With interest and some surprise.）* Indeed?

**Lubin**：Yes. It occurred quite at the beginning of my career. I was asked to deliver an address to the students at the Working Men’s College; and I was strongly advised to comply, as Gladstone and Morley and others were doing that sort of thing at the moment. It was rather a troublesome job, because I had not gone into political economy at the time. As you know, at the university I was a classical scholar; and my profession was the Law. But I looked up the textbooks, and got up the case most carefully. I found that the correct view is that all this Trade Unionism and Socialism and so forth is founded on the ignorant delusion that wages and the production and distribution of wealth can be controlled by legislation or by any human action whatever. They obey fixed scientific laws, which have been ascertained and settled finally by the highest economic authorities. Naturally I do not at this distance of time remember the exact process of reasoning; but I can get up the case again at any time in a couple of days; and you may rely on me absolutely, should the occasion arise, to deal with all these ignorant and unpractical people in a conclusive and convincing way, except, of course, as far as it may be advisable to indulge and flatter them a little so as to let them down without creating ill feeling in the working-class electorate. In short, I can get that lecture up again almost at a moment’s notice.

**Savvy**：But, Mr. Lubin, I have had a university education too; and all this about wages and distribution being fixed by immutable laws of political economy is obsolete rot.

**Franklyn**：*（Shocked.）* Oh, my dear! That is not polite.

**Lubin**：No, no, no. Don’t scold her. She mustn’t be scolded. *（To **Savvy**.）* I understand. You are a disciple of Karl Marx.

**Savvy**：No, no. Karl Marx’s economics are all rot.

**Lubin**：*（At last a little taken aback.）* Dear me!

===Chinese===

**卢宾**：不明白这一点的，正是我们这些危险地半受教育的人。就拿工党闹的这一场来说，连同它那些想象出来的新原则、新政治。工党议员们会发现，政治经济学的铁律对他们的大志和热望，会同万有引力定律一样不予理睬。恕我自夸，我是有根有据地说话；因为劳工问题，我下过专门功夫。

**富兰克林**：*（又感兴趣又惊讶。）*当真？

**卢宾**：当真。那还是我生涯的开头。有人请我去工人学院对学生演说；人家极力劝我应下，因为格拉德斯通啦、莫利啦，那阵子都在做这类事。这差事相当麻烦，因为那时我还没钻过政治经济学。您知道的，在大学里我是古典学者；我的本行是法律。可我翻了教本，把案情细细备了课。我发现，正统的观点是：这一整套工会主义、社会主义等等，都建立在一个无知的妄念上——以为工资和财富的生产与分配可以靠立法或任何人为的行动来控制。它们服从固定的科学规律，这些规律已由最高级的经济权威一锤定音。时隔这么久，推理的详细步骤我自然记不得了；可只要两天，我随时能把这案子重新备出来；一旦需要，尽管放心，我能把这些无知而不切实际的人对付得结论确凿、令人信服——当然，多多少少还得迁就他们、奉承他们一点，好把他们安顿下来，不叫工人阶级选民生怨。总而言之，这场演说我几乎随叫随到，都能重新备齐。

**萨维**：可是卢宾先生，我也受过大学教育；这套工资和分配由政治经济学铁律定死的说法，是过时的臭大粪。

**富兰克林**：*（吓一跳。）*哦，亲爱的！这可失礼。

**卢宾**：不，不，不。别训她。不许训她。*（对**萨维**。）*我懂了。您是卡尔·马克思的信徒。

**萨维**：不不。卡尔·马克思的经济学也全是臭大粪。

**卢宾**：*（到底有点吃了一惊。）*哎呀！

===Original===

**Savvy**：You must excuse me, Mr. Lubin; but it’s like hearing a man talk about the Garden of Eden.

**Conrad**：Why shouldn’t he talk about the Garden of Eden? It was a first attempt at biology anyhow.

**Lubin**：*（Recovering his self-possession.）* I am sound on the Garden of Eden. I have heard of Darwin.

**Savvy**：But Darwin is all rot.

**Lubin**：What! Already!

**Savvy**：It’s no good your smiling at me like a Cheshire cat, Mr. Lubin; and I am not going to sit here mumchance like an old-fashioned goody goody wife while you men monopolize the conversation and pay out the very ghastliest exploded drivel as the latest thing in politics. I am not giving you my own ideas, Mr. Lubin, but just the regular orthodox science of today. Only the most awful old fossils think that Socialism is bad economics and that Darwin invented Evolution. Ask Papa. Ask Uncle. Ask the first person you meet in the street. *（She rises and crosses to **Haslam**.）* Give me a cigarette, Bill, will you?

**Haslam**：Priceless. *（He complies.）*

**Franklyn**：Savvy has not lived long enough to have any manners, Mr. Lubin; but that is where you stand with the younger generation. Don’t smoke, dear.

**Lubin**：*（Shrewd and serious.）* Mr. Barnabas: I confess I am surprised; and I will not pretend that I am convinced. But I am open to conviction. I may be wrong.

**Burge**：*（In a burst of irony.）* Oh no. Impossible! Impossible!

**Lubin**：Yes, Mr. Barnabas, though I do not possess Burge’s genius for being always wrong, I have been in that position once or twice. I could not conceal from you, even if I wished to, that my time has been so completely filled by my professional work as a lawyer, and later on by my duties as leader of the House of Commons in the days when Prime Ministers were also leaders﻿—

**Burge**：*（Stung.）* Not to mention bridge and smart society.

===Chinese===

**萨维**：您得包涵，卢宾先生；可听着这个，就像听人谈论伊甸园。

**康拉德**：他怎么就不能谈伊甸园？那反正是生物学的第一次尝试。

**卢宾**：*（恢复自持。）*伊甸园我是稳的。达尔文我是听说过的。

**萨维**：可达尔文也全是臭大粪。

**卢宾**：什么！这就来了！

**萨维**：您别冲我笑得像只柴郡猫，卢宾先生；我也不打算像旧式贤惠太太那样闷坐不言语，由着你们男人包办谈话，把顶吓死人的、早就炸碎了的老生常谈，当作政治上的最新鲜货色端出来。我给您的不是我自己的主意，卢宾先生，只是如今正规的正统科学。只有顶要命的老化石，才还以为社会主义是坏经济学、达尔文发明了进化论。问爸爸。问叔叔。问您在街上碰到的第一个人。*（她起身走到**哈斯拉姆**跟前。）*给我支烟，比尔，行吗？

**哈斯拉姆**：绝了。*（他照办。）*

**富兰克林**：萨维活得还不够长，还没来得及长出教养，卢宾先生；可这正是您在年轻一辈那里的处境。别抽烟，亲爱的。

**卢宾**：*（精明而郑重。）*巴纳巴斯先生：我承认我很惊讶；我也不假装被说服了。可我愿意接受说服。我也许错了。

**伯奇**：*（一阵讥讽。）*哦不。不可能！不可能！

**卢宾**：是的，巴纳巴斯先生，我虽没有伯奇那种永远不错的天才，这样的处境我也赶上过一两回。这一点我瞞不了您，即使想瞞：我的时间一直被我的律师本行填得满满当当，后来又被下议院领袖的职务填满——那是首相还兼做领袖的年代——

**伯奇**：*（被刺着。）*更别提桥牌和时髦社交了。

===Original===

**Lubin**：Not to mention the continual and trying effort to make Burge behave himself, that I have not been able to keep my academic reading up to date. I have kept my classics brushed up out of sheer love for them; but my economics and my science, such as they were, may possibly be a little rusty. Yet I think I may say that if you and your brother will be so good as to put me on the track of the necessary documents, I will undertake to put the case to the House or to the country to your entire satisfaction. You see, as long as you can show these troublesome half-educated people who want to turn the world upside down that they are talking nonsense, it really does not matter very much whether you do it in terms of what Miss Barnabas calls obsolete rot or in terms of what her granddaughter will probably call unmitigated tosh. I have no objection whatever to denounce Karl Marx. Anything I can say against Darwin will please a large body of sincerely pious voters. If it will be easier to carry on the business of the country on the understanding that the present state of things is to be called Socialism, I have no objection in the world to call it Socialism. There is the precedent of the Emperor Constantine, who saved the society of his own day by agreeing to call his Imperialism Christianity. Mind: I must not go ahead of the electorate. You must not call a voter a Socialist until﻿—

**Franklyn**：Until he is a Socialist. Agreed.

**Lubin**：Oh, not at all. You need not wait for that. You must not call him a Socialist until he wishes to be called a Socialist: that is all. Surely you would not say that I must not address my constituents as gentlemen until they are gentlemen. I address them as gentlemen because they wish to be so addressed. *（He rises from the sofa and goes to **Franklyn**, placing a reassuring hand on his shoulder.）* Do not be afraid of Socialism, Mr. Barnabas. You need not tremble for your property or your position or your dignity. England will remain what England is, no matter what new political names may come into vogue. I do not intend to resist the transition to Socialism. You may depend on me to guide it, to lead it, to give suitable expression to its aspirations, and to steer it clear of Utopian absurdities. I can honestly ask for your support on the most advanced Socialist grounds no less than on the soundest Liberal ones.

===Chinese===

**卢宾**：更别提还要不断地、费劲地管着伯奇守规矩，我的学术阅读便一直没能跟上时代。古典作品我出于纯粹的爱还时时温习；可我的经济学和我的科学，本来就那么一点，如今也许有点生锈了。不过我想我可以说：只要您和令弟肯行个好，把我引上必要文献的门径，我担保把这桩案子向议院、向全国陈词，陈得二位完全满意。您看，只要你能叫这批想把世界翻个身的、麻烦的半受教育的人明白他们在胡说，那么用的是巴纳巴斯小姐所谓的「过时臭话」、还是用她的孙女儿将来多半会叫的「彻头彻尾的胡扯」，其实没有多大关系。抨击卡尔·马克思，我毫无异议。我能反达尔文说的话，会讨一大群虔敬的选民欢喜。倘若把国事照旧办下去、只须把现状改称社会主义更方便些，我也毫无异议就叫它社会主义。先例有的是：君士坦丁皇帝，就是靠同意把他的帝政称作基督教，救下了他那个时代的社会。请注意：我不可跑到选民前头去。你不可把一个选民叫作社会主义者，直到——

**富兰克林**：直到他真是社会主义者。同意。

**卢宾**：哦，完全不必等到那时候。你不可叫他社会主义者，直到他愿意被叫作社会主义者：就这么回事。您总不至于说，我的选民们在当上绅士之前，我就不可称他们为绅士吧。我称他们绅士，因为他们愿意被这样称呼。*（他从长榻上起身，走向**富兰克林**，把一只叫他安心的手搭在他肩上。）*别怕社会主义，巴纳巴斯先生。您不必为您的财产、您的地位、您的体面发抖。英格兰还会是英格兰，不管时兴什么新的政治名目。我不打算抗拒向社会主义的过渡。您可以放心依靠我来引导它、领导它、替它的热望配上得体的说法，并且驾着它躲开乌托邦的荒唐。我完全可以凭最先进的社会主义立场，同凭最稳健的自由党立场一样，诚心请求您的支持。

===Original===

**Burge**：In short, Lubin, you’re incorrigible. You don’t believe anything is going to change. The millions are still to toil﻿—the people﻿—my people﻿—for I am a man of the people﻿—

**Lubin**：*（Interrupting him contemptuously.）* Don’t be ridiculous, Burge. You are a country solicitor, further removed from the people, more foreign to them, more jealous of letting them up to your level, than any duke or any archbishop.

**Burge**：*（Hotly.）* I deny it. You think I have never been poor. You think I have never cleaned my own boots. You think my fingers have never come out through the soles when I was cleaning them. You think﻿—

**Lubin**：I think you fall into the very common mistake of supposing that it is poverty that makes the proletarian and money that makes the gentleman. You are quite wrong. You never belonged to the people: you belonged to the impecunious. Impecuniosity and broken boots are the lot of the unsuccessful middle class, and the commonplaces of the early struggles of the professional and younger son class. I defy you to find a farm laborer in England with broken boots. Call a mechanic one of the poor, and he’ll punch your head. When you talk to your constituents about the toiling millions, they don’t consider that you are referring to them. They are all third cousins of somebody with a title or a park. I am a Yorkshireman, my friend. I know England; and you don’t. If you did you would know﻿—

**Burge**：What do you know that I don’t know?

**Lubin**：I know that we are taking up too much of Mr. Barnabas’s time. *（**Franklyn** rises.）* May I take it, my dear Barnabas, that I may count on your support if we succeed in forcing an election before the new register is in full working order?

**Burge**：*（Rising also.）* May the party count on your support? I say nothing about myself. Can the party depend on you? Is there any question of yours that I have left unanswered?

**Conrad**：We haven’t asked you any, you know.

**Burge**：May I take that as a mark of confidence?

**Conrad**：If I were a laborer in your constituency, I should ask you a biological question?

**Lubin**：No you wouldn’t, my dear Doctor. Laborers never ask questions.

**Burge**：Ask it now. I have never flinched from being heckled. Out with it. Is it about the land?

**Conrad**：No.

**Burge**：Is it about the Church?

**Conrad**：No.

**Burge**：Is it about the House of Lords?

===Chinese===

**伯奇**：总之，卢宾，你不可救药。你什么都不信会变。千百万照旧受苦受累——人民——我的人民——因为我就是人民的人——

**卢宾**：*（轻蔑地打断他。）*别可笑啦，伯奇。你是个乡下事务律师，比任何公爵、任何大主教都离人民更远、更隔膜、更不肯放他们上来同你平起平坐。

**伯奇**：*（火爆地。）*我否认。你以为我没穷过。你以为我没自己擦过靴子。你以为我擦靴子的时候，手指头没从靴底里顶出来过。你以为——

**卢宾**：我以为你犯了一个顶普通的错误：以为造成无产者的是贫穷，造成绅士的是金钱。你大错特错。你从来不属于人民：你属于没钱的阶级。没钱和破靴子，是不成功的中产阶级的命，是专业人士和次子阶级早年挣扎的老生常谈。你敢在英国找一个穿破靴子的农业工人吗。管一个机修工叫穷人，他一拳打爆你的头。你对你的选民谈千百万劳苦大众，他们并不认为你说的是他们。他们个个是哪个有爵位、有园子的人的三代表亲。我是约克郡人，我的朋友。我懂英格兰；你不懂。你要是懂，你就会知道——

**伯奇**：你知道什么我不知道的？

**卢宾**：我知道我们占巴纳巴斯先生的时间太多了。*（**富兰克林**起身。）*我是否可以这么理解，亲爱的巴纳巴斯：倘若我们赶在新选民册全部生效之前成功地促成一次大选，我可以指望您的支持？

**伯奇**：*（也起身。）*党可以指望您的支持吗？我自己一字不提。党能指望您吗？您有什么问题我没答的吗？

**康拉德**：您知道，我们一个问题也没问过您。

**伯奇**：我能把这看作信任的表示吗？

**康拉德**：我要是您选区里的一个雇工，我会问您一个生物学的问题。

**卢宾**：不你不会，亲爱的博士。雇工从来不问问题。

**伯奇**：现在就问。我从不怕人起哄。说吧。是关于土地的？

**康拉德**：不是。

**伯奇**：是关于教会的？

**康拉德**：不是。

**伯奇**：是关于上议院的？

===Original===

**Conrad**：No.

**Burge**：Is it about Proportional Representation?

**Conrad**：No.

**Burge**：Is it about Free Trade?

**Conrad**：No.

**Burge**：Is it about the priest in the school?

**Conrad**：No.

**Burge**：Is it about Ireland?

**Conrad**：No.

**Burge**：Is it about Germany?

**Conrad**：No.

**Burge**：Well, is it about Republicanism? Come! I won’t flinch. Is it about the Monarchy?

**Conrad**：No.

**Burge**：Well, what the devil is it about, then?

**Conrad**：You understand that I am asking the question in the character of a laborer who earned thirteen shillings a week before the war and earns thirty now, when he can get it?

**Burge**：Yes: I understand that. I am ready for you. Out with it.

**Conrad**：And whom you propose to represent in parliament?

**Burge**：Yes, yes, yes. Come on.

**Conrad**：The question is this. Would you allow your son to marry my daughter, or your daughter to marry my son?

**Burge**：*（Taken aback.）* Oh, come! That’s not a political question.

**Conrad**：Then, as a biologist, I don’t take the slightest interest in your politics; and I shall not walk across the street to vote for you or anyone else at the election. Good evening.

**Lubin**：Serve you right, Burge! Dr. Barnabas: you have my assurance that my daughter shall marry the man of her choice, whether he be lord or laborer. May *I* count on your support?

**Burge**：*（Hurling the epithet at him.）* Humbug!

**Savvy**：Stop. *（They all stop short in the movement of leave-taking to look at her.）* Daddy: are you going to let them off like this? How are they to know anything if nobody ever tells them? If you don’t, I will.

**Conrad**：You can’t. You didn’t read my book; and you know nothing about it. You just hold your tongue.

**Savvy**：I just won’t, Nunk. I shall have a vote when I am thirty; and I ought to have it now. Why are these two ridiculous people to be allowed to come in and walk over us as if the world existed only to play their silly parliamentary game?

**Franklyn**：*（Severely.）* Savvy: you really must not be uncivil to our guests.

===Chinese===

**康拉德**：不是。

**伯奇**：是关于比例代表制的？

**康拉德**：不是。

**伯奇**：是关于自由贸易的？

**康拉德**：不是。

**伯奇**：是关于学校里的教士的？

**康拉德**：不是。

**伯奇**：是关于爱尔兰的？

**康拉德**：不是。

**伯奇**：是关于德国的？

**康拉德**：不是。

**伯奇**：那，是关于共和主义的？来吧！我不怕。是关于君主制的？

**康拉德**：不是。

**伯奇**：那，它到底是关于什么的？

**康拉德**：您明白，我这是以一个雇工的身份在问：战前他一星期挣十三先令，如今挣三十，还要挣得着的时候。

**伯奇**：是：这我明白。我候着您呢。说吧。

**康拉德**：也是以您打算在议会里代表的人的身份？

**伯奇**：是，是，是。来吧。

**康拉德**：问题是这样的。您会允许您的儿子娶我的女儿，或者您的女儿嫁我的儿子吗？

**伯奇**：*（猝不及防。）*哦，得了！这可不是政治问题。

**康拉德**：那么，作为一个生物学家，我对您的政治就毫无兴趣；大选的时候，我也不会为您或者任何别人横过一条街去投票。晚安。

**卢宾**：活该，伯奇！巴纳巴斯博士：我向您保证，我的女儿要嫁哪个男人就嫁哪个男人，管他是勋爵还是雇工。**我**可以指望您的支持吗？

**伯奇**：*（把这个词劈头掷去。）*伪君子！

**萨维**：慢着。*（众人都在告辞的动势里篱地停住，看她。）*爸爸：你就这样放他们过门？要是没有人告诉他们，他们怎么会知道什么？你不讲，我讲。

**康拉德**：你讲不了。你没读我的书；你对此一无所知。闭上你的嘴。

**萨维**：我就不闭，努克。我到三十岁就有一张选票了；而且现在就该有我一张。凭什么放这两个可笑的人进来，从我们身上踏过去，好像这世界生下来就只为玩他们那套愚蠢的议会把戏？

**富兰克林**：*（严厉地。）*萨维：对客人不可无礼，这是真真不可的。

===Original===

**Savvy**：I’m sorry. But Mr. Lubin didn’t stand on much ceremony with me, did he? And Mr. Burge hasn’t addressed a single word to me. I’m not going to stand it. You and Nunk have a much better program than either of them. It’s the only one we are going to vote for; and they ought to be told about it for the credit of the family and the good of their own souls. You just tip them a chapter from *The Gospel of the Brothers Barnabas*, Daddy.

**Franklyn**：It is quite true, Mr. Lubin, that I and my brother have a little program of our own which﻿—

**Conrad**：*（Interrupting.）* It’s not a little program: it’s an almighty big one. It’s not our own: it’s the program of the whole of civilization.

**Burge**：Then why split the party before you have put it to us? For God’s sake let us have no more splits. I am here to learn. I am here to gather your opinions and represent them. I invite you to put your views before me. I offer myself to be heckled. You have asked me only an absurd nonpolitical question.

**Franklyn**：Candidly, I fear our program will be thrown away on you. It would not interest you.

**Burge**：*（With challenging audacity.）* Try. Lubin can go if he likes; but I am still open to new ideas, if only I can find them.

**Franklyn**：*（To **Lubin**.）* Are you prepared to listen, Mr. Lubin; or shall I thank you for your very kind and welcome visit, and say good evening?

**Lubin**：*（Sitting down resignedly on the settee, but involuntarily making a movement which looks like the stifling of a yawn.）* With pleasure, Mr. Barnabas. Of course you know that before I can adopt any new plank in the party platform, it will have to reach me through the National Liberal Federation, which you can approach through your local Liberal and Radical Association.

**Franklyn**：I could recall to you several instances of the addition to your party program of measures of which no local branch of your Federation had ever dreamt. But I understand that you are not really interested. I will spare you, and drop the subject.

**Lubin**：*（Waking up a little.）* You quite misunderstand me. Please do not take it in that way. I only﻿—

**Burge**：*（Talking him down.）* Never mind the Federation: *I* will answer for the Federation. Go on, Barnabas: go on. Never mind Lubin. *（He sits down in the chair from which **Lubin** first displaced him.）*

===Chinese===

**萨维**：对不起。可是卢宾先生同我也没讲多少客气，是不是？伯奇先生更是一句都没对我讲过。我不受这个。你和努克有个比他们俩哪个都强得多的纲领。我们将来投的票只投它；为了这个家的体面、也为了他们自己灵魂的好处，也该讲给他们听听。爸爸，你就从《巴纳巴斯兄弟的福音》里给他们倒一章。

**富兰克林**：千真万确，卢宾先生，我和我弟弟是有一套我们自己的小纲领，它——

**康拉德**：*（打断。）*不是小纲领：是一套其大无比的纲领。也不是我们的：是整个文明的纲领。

**伯奇**：那为什么不先摆给我们，就先分裂党？看在上帝分上，别再分裂了。我是来学习的。我是来收集你们的意见、再拿去代表的。我请你们把看法摆到我面前。我把自己交出来任人起哄。你们只问了我一个荒唐的非政治问题。

**富兰克林**：坦白说，我怕我们的纲领在你们身上是白扔。它引不起你们的兴趣。

**伯奇**：*（挑战般地放胆。）*试试看。卢宾要走随他；我还对新思想敞开着门，只要我找得着它们。

**富兰克林**：*（对**卢宾**。）*您预备听吗，卢宾先生？还是我谢过您这次极其好客的造访，道晚安？

**卢宾**：*（听天由命地在长榻上坐下，却下意识地做了个像是把呵欠摔回去的动作。）*乐意之至，巴纳巴斯先生。您当然知道，党的纲领凡要添新条目，都得通过全国自由党联合会到我这里，而联合会呢，您可以通过您那里的自由党和激进派协会去接近。

**富兰克林**：贵党纲领添过的那些条目，我随时可以给您举出几件，贵会哪个地方分会都做梦没想到过。不过我明白您实在没有兴趣。我体恤您，这个题目不谈了。

**卢宾**：*（略略醒了一点。）*您完全误会我了。请千万别那样想。我只是——

**伯奇**：*（用话把他压下去。）*别管联合会：**我**替联合会担保。往下讲，巴纳巴斯：往下讲。别管卢宾。*（他在**卢宾**先把他挤掉的那把椅子上坐下。）*

===Original===

**Franklyn**：Our program is only that the term of human life shall be extended to three hundred years.

**Lubin**：*（Softly.）* Eh?

**Burge**：*（Explosively.）* What!

**Savvy**：Our election cry is “Back to Methuselah!”

**Haslam**：Priceless!

**Conrad**：No. We are not mad.

**Savvy**：They’re not joking either. They mean it.

**Lubin**：*（Cautiously.）* Assuming that, in some sense which I am for the moment unable to fathom, you are in earnest, Mr. Barnabas, may I ask what this has to do with politics?

**Franklyn**：The connection is very evident. You are now, Mr. Lubin, within immediate reach of your seventieth year. Mr. Joyce Burge is your junior by about eleven years. You will go down to posterity as one of a European group of immature statesmen and monarchs who, doing the very best for your respective countries of which you were capable, succeeded in all-but-wrecking the civilization of Europe, and did, in effect, wipe out of existence many millions of its inhabitants.

**Burge**：Less than a million.

**Franklyn**：That was our loss alone.

**Burge**：Oh, if you count foreigners﻿—!

**Haslam**：God counts foreigners, you know.

**Savvy**：*（With intense satisfaction.）* Well said, Bill.

**Franklyn**：I am not blaming you. Your task was beyond human capacity. What with our huge armaments, our terrible engines of destruction, our systems of coercion manned by an irresistible police, you were called on to control powers so gigantic that one shudders at the thought of their being entrusted even to an infinitely experienced and benevolent God, much less to mortal men whose whole life does not last a hundred years.

**Burge**：We won the war: don’t forget that.

**Franklyn**：No: the soldiers and sailors won it, and left you to finish it. And you were so utterly incompetent that the multitudes of children slain by hunger in the first years of peace made us all wish we were at war again.

**Conrad**：It’s no use arguing about it. It is now absolutely certain that the political and social problems raised by our civilization cannot be solved by mere human mushrooms who decay and die when they are just beginning to have a glimmer of the wisdom and knowledge needed for their own government.

**Lubin**：Quite an interesting idea, Doctor. Extravagant. Fantastic. But quite interesting. When I was young I used to feel my human limitations very acutely.

===Chinese===

**富兰克林**：我们的纲领只有一条：人的寿数要延长到三百年。

**卢宾**：*（轻轻地。）*嗯？

**伯奇**：*（炸了。）*什么！

**萨维**：我们的竞选口号是「回到玛土撒拉！」

**哈斯拉姆**：绝了！

**康拉德**：不。我们没有疯。

**萨维**：他们也不是开玩笑。他们是认真的。

**卢宾**：*（谨慎地。）*姑且假定，巴纳巴斯先生，您是认真的——这层意思我一时还参不透——可否请问，这同政治有什么相干？

**富兰克林**：关联明摆着。卢宾先生，您如今离七十只有一眨眼了。乔伊斯·伯奇先生比您小十一岁上下。你们留给后世的形象，将是欧洲一群不成熟的政治家和君主：人人给自己的国家出了自己能出的全力，结果险些把欧洲的文明彻底掀翻，而且事实上，确实把它几百万居民从存在里抹掉了。

**伯奇**：不到一百万。

**富兰克林**：那只是我们一国的损失。

**伯奇**：哦，要是连外国人也算上——！

**哈斯拉姆**：您知道的，上帝是算外国人的。

**萨维**：*（极度满意。）*说得好，比尔。

**富兰克林**：我不是怪你们。你们的任务本非人力所能胜任。你们要驾驭的，是我们庞大的军备、可怕的毁灭机器、由一支不可抗拒的警察队伍操纵的强制体系，那权力大得一想到把它托付给一位无限经验和无限仁慈的上帝都叫人发抖，何况托付给一批满打满算活不过百年的凡人。

**伯奇**：仗是我们打赢的：别忘了这个。

**富兰克林**：不：打赢它的是士兵和水兵，他们打赢后把收尾留给了你们。而你们无能到这般地步：和平头几年里饿死的孩子成群，叫我们人人巴不得重新开战。

**康拉德**：争这个没有用。如今可以完全确定：我们的文明提出的种种政治问题和社会问题，光靠一批朝生暮死的人蘑菇是解不了的——他们刚对自治所需的那点智慧和知识开始有点微光，就腐朽了、死了。

**卢宾**：相当有趣的想法，博士。夸张。离奇。但相当有趣。我年轻时也常常痛感到自己人的限度。

===Original===

**Burge**：God knows I have often felt that I could not go on if it had not been for the sense that I was only an instrument in the hands of a Power above us.

**Conrad**：I’m glad you both agree with us, and with one another.

**Lubin**：I have not gone so far as that, I think. After all, we have had many very able political leaders even within your recollection and mine.

**Franklyn**：Have you read the recent biographies﻿—Dilke’s, for instance﻿—which revealed the truth about them?

**Lubin**：I did not discover any new truth revealed in these books, Mr. Barnabas.

**Franklyn**：What! Not the truth that England was governed all that time by a little woman who knew her own mind?

**Savvy**：Hear, hear!

**Lubin**：That often happens. Which woman do you mean?

**Franklyn**：Queen Victoria, to whom your Prime Ministers stood in the relation of naughty children whose heads she knocked together when their tempers and quarrels became intolerable. Within thirteen years of her death Europe became a hell.

**Burge**：Quite true. That was because she was piously brought up, and regarded herself as an instrument. If a statesman remembers that he is only an instrument, and feels quite sure that he is rightly interpreting the divine purpose, he will come out all right, you know.

**Franklyn**：The Kaiser felt like that. Did he come out all right?

**Burge**：Well, let us be fair, even to the Kaiser. Let us be fair.

**Franklyn**：Were you fair to him when you won an election on the program of hanging him?

**Burge**：Stuff! I am the last man alive to hang anybody; but the people wouldn’t listen to reason. Besides, I knew the Dutch wouldn’t give him up.

**Savvy**：Oh, don’t start arguing about poor old Bill. Stick to our point. Let these two gentlemen settle the question for themselves. Mr. Burge: do you think Mr. Lubin is fit to govern England?

**Burge**：No. Frankly, I don’t.

**Lubin**：*（Remonstrant.）* Really!

**Conrad**：Why?

**Burge**：Because he has no conscience: that’s why.

**Lubin**：*（Shocked and amazed.）* Oh!

**Franklyn**：Mr. Lubin: do you consider Joyce Burge qualified to govern England?

===Chinese===

**伯奇**：上帝知道，若不是觉得自己不过是一桩高于我们的伟力手里的工具，我常常会觉得撑不下去了。

**康拉德**：你们两位都同意我们、也彼此同意了，我很高兴。

**卢宾**：我想我还没走到那一步。毕竟，就在你我记得起的年月里，我们就有过许多很能干的政治领袖。

**富兰克林**：近来的传记您读过吗——比如迪尔克的？把他们的真相揭出来了。

**卢宾**：我没有在这些书里发现什么新的真相，巴纳巴斯先生。

**富兰克林**：什么！那个真相您没发现——英格兰那些年原来是由一位很有主见的小妇人统治着的？

**萨维**：好哇，好哇！

**卢宾**：那是常事。您指哪位妇人？

**富兰克林**：维多利亚女王。你们的各位首相在她跟前，就是一群顽皮孩子，闹得不像话时，叫她拎着脑袋相撞。她死后不出十三年，欧洲变成了地狱。

**伯奇**：很对。那是因为她受过虔敬的教养，把自己看作一件工具。一位政治家只要记得自己只是工具，而且确信自己正确地解读了上天的旨意，他就会有好结果，您知道的。

**富兰克林**：德皇也是这么觉得的。他的结果好吗？

**伯奇**：呃，我们公道些，连对德皇也要公道。公道些吧。

**富兰克林**：你们拿绞死他当纲领赢得大选的时候，对他公道了吗？

**伯奇**：胡扯！我是世上最后一个肯绞人的人；可人民不听道理。再说，我知道荷兰人不会交出他来。

**萨维**：哦，别去可怜的老比尔身上绕了。扣住咱们的正题。让这两位先生自己把问题解决了。伯奇先生：您觉得卢宾先生配治理英格兰吗？

**伯奇**：不。坦白说，不配。

**卢宾**：*（抗议。）*岂有此理！

**康拉德**：为什么？

**伯奇**：因为他没有良心：就这么回事。

**卢宾**：*（又惊又骇。）*哦！

**富兰克林**：卢宾先生：您认为乔伊斯·伯奇配治理英格兰吗？

===Original===

**Lubin**：*（With dignified emotion, wounded, but without bitterness.）* Excuse me, Mr. Barnabas; but before I answer that question I want to say this. Burge: we have had differences of opinion; and your newspaper friends have said hard things of me. But we worked together for years; and I hope I have done nothing to justify you in the amazing accusation you have just brought against me. Do you realize that you said that I have no conscience?

**Burge**：Lubin: I am very accessible to an appeal to my emotions; and you are very cunning in making such appeals. I will meet you to this extent. I don’t mean that you are a bad man. I don’t mean that I dislike you, in spite of your continual attempts to discourage and depress me. But you have a mind like a looking-glass. You are very clear and smooth and lucid as to what is standing in front of you. But you have no foresight and no hindsight. You have no vision and no memory. You have no continuity; and a man without continuity can have neither conscience nor honor from one day to another. The result is that you have always been a damned bad minister; and you have sometimes been a damned bad friend. Now you can answer Barnabas’s question and take it out of me to your heart’s content. He asked you was I fit to govern England.

**Lubin**：*（Recovering himself.）* After what has just passed I sincerely wish I could honestly say yes, Burge. But it seems to me that you have condemned yourself out of your own mouth. You represent something which has had far too much influence and popularity in this country since Joseph Chamberlain set the fashion; and that is mere energy without intellect and without knowledge. Your mind is not a trained mind: it has not been stored with the best information, nor cultivated by intercourse with educated minds at any of our great seats of learning. As I happen to have enjoyed that advantage, it follows that you do not understand my mind. Candidly, I think that disqualifies you. The peace found out your weaknesses.

**Burge**：Oh! What did it find out in you?

**Lubin**：You and your newspaper confederates took the peace out of my hands. The peace did not find me out because it did not find me in.

===Chinese===

**卢宾**：*（带着庄重的激动，受了伤，却不怀怨恨。）*对不起，巴纳巴斯先生；回答这个问题之前，我想说一句。伯奇：我们有过意见相左；你的报界朋友说过我的难听话。可我们共事多年；我希望我没有做过什么，叫你刚才那惊人的指控站得住。你可意识到你说我没有良心？

**伯奇**：卢宾：我的感情最容易被打动；而你最会打动人的感情。我就让到这一步。我不是说你是个坏人。也不是说我不喜欢你，尽管你不断泼我冷水、叫我泄气。可是你的头脑像一面镜子。眼前立着什么，你照得又清楚、又平滑、又透亮。可是你没有前见，也没有后见。你没有远视，也没有记忆。你没有连续性；而没有连续性的人，今天到明天就既不会有良心，也不会有体面。结果是，你一直是个糟糕透顶的大臣；有几回还是个糟糕透顶的朋友。现在你可以回答巴纳巴斯的问题，把我尽情骂回来了。他问的是我配不配治理英格兰。

**卢宾**：*（定住神。）*经过方才这一场，我真心希望自己能老老实实说声「配」，伯奇。可在我看，你已用自己的嘴定了自己的罪。你代表的是自约瑟夫·张伯伦开风气以来，在这个国家影响太大、太走红的一样东西：光有干劲，没有头脑，没有学问。你的头脑不是受过训练的头脑：既不曾装满最好的资讯，也不曾在我们哪所大学府里同受过教育的头脑砰砺切磋过。我碰巧享有过那份好处，于是你便不懂我的头脑。坦白说，我认为这一点就使你不合格。和平把你的短处查出来了。

**伯奇**：哦！和平在你身上查出了什么？

**卢宾**：你和你的报界同伙把和平从我手里夺走了。和平没有查出我的底，因为它压根没找着我。

===Original===

**Franklyn**：Come! Confess, both of you! You were only flies on the wheel. The war went England’s way; but the peace went its own way, and not England’s way nor any of the ways you had so glibly appointed for it. Your peace treaty was a scrap of paper before the ink dried on it. The statesmen of Europe were incapable of governing Europe. What they needed was a couple of hundred years training and experience: what they had actually had was a few years at the bar or in a countinghouse or on the grouse moors and golf courses. And now we are waiting, with monster cannons trained on every city and seaport, and huge aeroplanes ready to spring into the air and drop bombs every one of which will obliterate a whole street, and poison gases that will strike multitudes dead with a breath, until one of you gentlemen rises in his helplessness to tell us, who are as helpless as himself, that we are at war again.

**Conrad**：Aha! What consolation will it be for us then that you two are able to tell off one another’s defects so cleverly in your afternoon chat?

**Burge**：*（Angrily.）* If you come to that, what consolation will it be that you two can sit there and tell both of us off? you, who have had no responsibility! you, who haven’t lifted a finger, as far as I know, to help us through this awful crisis which has left me ten years older than my proper age! Can you tell me a single thing you did to help us during the whole infernal business?

**Conrad**：We’re not blaming you: you hadn’t lived long enough. No more had we. Can’t you see that threescore-and-ten, though it may be long enough for a very crude sort of village life, isn’t long enough for a complicated civilization like ours? Flinders Petrie has counted nine attempts at civilization made by people exactly like us; and every one of them failed just as ours is failing. They failed because the citizens and statesmen died of old age or overeating before they had grown out of schoolboy games and savage sports and cigars and champagne. The signs of the end are always the same: Democracy, Socialism, and Votes for Women. We shall go to smash within the lifetime of men now living unless we recognize that we must live longer.

**Lubin**：I am glad you agree with me that Socialism and Votes for Women are signs of decay.

===Chinese===

**富兰克林**：来！二位，招了吧！你们不过是轮子上的苍蝇。战争顺着英格兰的路走了；和平却走了它自己的路，既不是英格兰的路，也不是你们随口替它指派的任何一条路。你们的和约，墨迹未干就已经是一纸空文。欧洲的政治家们没有能力治理欧洲。他们需要的是两百年的训练和历练：他们实际有的，是在律师楼或账房里的几年，或在松鸡荒原和高尔夫球场上的几年。而此刻我们正等着——巨炮瞄着每一座城市和海港，庞大的飞机随时准备腾空投弹，每一颗都能抹掉一整条街，毒气一口就能扫倒大批的人——等你们二位中哪一位，在他那无能为力里站起来，告诉我们这些同他一样无能为力的人：我们又开战了。

**康拉德**：啊哈！到那时候，你们二位能在午后的闲谈里这样伶牙俐齿地数说彼此的毛病，这对我们又算什么安慰呢？

**伯奇**：*（怒。）*要说这个，你们二位坐在那儿把我们俩一并数说，又算什么安慰？你们，没有担过一点责任的！你们，据我所知，连一根指头也没抬过，没帮我们挨过这场把我熬老了十岁的可怕的危机！这整个混账买卖里，你们为帮我们做过一件事吗？说一件给我听？

**康拉德**：我们不怪你们：你们活得不够长。我们也不够。你们难道看不出，人生七十，对付一种顶粗糙的乡村生活也许够了，对付我们这样繁复的文明是不够的？弗林德斯·皮特里数过，同我们一模一样的人做过九次建立文明的尝试；每一次都败了，败得同我们这次正在败的一样。他们败，是因为公民和政治家们还没跳出学童的游戏、野蛮的运动、雪茄和香槟，就已经老死或者吃撑死了。末日的征象永远是一样的：民主，社会主义，妇女选票。除非我们认清必须活得更长，我们将在今日活着的人的有生之年里完蛋。

**卢宾**：你们同意我说的社会主义和妇女选票是衰败的征象，我很高兴。

===Original===
